The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 4.1 · The Loop & Template Tags
  1. C Dune<p></p>: PHP evaluates the expression first: the_title prints Dune and returns null, then echo prints the empty paragraph. Inside a string use get_the_title with esc_html.
  2. A the_title_attribute(), or esc_attr( get_the_title() ): the_title_attribute prints the title escaped for an attribute; esc_attr( get_the_title() ) is the returning equivalent. the_title prepares HTML text, not attribute values.
  3. B index.php: The search chain is search, then index. A search is not an archive, so archive.php is never a candidate and index.php draws it.
  4. C It returns false, and WordPress notices that it was called incorrectly: The main query has not run when must-use plugins load, so conditional tags return false, and with WP_DEBUG on WordPress reports that they were called incorrectly. Ask on template_redirect instead.
  5. A template-parts/content-none.php: The name is joined to the slug with a hyphen: template-parts/content-none.php, looked up in the child theme and then the parent. Only if it is missing does WordPress fall back to template-parts/content.php.
Lesson 4.2 · Custom Queries
  1. A A pre_get_posts callback that sets posts_per_page to 5 when the main query is_category( 'news' ): Changing the main query before it runs keeps the 404 decision, the template, the conditional tags and the_posts_pagination in agreement.
  2. B The title of the last post of the secondary Loop: Each the_post() of the secondary query replaced the global post, so the last post of that Loop is still current until wp_reset_postdata() restores the main query's post.
  3. C 4, 10, 3: post_count is the 4 posts on this page, found_posts the 10 that match in total, and max_num_pages is 10 divided by 4, rounded up: 3.
  4. C is_admin(): Admin list screens run a main query too, so is_main_query alone does not exclude them; is_admin() returns early for every admin request.
  5. B All four published posts, without any warning: cat takes category IDs; the string news is not one, so the filter is dropped silently and every published post comes back. category_name takes the slug.

Exit Ticket

  1. The Loop walks the posts the main query already found; the_post() moves to the next one and sets the global $post (and fires loop_start the first time).
  2. A pair: the_title() prints HTML-ready text for the page; get_the_title() returns the value, for an attribute (with esc_attr()), a string, or a comparison. Same for the_permalink() / get_permalink() (esc_url()) and the_date() / get_the_date() (esc_html()).
  3. Three per page: a pre_get_posts callback in functions.php (or a plugin), returning early on is_admin() or ! $query->is_main_query(), then if ( $query->is_home() ) $query->set( 'posts_per_page', 3 );.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 C. Believes WordPress makes missing templates. Show the hierarchy falling through to index.php.
  • Q3 B. Knows the_title( $before, $after ) but not that attributes need their own escaping.
  • Q5 A or B. Mixes up "WordPress is loaded" and "the main query has run". Redraw the request order.
  • Q8 A. Thinks the global is_home() describes the query being built. Ask how many queries run on the blog home.
  • Q9 B. Confuses post_count with posts_per_page.
  • Q10 B or C. Thinks the reset is optional inside templates. Rerun the broken navigation.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 7: functions.php: Supports, Assets, Menus & Widgets

Answers and reasoning for every Session 7 item: the warm-up, the self-check (Lesson 4.3 and the Part 1 project skills), the predict-then-run items, the Part 1 kickoff outputs, the lab reference outputs, the lesson page's own quiz and the exit ticket.

Session 7 12 self-check items Confidential

Start Here · Warm-up

Session 6 retrieval, not graded. W5's output on a site ready for today:

wp theme list --status=active --field=name
curl -s http://localhost:8080/ | grep -c 'class="entry-title"'
pagesco-classic
3
  1. W1 What does the Loop do, and what does the_post() do inside it? Answer: It walks the posts the main query already found; the_post() moves to the next one and sets the global $post (Lesson 4.1).
  2. W2 the_title() or get_the_title() inside echo '<h2>' . ... . '</h2>'? Answer: get_the_title(), escaped: the_title() prints on the spot and leaves an empty heading.
  3. W3 posts_per_page 2, three matches: post_count, found_posts, max_num_pages on page 1? Answer: 2, 3, 2 (Lesson 4.2).
  4. W4 Name the three guards of a pre_get_posts callback that changes the blog home. Answer: is_admin(), $query->is_main_query(), $query->is_home().
  5. W5 Run the two commands on the slide. What should a site ready for today print? Answer: pagesco-classic (active since Lesson 4.1), then 3: three news items on the home page (the 4.2 callback).

4.3 · functions.php: Supports, Assets, Menus & Widgets: self-check

Q1
Which of these actions fires FIRST in a front-end request?
A) wp_enqueue_scripts
B) widgets_init
C) after_setup_theme
D) wp_head
Why: The order of Lesson 4.3's timeline: the theme's functions.php is included, after_setup_theme fires (supports, menu locations), then init with widgets_init at priority 1, then the main query and the template, and wp_head runs wp_enqueue_scripts at priority 1.
A is wrong: It runs inside wp_head, after the query.
B is wrong: It runs on init, after after_setup_theme.
D is wrong: wp_head is printed by the template, near the end.
Q2
At the end of Lesson 4.3, what does this print, and why does the version column matter for the theme's CSS?
wp theme list --name=pagesco-classic --fields=name,status,version
pagesco-classic | inactive | 1.1.0: pagesco is active again; 1.1.0 is the Version the stylesheet is enqueued with (style.css?ver=1.1.0), so raising it on a release makes every browser fetch the new CSS
Why: The enqueue reads the version from the theme header (wp_get_theme( get_template() )->get( 'Version' )), never a typed number.
Q3
pagesco-classic enqueued its stylesheet with get_stylesheet_uri() instead of get_parent_theme_file_uri( 'style.css' ). A client builds a child theme of it. What happens?
A) The page loads the CHILD's style.css under the parent's handle, and the parent's CSS is gone
B) Nothing changes: both functions return the parent's file
C) WordPress loads both files automatically
D) A notice: get_stylesheet_uri() cannot be used in a parent theme
Why: get_stylesheet_uri() is the ACTIVE (child) theme's style.css; get_parent_theme_file_uri() always the theme that holds the code (Lesson 3.4's template and stylesheet). The same reasoning picks wp_get_theme( get_template() ) for the version.
B is wrong: Without a child they are the same, which is why the mistake hides.
C is wrong: Only what is enqueued loads.
D is wrong: There is no such notice; the mistake is silent.
Q4
Why does header.php wrap wp_nav_menu() in has_nav_menu( 'primary' )?
A) It makes the menu load faster
B) So a location without an assigned menu prints nothing instead of wp_page_menu()'s list of every page
C) wp_nav_menu() refuses to run without it
D) It registers the location
Why: With no menu in the location, wp_nav_menu() calls its fallback, wp_page_menu(), which lists all published pages, Sample Page included (Lesson 4.3, Common Mistakes 3). The guard (or 'fallback_cb' => false) keeps the header empty until the owner assigns a menu.
A is wrong: It saves nothing measurable.
C is wrong: It runs fine; that is the problem.
D is wrong: register_nav_menus() registers locations, on after_setup_theme.
Q5
pagesco-classic ships languages/es_ES.l10n.php and says Requires at least: 6.6. Does it still need load_theme_textdomain()?
A) Yes, every theme needs it on every version
B) No, never: translations always load themselves
C) Only plugins need it
D) Not on WordPress 6.8 and later, which load the active theme's languages/ folder itself; it stays for 6.6 and 6.7
Why: Since 6.8 wp-settings.php calls $wp_theme->load_textdomain() for the active theme (the lesson's version note, checked in the 6.7 and 6.8 images). A theme that supports older versions keeps the call; on 7.1 it is harmless.
A is wrong: Measured otherwise on 6.8+.
B is wrong: Not before 6.8.
C is wrong: Plugins and themes follow the same rule since 6.8.
Q6
A must-use plugin calls wp_enqueue_style() at the top level of its file. What does WordPress say, and what is the real damage?
A "called incorrectly" notice naming the handle (scripts and styles should not be enqueued until wp_enqueue_scripts...), plus two follow-on notices; the real damage: top-level code runs on every request, so the CSS also loads on admin screens and the login page
Why: Lesson 4.3's Common Mistake 1. Read the first notice; wrap the call in a function on wp_enqueue_scripts.

4.3 · functions.php: Supports, Assets, Menus & Widgets: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'var_dump( current_theme_supports( "title-tag" ), current_theme_supports( "menus" ) );'
bool(false)
bool(false)
Why: Neither: pagesco-classic declares no supports yet and registers no location. Registering a location (Lesson 4.3) is what switches menus on; nobody needs add_theme_support( 'menus' ).
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'var_dump( current_theme_supports( "title-tag" ), current_theme_supports( "html5", "search-form" ) );'
bool(false)
bool(true)
Why: pagesco declares nothing, yet html5 search-form is true: _add_default_theme_supports() adds it for block themes. title-tag is false although every page has a title: block themes print it unconditionally (the lesson's Exercise 3).

Part 1 Project: self-check (project skills)

Q7
Fernhill is a block theme. What is its style.css for?
A) It names the theme: the header fields WordPress reads (Theme Name, Text Domain, Version, Requires at least ...)
B) It is the theme's stylesheet, loaded on every page
C) It is a fallback when theme.json has an error
D) It registers the patterns
Why: Block themes do not load style.css on the front end (Lesson 3.2's FAQ); the design lives in theme.json, and extra CSS is enqueued (R5: fernhill-style). The header is R1 and the self-check's first line.
B is wrong: Not in a block theme; Fernhill enqueues assets/css/fernhill.css instead.
C is wrong: A broken theme.json is just ignored, with a decode notice.
D is wrong: Pattern files register themselves from patterns/.
Q8
The brief wants links and buttons in the primary color. Where does that go?
A) A hex color typed into each template's markup
B) assets/css/fernhill.css
C) theme.json: styles.elements.link and styles.elements.button with var:preset|color|primary
D) A pattern that wraps every link
Why: The design system lives in theme.json (Lesson 3.2): one preset, used everywhere, and a style variation (Autumn, R4) can recolor it without touching a template. Hard-coded hex in markup costs a rubric point.
A is wrong: The rubric's Partly column names exactly this.
B is wrong: Possible, but not where a variation can change it.
D is wrong: Patterns hold sections, not global styles.
Q9
R6: the blog home lists three posts per page with working pagination. Which way does the brief want?
A) A Query Loop with perPage 3 that does not inherit
B) A pre_get_posts callback in functions.php with the three guards: not the admin, the main query only, the blog home only
C) Settings > Reading set to 3
D) A second WP_Query in the front-page pattern
Why: One main query, changed before it runs, so the Query Loop that inherits it, the pagination and the 404 decision all agree (Lesson 4.2). The self-check's line 11 counts 3 on / and 1 on /page/2/.
A is wrong: A non-inheriting Query Loop runs a second query; /page/2/ then disagrees.
C is wrong: That is the owner's setting, not the theme's, and it changes every list.
D is wrong: The second-query mistake of Lesson 4.2.
Q10
You tried a header change in the Site Editor and clicked Save. Now your edits to parts/header.html no longer show. What happened, and what do you do?
A) The theme cache needs 30 minutes
B) parts/ files are only read on activation: switch themes twice
C) theme.json overrides parts
D) A wp_template_part copy in the database wins over the file: move the change into header.html, then delete the copy
Why: Lesson 3.2's Mistake 3 and 3.3's loop: list the template, template-part and global-styles posts (wp post list), find the change, put it in the file, delete the copy. A copy left behind is also missing from the zip you hand in (R8).
A is wrong: That cache is for pattern files, and development mode is on.
B is wrong: Parts are read on every request.
C is wrong: theme.json declares part areas; it does not replace markup.
Q11
Odette wants the autumn look she can switch on in October (R4). Which file, and what may it change?
styles/autumn.json, titled Autumn, without blockTypes: the same five color slugs with autumn values, nothing else
Why: A style variation is a layer over theme.json (Lesson 3.3); keeping the slugs means every template and pattern recolors by itself. With blockTypes it would be a section style instead.
Q12
You stop working on Fernhill for the day. Which command do you run, and why?
wp theme activate pagesco: the labs, Session 8's assessment and Part 2 (Lesson 5.1) expect pagesco active, the state at the end of Lesson 4.3
Why: The self-check switches nothing; you activate fernhill yourself and switch back yourself (the brief's rules).

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, on a course site at the state each file declares (WordPress 7.1.3, PHP 8.5, MySQL 9.7, WP-CLI 2.12).

Lab 4.3-A (guided): labs/module-07/l4_3-functions-php/corner_theme_starter/ → corner_theme_solution/ with test_corner_theme.sh

The test with the starter:

Success: Switched to 'Pages &amp; Co. Lab: Reading Corner' theme.

With the solution:

Success: Switched to 'Pages &amp; Co. Lab: Reading Corner' theme.
<title>:      1

style:        style.css?ver=2.0.0
script:       corner.js?ver=2.0.0, defer
menu places:  corner (Reading corner menu)
widget areas: corner-footer

Reading it: no title at all until title-tag (the header has no hand-written one: the lesson's two-titles mistake cannot happen); the xhtml form until html5 with an ARRAY; the stylesheet and the deferred script carry the theme's Version 2.0.0 read from the header; registering the location and the area is enough for WP-CLI to list them (they stay empty until the owner fills them, which is why the templates guard with has_nav_menu() and is_active_sidebar()). Accept the menu and the supports in one callback; insist on one function per hook family and the prefix.

The Lesson Page's Own Quick Quiz

Lesson 4.3 · functions.php: Supports, Assets, Menus & Widgets
  1. B In a functions.php callback hooked to after_setup_theme: Theme supports are declared on after_setup_theme, which fires right after functions.php is included and long before any template runs.
  2. C ?ver=7.1.3: Without a version WordPress adds its own, 7.1.3, which does not change when your CSS does, so browsers keep the old file. Pass the theme's Version instead.
  3. A A list of all published pages, from the fallback wp_page_menu(): With no menu found, wp_nav_menu calls its default fallback, wp_page_menu, which lists every published page. fallback_cb false or a has_nav_menu check prevents it.
  4. B widgets_init: widgets_init fires during init (at priority 1), the moment WordPress sets up widgets, on every request including the admin's Widgets screen.
  5. C Add the standard .screen-reader-text CSS that clips it visually but keeps it for screen readers: Core marks text meant for screen readers with screen-reader-text and expects the theme to hide it visually only. Block themes get this CSS from core; classic themes must add it.

Exit Ticket

  1. Hooks: after_setup_theme (supports, menu locations); wp_enqueue_scripts (styles and scripts); widgets_init (widget areas).
  2. The version: it ends the file's URL (?ver=), so a new release means a new URL and every browser and cache fetches the new file; the theme's Version changes exactly when you ship.
  3. Personal. Use the answers to plan the Session 8 studio: group learners by the requirement they named.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A or D. Thinks assets come first because they are "the theme". Redraw the timeline.
  • Q3 B. Has never built a child theme of a classic theme; tie it to Lesson 3.4's template/stylesheet.
  • Q5 A. Copied from older tutorials; show the 6.8 version note.
  • Q7 B. Still thinks a block theme loads style.css: Lesson 3.2's FAQ and Lesson 3.4's "stylesheet nobody loads".
  • Q9 A. A non-inheriting Query Loop: the most common Fernhill bug in the studio; /page/2/ gives it away.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 8: Part 1 Project and Assessment

Answers and reasoning for Session 8: the warm-up, the Part 1 self-check, the predict items, the review lab and handout, and the project's and the assessment's self-check outputs (rubrics and scoring in final-assessment.html).

Session 8 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up

  1. W1 Hooks (Lesson 2.3), themes (Modules 3 and 4), plugins (Module 5), blocks (Module 6), APIs (Module 7). Part 1 covered the first two.
  2. W2 Block: templates/*.html and parts/*.html, block markup. Classic: *.php in the theme root (index.php, singular.php...), PHP with template tags.
  3. W3 Not is_admin(); $query->is_main_query(); $query->is_home() (the query's method, not the global function).
  4. W4 esc_html() text, esc_attr() attributes, esc_url() href/src, wp_kses_post() HTML with safe tags (and the _e / __ variants for translated text).
  5. W5 Each learner's own number: the status round (see the studio triage).

Self-check: Part 1

Q1
The site shows the latest posts on its front page. The active block theme has templates/front-page.html, templates/home.html and templates/index.html. Which template draws /?
A) home.html, because the front page lists posts
B) index.html, the fallback for everything
C) front-page.html
D) page.html, because the front page is a page
Why: front-page is checked first for the front page, whatever it shows (Lesson 3.1's hierarchy). home draws the posts list only when it is NOT the front page. That is why Fernhill's news list lives in front-page.html.
A is wrong: home.html would win only if front-page.html were missing (or the posts list were on another page).
B is wrong: index.html is the last resort, used when nothing more specific exists.
D is wrong: With show_on_front set to posts there is no front PAGE at all.
Q2
What does this print?
wp eval 'add_filter( "the_title", function ( $t ) { return "A" . $t; }, 20 );
add_filter( "the_title", function ( $t ) { return "B" . $t; } );
echo apply_filters( "the_title", "x" ), "\n";'
ABx
Why: Callbacks run by priority, lowest first: the default 10 ("B") before 20 ("A"). Each filter receives the value the previous one returned, so A wraps the result of B (Lesson 2.3).
Q3
Which file must a block theme have, besides style.css, for WordPress to accept it?
A) functions.php
B) templates/index.html
C) theme.json
D) index.php
Why: A block theme needs style.css (its name) and templates/index.html (the last resort of the hierarchy); without the template WordPress reports "Template is missing" (Lesson 3.1, Mistake 1). theme.json is strongly recommended, functions.php optional.
A is wrong: Optional: many block themes have none.
C is wrong: Without theme.json the theme works on core's defaults.
D is wrong: That is the CLASSIC theme's required template.
Q4
pagesco-tt5-child is a child theme of Twenty Twenty-Five. What does the middle command print?
wp theme activate pagesco-tt5-child
wp eval 'echo get_template(), " ", get_stylesheet(), "\n";'
wp theme activate pagesco
twentytwentyfive pagesco-tt5-child
Why: get_template() is the PARENT's folder (the Template header), get_stylesheet() the active theme's own folder: the child (Lesson 3.4). Without a child theme both print the same name.
Q5
A learner changed Fernhill's footer in the Site Editor yesterday. Today every edit to parts/footer.html is ignored. Why, and what fixes it?
A) The database copy of the part (a wp_template_part post) wins over the file: copy what you want into the file, then reset or delete the copy
B) OPcache: restart the containers
C) The footer area in theme.json is wrong: change it to "footer-1"
D) Template parts are cached for 30 minutes: wait
Why: A Site Editor save creates a database copy (source custom) that WordPress prefers to the theme's file. Export or copy the markup into the file, then Reset in the Site Editor or delete the post (Lesson 3.2, Mistake 3; the project's rule R8).
B is wrong: OPcache caches PHP, and the course setup revalidates on every request; .html parts are not PHP.
C is wrong: The area only says where the part belongs in the editor; it does not decide which copy wins.
D is wrong: The 30-minute cache is for PATTERN files, and WP_DEVELOPMENT_MODE theme switches it off on the course site.
Q6
This callback forgets the main-query guard. What does the last line print on the course site (four published posts)?
wp eval 'add_action( "pre_get_posts", function ( $q ) {
	if ( is_admin() ) { return; }
	if ( $q->is_home() ) { $q->set( "posts_per_page", 3 ); }
} );
$q = new WP_Query( array( "posts_per_page" => 5 ) );
echo $q->post_count, "\n";'
A) 4
B) 5
C) 0
D) 3
Why: A WP_Query without query variables counts as the blog home (is_home() is true), so the unguarded callback changes YOUR query too: 3 posts instead of the four you asked for. $query->is_main_query() is the guard (Lesson 4.2, Mistake 3; the project's R6).
A is wrong: That is what the query returns WITH the guard (all four posts, fewer than five).
B is wrong: There are only four published posts.
C is wrong: Nothing is excluded; the query only gets a smaller page size.
Q7
What does this print? (Three posts are in News.)
wp eval '$q = new WP_Query( array( "category_name" => "news", "posts_per_page" => 2 ) );
echo $q->post_count, " ", $q->found_posts, "\n";'
A) 2 2
B) 2 3
C) 3 3
D) 3 2
Why: post_count is how many posts this page of results holds (posts_per_page 2), found_posts how many match in all (3). max_num_pages would be 2: that is what pagination needs (Lesson 4.2).
A is wrong: found_posts ignores the page size.
C is wrong: post_count respects the page size.
D is wrong: The two are swapped.
Q8
A pattern prints a link to an address that comes from home_url(). Which function goes around it inside href="..."?
A) esc_html()
B) esc_attr()
C) esc_url()
D) wp_kses_post()
Why: esc_url() checks the protocol and encodes the characters a URL may not carry; it is the escaping for href and src, even for an address WordPress built (Lessons 1.3 and 3.3). Escape late, at the echo.
A is wrong: For text between tags, not for URLs.
B is wrong: For other attribute values; it does not check the protocol (javascript: would pass).
D is wrong: For HTML that may keep safe tags, such as post content.
Q9
What does this print? (Post 17 is "Book club: Dune".)
wp eval 'global $post; $post = get_post( 17 ); setup_postdata( $post );
echo "<h2>" . the_title() . "</h2>\n";'
Book club: Dune<h2></h2>
Why: the_title() ECHOES and returns nothing, so the title is printed while PHP builds the string, before the concatenated <h2></h2>. Use the_title( "<h2>", "</h2>" ) or get_the_title() with esc_html() (Lesson 4.1, Mistake 1).
Q10
Fernhill must load assets/css/fernhill.css on the front end. On which hook does wp_enqueue_style() belong?
A) init
B) after_setup_theme
C) wp_head
D) wp_enqueue_scripts
Why: wp_enqueue_scripts is the hook for front-end styles and scripts (admin_enqueue_scripts and login_enqueue_scripts for those screens). Called anywhere earlier, WordPress says the function was called incorrectly; wp_head is too late for the queue to be printed in order (Lesson 4.3, Mistake 1).
A is wrong: Too early: "wp_enqueue_style was called incorrectly".
B is wrong: For theme supports and menus, not assets.
C is wrong: wp_head is where the queue is PRINTED (at priority 8); enqueueing on it is too late or out of order.
Q11
A theme has styles/autumn.json with a title and a palette, and no blockTypes. What is it?
A) A style variation the site owner can switch on for the whole site
B) A section style for Group blocks
C) A child theme of the theme
D) A pattern
Why: A JSON file in styles/ without blockTypes is a style variation: it overlays the theme's theme.json for the whole site when chosen in Styles (it saves as user global styles). With blockTypes it is a section (block style) variation (Lesson 3.3).
B is wrong: Section styles name the block types they apply to.
C is wrong: A child theme is its own folder with Template: in style.css (Lesson 3.4).
D is wrong: Patterns are PHP (or HTML) files in patterns/ with a Slug header.
Q12
pagesco's theme.json has the palette color oxblood, #7a2e2e. What does this print?
wp eval 'echo wp_get_global_stylesheet( array( "variables" ) ), "\n";' \
	| grep -o -- '--wp--preset--color--oxblood: [^;]*'
--wp--preset--color--oxblood: #7a2e2e
Why: Every preset becomes a CSS custom property, --wp--preset--color--<slug>; var:preset|color|oxblood in theme.json and the has-oxblood-* classes point at it (Lesson 3.2). That is why slugs name ROLES: a variation changes the value, never the name.

Predict, Then Run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo wp_get_theme( "pagesco-tt5-child" )->get( "Template" ), "\n";'
twentytwentyfive
Why: The child's style.css names its parent FOLDER in the Template header (Lesson 3.4); WP_Theme reads it as Template.
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' http://localhost:8080/page/2/
404
Why: pagesco shows ten posts per page (the Reading setting) and the site has four posts, so there is no page 2: WordPress answers 404. On Fernhill (three per page) or Brightwater (two) it exists.
P3
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval '$q = new WP_Query( array( "post_type" => "page", "orderby" => "title", "order" => "ASC" ) );
echo implode( ", ", wp_list_pluck( $q->posts, "post_title" ) ), "\n";'
About Us, Contact, Events, Sample Page
Why: A WP_Query of pages with no post_status finds only published pages: the Privacy Policy is a draft. orderby title sorts alphabetically; posts_per_page defaults to the Reading setting (ten).
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo esc_html( "<b>Fernhill</b> & friends" ), "\n";'
&lt;b&gt;Fernhill&lt;/b&gt; &amp; friends
Why: esc_html() turns <, > and & into entities. The browser shows the text exactly as written; that is the point, even for your own strings (a translation might contain anything).

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, on a course site at the end of Lesson 4.3 (WordPress 7.1.3, PHP 8.5), pagesco active.

Review lab: labs/module-08/p1-review/request_map_starter.sh → request_map_solution.sh

As shipped:

/  200  TODO
/book-club-dune/  200  TODO

The solution prints:

/  200  home blog 
/page/2/  404  error404 
/book-club-dune/  200  single single-post 
/about/  200  page-template-default page 
/category/news/  200  archive category category-news 
/?s=dune  200  search search-results 
/no-such-path/  404  error404

Reading it: body classes are WordPress's answer to "what is this request?" (the query flags is_home, is_single, is_page, is_category, is_search, is_404), printed by body_class() in every theme. The hierarchy then looks for a template for that answer. /page/2/ is a 404 on pagesco because ten posts per page leave no page 2; /about/ adds page-template-default because no page template is assigned.

Exit Ticket

  1. pre_get_posts: a callback in functions.php, guarded (not admin, $query->is_main_query(), $query->is_home()), sets posts_per_page to 3. The Query Loop inherits the main query, so pagination and /page/2/ work; a second query in the template would not page, and the main query would still run.
  2. esc_html_e(): it makes the string translatable with the theme's text domain and escapes it as it prints; a translation is data the theme did not write. It is also the habit that keeps a later dynamic value safe.
  3. Own answer: use the "review" answers to choose Session 9's Part 1 refresher topics.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A (home.html). Mixes up "shows posts" with "is the posts page". front-page wins for the front page whatever it shows.
  • Q2 BAx. Assumes the order of add_filter() calls is the order of running; priority decides, then the order of adding.
  • Q5 B or D. Blames caching. Ask: where does the Site Editor save? (The database: Lesson 3.2.)
  • Q6 A. Trusts the code to do what it says. The guard is the whole point; that is also why the self-check cannot catch the missing guard and the rubric reads the code.
  • Q9 <h2>Book club: Dune</h2>. the_ functions echo; the get_ functions return (Lesson 4.1).
  • Q10 C (wp_head). Remembers that styles print in the head. Enqueue on wp_enqueue_scripts; WordPress prints the queue in wp_head.

Session 9: Your First Plugin and Custom Post Types

Session 9 12 self-check items Confidential

Start Here · Part 1 Refresher

Not graded. Each answer says where Part 2 uses it.

R1
The store notice of Lesson 2.3 lives in which file, and which of its three hooks is a filter?
wp-content/mu-plugins/pagesco-site.php; pagesco_store_notice is the filter (the text), pagesco_before_store_notice and pagesco_store_notice_end are actions. Lesson 5.1 hooks a plugin feature into the filter and the end action.
R2
Load, query, template: in which of the three do plugins load, and which action tells them WordPress is ready to register things?
Load (wp-settings.php): must-use plugins, plugins, plugins_loaded, the theme, then init. pagesco-books starts its features on plugins_loaded; post types register on init.
R3
What makes pagesco a block theme, as wp_is_block_theme() sees it?
A templates/index.html (Lesson 3.1); its theme.json holds the design. Part 2 never edits the theme's looks: books get their template in Module 6.
R4
Why does a pre_get_posts callback check is_admin() and $query->is_main_query() first?
Because the action fires for every query, in the admin too: without the guards it would change the Books screen, widgets and REST lists (Lesson 4.2). The book archive at /books/ is a main query again.
R5
A feature should survive the next redesign. Theme or plugin?
A plugin (Lessons 3.4 and 4.3: the theme is the looks, the plugin is what the shop does). That rule is why Part 2 builds pagesco-books.

Start Here · The Part 2 Check

check_environment.sh --part2 prints every Session 1 line and then three more. A learner who is ready for Sessions 9 and 10 usually sees OK for Composer and the site, and the note CHECK no Node 24 on this computer (v20.20.2): use the node:24 image as Lesson 6.1 shows (docker run ... node:24 npm ...) (verdict READY WITH NOTES). The FIX lines and what to do:

  • FIX the composer:2 image does not run: docker pull composer:2 (Lesson 1.3): Docker cannot run the composer:2 image (offline, or not pulled yet): docker pull composer:2, on a good connection.
  • FIX no Node 24 and no node:24 image: install Node 24 LTS (nvm install 24) before Module 6: neither Node 24 nor the image: not a problem for Sessions 9 and 10; fix it before Session 11 (Lesson 6.1).
  • FIX the site is not where Part 2 starts (active theme 'twentytwentyfive'): finish Lesson 4.3, or import the Part 2 starting site (the course download pagesco-part2-start.zip: its README.txt) (the active theme is named): the site is not the Part 2 starting site; import pagesco-part2-start.zip (its README.txt; the setup guide's Part 2 section), or finish Lesson 4.3.

5.1 · Your First Plugin: self-check

Q1
A client's server runs PHP 8.1. pagesco-books says Requires PHP: 8.2. What happens when the administrator clicks Activate?
A) The plugin activates, and the first page that uses PHP 8.2 syntax dies with a fatal error
B) WordPress refuses to activate it and says which PHP version the plugin requires
C) The plugin activates with the features that need PHP 8.2 switched off
D) Composer's platform_check.php deletes the vendor folder
Why: The requirement lines are read before a line of the plugin runs; activation fails with "Current PHP version ... does not meet minimum requirements" (Lesson 5.1, Exercise 1). Without the header, PHP 8.2 syntax on an old server would have been a fatal error instead.
A is wrong: That is what happens WITHOUT the header line.
C is wrong: WordPress knows nothing about your features; it refuses the whole plugin.
D is wrong: platform_check.php stops with a message on an old PHP; it never deletes anything, and WordPress refuses earlier anyway.
Q2
On the course site after Lesson 5.1, what does this print?
wp eval 'echo PAGESCO_BOOKS_DIR, "\n";'
/var/www/html/wp-content/plugins/pagesco-books/
Why: plugin_dir_path( __FILE__ ) returns the folder of the main file WITH a trailing slash, as the container sees it (your wordpress/wp-content/plugins/pagesco-books/). The trailing slash is why the main file can write PAGESCO_BOOKS_DIR . 'vendor/autoload.php' with no slash in between.
Q3
A learner creates src/Admin/BookColumns.php but writes namespace PagesCo\Books; at the top of it, and lists Admin\BookColumns::class in feature_classes(). What happens on the next request?
A) Nothing: PSR-4 only looks at the file name
B) Composer must be run again before the class is found
C) A fatal error: Class PagesCo\Books\Admin\BookColumns not found
D) WordPress shows a "called incorrectly" notice and skips the feature
Why: The autoloader finds and includes src/Admin/BookColumns.php, but that file declares PagesCo\Books\BookColumns, so the class boot() asked for still does not exist: the same fatal as the project's Step 3. The namespace line must match the folder: PagesCo\Books\Admin for src/Admin/.
A is wrong: PSR-4 builds the file name from the class name, then expects the file to declare exactly that class.
B is wrong: dump-autoload is only needed when composer.json's autoload section changes.
D is wrong: WordPress is not involved: this is PHP's own class loading, and it is fatal.
Q4
A site owner deactivates pagesco-books for five minutes to find a conflict. Which of these must the deactivation callback NOT do?
A) Delete the Bookseller role and the plugin's options
B) Clear the plugin's scheduled events
C) Flush the rewrite rules
D) Nothing at all (an empty callback)
Why: Deactivation is a pause: it undoes what is temporary (scheduled events, rewrite rules) and keeps the owner's data. Deleting data is uninstall.php's job, and only when the plugin is deleted (Lesson 5.1, the life-cycle table).
B is wrong: Clearing scheduled events is exactly a deactivation job (Lesson 7.3).
C is wrong: Lesson 5.2's deactivate() does this so /books/ stops answering while the plugin is off.
D is wrong: An empty callback is fine: Lesson 5.1's first Activator::deactivate() was one.
Q5
On the course site after Lesson 5.1, what does the second command print, and why?
wp plugin uninstall pagesco-books --deactivate --skip-delete > /dev/null
wp eval 'var_dump( get_role( "pagesco_bookseller" ) );'
NULL: uninstall.php removed the role, so get_role() finds nothing
Why: --skip-delete runs the uninstall procedure (uninstall.php) without deleting the files: the safe way to test cleanup. Afterwards wp plugin activate pagesco-books re-creates the role, because activation runs Activator::activate() again.
Q6
Why must register_activation_hook() be called at the top level of the main file, not inside a plugins_loaded callback?
A) Because hooks registered on plugins_loaded run twice
B) Because the activation hook needs the autoloader, which loads later
C) Because WordPress only reads the main file's header at the top level
D) Because on the activation request plugins_loaded has already fired before WordPress includes the new plugin's file
Why: WordPress activates a plugin by including its main file during an admin (or WP-CLI) request that has already loaded everything else. A plugins_loaded callback added then never runs, so the activation hook is never registered, and nothing warns you (Lesson 5.1, Common Mistakes 3).
A is wrong: Callbacks run once per firing; plugins_loaded fires once per request.
B is wrong: The autoloader is required at the top of the main file, before the hooks are registered.
C is wrong: The header is read without running the file at all; it has nothing to do with where hooks are registered.

5.1 · Your First Plugin: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
wp plugin deactivate pagesco-books
curl -s http://localhost:8080/ | grep -o '<div class="pagesco-notice">.*</div>'
wp plugin activate pagesco-books
Plugin 'pagesco-books' deactivated.
Success: Deactivated 1 of 1 plugins.
<div class="pagesco-notice">Free shipping on orders over $35.</div>
Plugin 'pagesco-books' activated.
Success: Activated 1 of 1 plugins.
Why: With pagesco-books off, StoreNotice is never created, so the notice shows only the must-use plugin's own text: the split Lesson 5.1's Step 2 set up. The last command switches it back on.
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'var_dump( class_exists( "PagesCo\Books\StoreNotice", false ), class_exists( "PagesCo\Books\StoreNotice" ) );'
bool(true)
bool(true)
Why: Both true: boot() ran on plugins_loaded and created StoreNotice, which made the autoloader include src/StoreNotice.php. false as the second argument only says "do not autoload just for this question"; the class is already there.

5.2 · Custom Post Types, Taxonomies & Meta: self-check

Q7
BookPostType registers 'has_archive' => 'books' and 'rewrite' => array( 'slug' => 'books' ). Which address lists all books?
A) /book/
B) /books/
C) /?post_type=books
D) /wp-json/wp/v2/book
Why: has_archive gives the archive its slug; the rewrite slug is the first part of each single book's address (/books/dune/). After a flush the rule books/?$ maps to index.php?post_type=book (Lesson 5.2's wp rewrite list).
A is wrong: That would be the default if rewrite and has_archive were just true: the type's NAME, book.
C is wrong: The query variable is the type's name, book, not the archive slug.
D is wrong: The REST route uses rest_base, books: /wp-json/wp/v2/books.
Q8
The Great Gatsby (book 33) is out of stock; pagesco_in_stock is registered as a boolean. What does this print on the finished Lesson 5.2 site?
wp eval 'var_export( get_post_meta( 33, "pagesco_in_stock", true ) ); echo "\n";'
'' (an empty string)
Why: Meta values are stored as strings: true is saved as '1', false as ''. get_post_meta() returns the stored string; only the REST API (and block bindings) cast it back to the registered type, which is why /wp/v2/books/33 says false. Compare a value with '' or cast it with (bool), as BookColumns does.
Q9
The shop wants a page /publisher/penguin/ listing every Penguin book, and filters by publisher on the Books screen. How should the publisher be stored?
A) As a taxonomy on the book type
B) As a meta key, pagesco_publisher
C) As an option
D) In the post content of each book
Why: Many books share one publisher, and the shop wants to browse by it: that is a taxonomy, with its own archive at /publisher/<slug>/ and an admin column for free. The lesson's test: would you want a page listing everything with that value? (Lesson 5.2 FAQ.)
B is wrong: Meta suits a fact that belongs to one item and that you read rather than browse, such as a price.
C is wrong: An option is one site-wide value, not one per book.
D is wrong: Content cannot be filtered, queried or listed by value.
Q10
"To be safe", a developer calls flush_rewrite_rules() on init. What is wrong with that?
A) Nothing: it is the recommended place
B) The rules are flushed before the post type is registered, so books 404
C) WordPress refuses to flush on init and logs a notice
D) It rewrites the whole rules option on every page load: a needless, slow database write
Why: Rebuilding the rules is expensive, which is why WordPress keeps them in an option and only rebuilds them when something flushes. Flush once, in the activator, after registering the type and taxonomy; deactivation flushes again (Lesson 5.2, Common Mistakes 1).
A is wrong: The handbook and the lesson both say: on activation, never on every request.
B is wrong: Registration at the default priority runs first and the URLs work; the cost is the problem.
C is wrong: WordPress lets you; it just costs a write per request.
Q11
Genre 8 is Fantasy. What does this print on the finished Lesson 5.2 site?
curl -s 'http://localhost:8080/wp-json/wp/v2/books?genres=8&_fields=title.rendered&orderby=id&order=asc'
[{"title":{"rendered":"The Hobbit"}},{"title":{"rendered":"Lord of the Rings"}}]
Why: The genre taxonomy is registered with rest_base genres, so the books route accepts genres=8 as a filter (term IDs, not slugs); _fields trims the response. Two books are filed under Fantasy (the term list's count).
Q12
A WP-CLI seeding script creates a book with wp_insert_post() and must give it two genres. Which reliably works?
A) 'tax_input' => array( 'genre' => $genres ) in the post array
B) A 'genres' key in the post array
C) wp_set_object_terms( $id, $genres, 'genre' ) after the insert
D) update_post_meta( $id, 'genre', $genres )
Why: tax_input is checked against the current user's assign_terms capability, and a script runs as no user, so the genres are silently dropped (Lesson 5.2, Common Mistakes 2). wp_set_object_terms() sets them without a capability check.
A is wrong: It works in the editor, as a logged-in user, and silently does nothing in a script.
B is wrong: wp_insert_post() ignores keys it does not know.
D is wrong: That stores a meta value named genre; the taxonomy never hears of it.

5.2 · Custom Post Types, Taxonomies & Meta: predict, then run

P3
Predict the output, then run it.
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' http://localhost:8080/genre/fantasy/
200
Why: 200: the seeding created the Fantasy term, and the activator's flush gave /genre/<slug>/ its rule. Before the seeding it was a 404 for lack of a term, not of a rule.
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'var_dump( get_post_meta( 30, "pagesco_pages", true ),
    get_post_meta( 30, "pagesco_pages" ) );'
string(3) "412"
array(1) {
  [0]=>
  string(3) "412"
}
Why: A string both times: the database stores meta as text, and get_post_meta() hands back what is stored (the single value, or an array of all values without true). The REST API casts it to an integer because the key is registered as one.

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, on the site each declares (after:lesson_13 = the Part 2 starting site, after:lesson_14 = after Lesson 5.1, after:lesson_15 = after Lesson 5.2), with WordPress 7.1.3, PHP 8.5, MySQL 9.7 and WP-CLI 2.12. Paths show /var/www/html/..., which is the learner's wordpress/ folder.

What each TODO shows: (1) the requirement lines are data WordPress reads without running the file (get_plugin_data()); (2) the guard turns a direct request from a 500 (the file ran without WordPress and died on add_filter(); the fatal went to the web server log, not debug.log) into a quiet 200; (3) the activation hook at the top level, with __FILE__, is what made activation store the price; (4) get_option() returns false when nothing is stored, so check before you print; (5) uninstall.php with its own guard deletes the option, which deactivation never does. Accept add_option() or update_option() in the activator; add_option() is the better choice because it never overwrites a price the shop changed.

Lab 5.1-B: labs/module-09/l5_1-first-plugin/psr4_paths_starter.php → psr4_paths_solution.php

As shipped:

PagesCo\Books\Plugin                 TODO
PagesCo\Books\Admin\SettingsPage     TODO
PagesCo\Books\Rest\BooksController   TODO
PagesCo\Shop\Cart                    TODO
pagesco\books\plugin                 TODO

The solution prints:

PagesCo\Books\Plugin                 src/Plugin.php
PagesCo\Books\Admin\SettingsPage     src/Admin/SettingsPage.php
PagesCo\Books\Rest\BooksController   src/Rest/BooksController.php
PagesCo\Shop\Cart                    shop/src/Cart.php
pagesco\books\plugin                 (no rule matches: not autoloaded)

The point of the last line: PHP compares class names without case, but an autoloader compares strings and builds a FILE name, and Linux file names are case-sensitive. That is the project's Step 3 in one line. Composer's real ClassLoader does exactly this, plus a cache.

Handout: labs/module-09/l5_1-first-plugin/debug_plugin_audit.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped, PHP prints:

Warning: Undefined array key "RequiresPhp" in /var/www/html/debug_plugin_audit.php on line 20
Akismet Anti-spam: Spam Protection inactive needs PHP 7.4  TOO OLD for this plugin
Warning: Undefined array key "inactive" in /var/www/html/debug_plugin_audit.php on line 23
Warning: Undefined array key "RequiresPhp" in /var/www/html/debug_plugin_audit.php on line 20
Hello Dolly  inactive needs PHP 7.4  TOO OLD for this plugin
2 plugin(s) installed, 2 inactive
  1. Warning, line 20: 'RequiresPhp' is not a key of get_plugins()'s data; the key is 'RequiresPHP' (array keys are case-sensitive: the theme of the whole lesson). With the key missing every plugin falls back to 7.4.
  2. Warning, line 23: ++$pagesco_totals[ $pagesco_status ] on an empty array: the first increment of each key warns. Start with array( 'active' => 0, 'inactive' => 0 ); the totals line then also has its key.
  3. Silent, line 21: version_compare( PHP_VERSION, $pagesco_needs, '<' ) is true when this server is OLDER than the requirement, so every plugin is called TOO OLD on PHP 8.5. The question is '>='. This is the check WordPress makes on activation (Lesson 5.1, Exercise 1).

Fixed (the three changes), it prints, without a message:

Akismet Anti-spam: Spam Protection inactive needs PHP 7.2  runs here
Hello Dolly  inactive needs PHP 7.4  runs here
2 plugin(s) installed, 2 inactive

Akismet's header asks for PHP 7.2; Hello Dolly declares none, so the script's 7.4 default applies. Both run here; neither is active on the Part 2 starting site.

Lab 5.2-A (guided): labs/module-09/l5_2-custom-post-types/book_quotes_starter/ → book_quotes_solution/ with test_book_quotes.sh

The test with the starter:

Plugin 'pagesco-quotes' activated.
Success: Activated 1 of 1 plugins.
archive /quotes/: 404
REST /wp/v2/quotes:
{"code":"rest_no_route","message":"No route was found matching the URL and request method.","data":{"status":404}}
quotes left: 0

With the solution:

Plugin 'pagesco-quotes' activated.
Success: Activated 1 of 1 plugins.
archive /quotes/: 200
REST /wp/v2/quotes:
[{"title":{"rendered":"A truth universally acknowledged"},"meta":{"pagesco_quote_source":"Pride and Prejudice"}}]
quotes left: 0

Reading it: (1) public plus has_archive => 'quotes' and the rewrite slug give /quotes/; with has_archive => true the archive would be /pagesco_quote/; (2) show_in_rest plus rest_base give /wp/v2/quotes (without rest_base the route is the type name); (3) the meta key needs show_in_rest to appear under meta, and supports custom-fields (already there) to reach REST at all; (4) the activator calls pagesco_quotes_register() before flush_rewrite_rules(): on the activation request init has already run without this plugin. A learner whose activator only flushes still sees a 404, which is the lesson's warning box made real. The test deletes its quote, so the count at the end is 0 either way.

Lab 5.2-B (homework): labs/module-09/l5_2-custom-post-types/shelf_report_starter.php → shelf_report_solution.php

As shipped (on the finished Lesson 5.2 site):

Fiction:         11
Science Fiction: 3
Classic:         6
Fantasy:         2
Dystopian:       3
Cheapest in stock: TODO
Average price: TODO
Published before 1950: TODO

The solution prints:

Fiction:         11
Science Fiction: 3
Classic:         6
Fantasy:         2
Dystopian:       3
Cheapest in stock: Pride and Prejudice ($7.99)
Average price: $10.76
Published before 1950: 6

Sticking points: in-stock is stored as '1' (true) and '' (false), so the meta_query compares with '1'; meta_value_num sorts prices as numbers (meta_value would sort them as text, the Lesson 2.2 trap); the average of the eleven prices is $10.76; 'type' => 'DATE' makes MySQL compare the Y-m-d strings as dates (string comparison happens to agree for this format, but the type says what you mean). Six books were first published before 1950.

Handout: labs/module-09/l5_2-custom-post-types/debug_meeting_type.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped, WordPress and PHP print:

Notice: Function register_post_type was called <strong>incorrectly</strong>. Post type names must be between 1 and 20 characters in length. Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information. (This message was added in version 4.2.0.) in /var/www/html/wp-includes/functions.php on line 6260

Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information. (This message was added in version 5.3.0.) in /var/www/html/wp-includes/functions.php on line 6260

[new lines in wp-content/debug.log]
[26-Oct-2026 13:25:20 UTC] PHP Notice:  Function register_post_type was called <strong>incorrectly</strong>. Post type names must be between 1 and 20 characters in length. Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information. (This message was added in version 4.2.0.) in /var/www/html/wp-includes/functions.php on line 6260
[26-Oct-2026 13:25:20 UTC] PHP Notice:  Function register_meta was called <strong>incorrectly</strong>. When registering an "array" meta type to show in the REST API, you must specify the schema for each array item in "show_in_rest.schema.items". Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information. (This message was added in version 5.3.0.) in /var/www/html/wp-includes/functions.php on line 6260
[26-Oct-2026 13:25:20 UTC] PHP Warning:  Attempt to read property "label" on null in /var/www/html/debug_meeting_type.php on line 43
  1. "called incorrectly", register_post_type: a post type name has at most 20 characters (it is stored in a varchar(20) column); pagesco_bookclub_meeting has 24, so WordPress refuses it and returns a WP_Error. Use pagesco_meeting.

Fixed, it prints:

type: Meetings
taxonomies on meetings: pagesco_room
meta keys: pagesco_reading

The order of the lesson's feature classes (type before taxonomy before meta) is the same for a reason: each one names the one before it.

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 5.1 · Your First Plugin
  1. C A Plugin Name line in the header comment: WordPress reads the header comment without running the file, and Plugin Name is the one required field; everything else is optional, though the requirement lines are strongly recommended.
  2. A Once, during the request that activates the plugin: It runs once, during the request that activates the plugin, which is why one-time setup such as add_role belongs there and per-request code does not.
  3. B src/Rest/BooksController.php: PSR-4 replaces the prefix PagesCo\Books\ with src/, turns the remaining backslashes into slashes and adds .php, with the exact same letters and case.
  4. C In uninstall.php: uninstall.php runs only when the plugin is deleted, guarded by WP_UNINSTALL_PLUGIN. Deactivation is a pause and must keep the data.
  5. A Nothing; the autoloader finds the new class by its name: Nothing: the PSR-4 autoloader finds new classes by name. composer dump-autoload is only needed when the autoload section of composer.json changes, as the project showed with StoreNotice.
Lesson 5.2 · Custom Post Types, Taxonomies & Meta
  1. B init: init is the action WordPress fires once it is loaded and ready for plugins to register post types, taxonomies and meta; registering earlier or later misses the editor and REST setup.
  2. A The rewrite rules were not flushed after the type was registered: New rewrite rules are written only when flush_rewrite_rules() runs, which is why the activation hook flushes once; without it the pretty URL matches nothing.
  3. C A leading underscore would make them protected: not writable through REST and refused by block bindings: A leading underscore marks meta as protected: hidden from the Custom Fields box, not writable through REST without an auth_callback, and refused by block bindings. The shop wants these keys editable through the API and usable in bindings, so it leaves the underscore off.
  4. B The post type (or array of types) the taxonomy applies to: The second argument is the object type (or array of types) the taxonomy is attached to, here the book post type; that is what makes genre a taxonomy of books.
  5. C wp_set_object_terms( $id, $genres, 'genre' ): wp_set_object_terms( $id, $genres, 'genre' ) assigns terms directly, with no capability check, so it works in a script where no user is logged in.

Exit Ticket

  1. Three files: a class in src/ that implements Feature (its hooks in register()); one line in Plugin::feature_classes(); and, if the feature stores data, a line in uninstall.php that removes it.
  2. The role: activation creates it (Activator::activate()), deactivation keeps it (a pause), uninstall.php removes it (and the next activation creates it again).
  3. The 404: the rewrite rules in the database were saved before the book type existed and nothing rebuilt them. wp rewrite flush fixes it today; the permanent fix is the activator: register the type and taxonomy, then flush_rewrite_rules() (and flush again in deactivate()).

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A ("it activates, then dies"). Has not seen the header do work. Run Exercise 1 of Lesson 5.1 (Requires PHP 9.0) together.
  • Q3 B ("run Composer again"). Thinks the autoloader keeps a list of classes. Show vendor/composer/autoload_psr4.php: one line per PREFIX, not per class.
  • Q4 C or D. Reads "what must deactivation NOT do" as "what does it do". Point at the life-cycle line on the board.
  • Q7 A (/book/). Mixes up the type's name and its archive slug. wp rewrite list --match=/books/.
  • Q8 false. Expects get_post_meta() to return the registered type. It returns the stored string; REST casts (P4 is the same idea).
  • Q9 B (meta). Fine for a fact you only display; the /publisher/penguin/ page is the clue.
  • Q12 A (tax_input). It works in the editor. The script runs as no user: Lesson 5.2's Common Mistakes 2.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 10: Security and the Settings API

Answers and reasoning for every Session 10 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-run items, the lab reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 10 12 self-check items Confidential

Start Here · Warm-up

The five retrieval questions on Session 9, with the answers to give.

W1
A new feature for pagesco-books: which three places does it touch?
A class in src/ implementing Feature (hooks in register()), one line in Plugin::feature_classes(), and uninstall.php if it stores data.
W2
Why does Activator::activate() register the book type itself before it flushes?
On the activation request the plugin's own init callback has not run, so the rules would be rebuilt without the type.
W3
get_post_meta( 33, 'pagesco_in_stock', true ) on The Great Gatsby returns what?
'': meta is stored as strings, false as an empty string. REST casts it to false.
W4
Where did Lesson 5.2's meta box already use a nonce?
wp_nonce_field( 'pagesco_books_save_details', ... ) in render() and wp_verify_nonce() in save(): today's lesson hardens that save.
W5
Deactivating pagesco-books: what happens to the eleven books?
Nothing: they stay in wp_posts; the type is unregistered, so they leave the admin and /books/ is a 404 until it is active again.

5.3 · Security in WordPress: self-check

Q1
A book title goes into a link's tooltip: <a href="/books/dune/" title="...">. Which function escapes it there?
A) esc_html()
B) esc_url()
C) esc_attr()
D) sanitize_text_field()
Why: The destination decides: inside an attribute value it is esc_attr(), which also escapes the double quote that would end the attribute (Lesson 5.3, "Escape Late"). The meta box uses it on every value="…".
A is wrong: esc_html() is for text between tags; it happens to escape quotes too, but say what you mean: the attribute function.
B is wrong: esc_url() is for href and src, and would mangle a title into a URL.
D is wrong: Sanitizing cleans input on the way IN; output is escaped on the way out.
Q2
What does this print?
wp eval 'echo esc_html( "Pride & Prejudice <2nd ed.>" ), "\n";'
Pride &amp; Prejudice &lt;2nd ed.&gt;
Why: esc_html() turns &, < and > into entities: the browser shows the text, never runs it. In a terminal you see the entities themselves, which is how you check escaping from WP-CLI (the lesson's XSS demo did the same with the Books column).
Q3
A form handler calls check_admin_referer() and it passes. What does that prove?
A) The user is allowed to make the change
B) The request came from a page this site served to this user, for this action, recently
C) The submitted values are safe to store
D) The request was not sent by a script
Why: A nonce is tied to a user, an action and a few hours: it stops a forged request from another site (CSRF). Whether the user may do it is a separate question, current_user_can(); whether the values are clean is a third, sanitizing (Lesson 5.3, Common Mistakes 2).
A is wrong: Any logged-in user who can open the form gets a valid nonce.
C is wrong: A nonce says nothing about the values; sanitize and validate them.
D is wrong: A script with the user's cookies and a fresh nonce passes too; the nonce defeats forgery from ANOTHER site.
Q4
What does this print?
wp eval 'global $wpdb; echo $wpdb->prepare( "SELECT ID FROM {$wpdb->posts} WHERE post_title = %s", "Ender\x27s Game" ), "\n";'
SELECT ID FROM wp_posts WHERE post_title = 'Ender\'s Game'
Why: prepare() quoted the %s value and escaped the apostrophe inside it, so the title cannot end the string early: SQL injection is impossible through this placeholder. {$wpdb->posts} is WordPress's own value (the prefixed table name), never user input.
Q5
This handler is reached from a link on the Books screen. Which defense is missing?
$book_id = absint( $_GET['book'] );
check_admin_referer( 'pagesco_books_feature_' . $book_id );
update_post_meta( $book_id, 'pagesco_featured', 1 );
A) current_user_can( 'edit_post', $book_id ) before the update
B) A nonce check
C) esc_html() on the book ID
D) $wpdb->prepare() around the update
Why: The nonce is checked, the ID is forced to an integer, and update_post_meta() builds safe SQL itself; but nothing asks whether THIS user may edit THIS book. Database functions trust their caller (Lesson 5.3, Common Mistakes 3), so a subscriber with a valid link could feature any book.
B is wrong: check_admin_referer() is the nonce check.
C is wrong: Nothing is printed; esc_html() is for output.
D is wrong: update_post_meta() prepares its own query.
Q6
The meta box's "First published" field receives soon. What keeps it out of the database?
A) sanitize_text_field(): it removes words that are not dates
B) esc_attr() when the box prints the value
C) absint(), which turns it into 0
D) Validating it: DateTime::createFromFormat( 'Y-m-d', $v ) must give back exactly $v, else store ''
Why: Sanitizing cleans characters; it happily stores "soon". A value that must have a shape needs validating: accept or reject. The hardened save's valid_date() helper does exactly the round trip in option D (Lesson 5.3, Exercise 1).
A is wrong: sanitize_text_field() strips tags and extra whitespace, not words.
B is wrong: Escaping on output is still required, but it does not stop a bad value being stored.
C is wrong: A date is not an integer; absint() would store 0.

5.3 · Security in WordPress: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo esc_url( "javascript:alert(1)" ) === "" ? "(empty)" : "kept", "\n";'
(empty)
Why: esc_url() only lets through the protocols WordPress allows (http, https, mailto and a few more); a javascript: URL becomes an empty string, so an href built from it cannot run code.
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo sanitize_text_field( "  Pages <b>&</b> Co.\n\tBooks  " ), "|\n";'
Pages & Co. Books|
Why: sanitize_text_field() strips the tags (keeping their text), turns the line break and tab into nothing it keeps, and trims the outer spaces: one clean line. The ampersand stays a plain &: sanitizing is not escaping; escape when you print it.

5.4 · Admin Pages, Settings API & Options: self-check

Q7
An administrator presses Save Changes on Settings > Pages & Co. Books. Which code checks the nonce and the capability?
A) Core's own handler, wp-admin/options.php, before it calls your sanitize callback
B) Your SettingsPage::render() method
C) Your Settings::sanitize() method
D) Nothing: the Settings API has no nonce
Why: The form posts to options.php, which checks the nonce settings_fields() printed (action <group>-options), current_user_can( 'manage_options' ) and the allowed options list, then runs update_option() and with it your sanitize callback (Lesson 5.4, "What Save Changes Does").
B is wrong: render() only prints the form; its own capability check is defense in depth.
C is wrong: The sanitize callback cleans values; by the time it runs the checks have passed.
D is wrong: settings_fields() prints the nonce; options.php verifies it.
Q8
On the finished Lesson 5.4 site (the uninstall test deleted the row; nothing was saved since), what does this print?
wp option get pagesco_books_settings --format=json
{"notice_extra":"Open until 9 p.m. on Thursdays.","show_events_link":true,"books_per_page":6,"events_limit":5}
Why: register_setting()'s default is handed out while the row is missing, so WP-CLI prints the defaults although wp_options has no row named pagesco_books_settings (count it: 0). Nothing is written until someone saves.
Q9
The Events link checkbox is unticked when the form is saved. What does options.php receive for it?
A) show_events_link=0
B) show_events_link=false
C) Nothing: an unticked checkbox is not sent at all
D) show_events_link=off
Why: Browsers send only checked boxes. A sanitize callback that reads the key without a test warns (Undefined array key) on every save with the box off; ! empty( $input['show_events_link'] ) turns "missing" into false (Lesson 5.4, Common Mistakes 2).
A is wrong: That would need a hidden field with the same name before the box.
B is wrong: HTML forms send strings, never booleans.
D is wrong: "on" is what a checkbox WITHOUT a value attribute sends when it is ticked; unticked sends nothing.
Q10
On the finished Lesson 5.4 site, what does this print, and why is the notice sentence empty?
wp eval 'echo wp_json_encode( sanitize_option( "pagesco_books_settings",
    array( "books_per_page" => "0", "events_limit" => "7" ) ) ), "\n";'
{"notice_extra":"","show_events_link":false,"books_per_page":6,"events_limit":7}: 0 is out of range, so books_per_page keeps the saved value (6, the default); notice_extra and show_events_link were not in the array, so they come back empty and false
Why: sanitize() always returns the complete option: a missing key means empty (the form always sends every field, code does not). That is Common Mistakes 3: merge before you update, or wp option patch update. The rejected 0 also queued an add_settings_error() message.
Q11
Maria, an editor, opens options-general.php?page=pagesco-books and gets a 403. Which line decided that?
A) The current_user_can() check inside render()
B) The nonce in settings_fields()
C) register_setting()'s type 'object'
D) The manage_options capability passed to add_options_page()
Why: The capability argument guards both the menu item and the URL: WordPress refuses the page before your callback runs ("Sorry, you are not allowed to access this page."). The check inside render() is the second, defensive layer.
A is wrong: It would also refuse, but WordPress stops the request earlier.
B is wrong: No form is posted when a page is opened.
C is wrong: The type describes the value, for REST and sanitizing.
Q12
Which value deserves its own option instead of a key in the pagesco_books_settings array?
A) The notice sentence
B) A counter of page views, updated on every request
C) Books per list
D) Whether the notice shows the events link
Why: One array option per plugin is the usual choice: one row, one autoloaded read, one delete_option(). Split out a value that changes on every request (rewriting the whole array to bump a number is wasteful) or one that is large and rarely needed (autoload off) (Lesson 5.4, the box "One array option, or several?").
A is wrong: A setting the shop edits now and then: exactly what the array is for.
C is wrong: The same; Module 6 reads it through Settings::get().
D is wrong: The same; a boolean in the array.

5.4 · Admin Pages, Settings API & Options: predict, then run

P3
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'var_dump( get_option( "pagesco_no_such_option", array() ) );'
array(0) {
}
Why: The option does not exist, so get_option() returns its second argument, here an empty array (without it: false). Settings::get() relies on exactly this, then fills the keys with wp_parse_args().
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo wp_json_encode( sanitize_option( "pagesco_books_settings", "not an array" ) ), "\n";'
{"notice_extra":"","show_events_link":false,"books_per_page":6,"events_limit":5}
Why: The callback treats hostile input as hostile: not an array becomes an empty array, every key falls back to its cleaned value (an empty sentence, the box off) and the two numbers keep the saved values (the defaults). It returns a complete option either way.

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, on the site each declares (after:lesson_15 = after Lesson 5.2, after:lesson_16 = after Lesson 5.3, after:lesson_17 = after Lesson 5.4), with WordPress 7.1.3, PHP 8.5, MySQL 9.7 and WP-CLI 2.12. Paths show /var/www/html/..., the learner's wordpress/ folder.

Lab 5.3-A (guided): labs/module-10/l5_3-security/staff_note_starter.php → staff_note_solution.php

As shipped:

no token, as Maria              saved                stored: Signed copy, ask at the counter.
forged token, as Maria          saved                stored: Signed copy, ask at the counter.
real token, as Devon            saved                stored: Signed copy, ask at the counter.
real token, as Maria, with tag  saved                stored: Signed copy<script>alert(1)</script>, ask at the counter.

The solution prints:

no token, as Maria              refused: no token    stored: (nothing)
forged token, as Maria          refused: bad token   stored: (nothing)
real token, as Devon            refused: not allowed stored: (nothing)
real token, as Maria, with tag  saved                stored: Signed copy, ask at the counter.

The order matters: the cheap structural check first (is there a token at all?), then the nonce, then the capability for THIS book, then the values. Devon carries a real token (he could open the form) and is still refused: a nonce is not a permission. sanitize_text_field() removed the script tag on the way in; any place that prints the note still escapes it on the way out. Accept wp_verify_nonce() with or without sanitize_key(); the lesson's save uses sanitize_key( wp_unslash() ) on the real $_POST.

Lab 5.3-B: labs/module-10/l5_3-security/prepared_search_starter.php → prepared_search_solution.php

As shipped:

SELECT COUNT(*) FROM wp_postmeta WHERE meta_key = 'pagesco_price' AND meta_value + 0 < 9.990000
=> 5 books under $9.99
TODO
TODO

The solution prints:

SELECT COUNT(*) FROM wp_postmeta WHERE meta_key = 'pagesco_price' AND meta_value + 0 < 9.990000
=> 5 books under $9.99
SELECT p.post_title FROM wp_posts p INNER JOIN wp_postmeta m ON m.post_id = p.ID WHERE p.post_type = 'book' AND m.meta_key = 'pagesco_author' AND m.meta_value LIKE '%Tolkien%' ORDER BY p.post_title
=> Lord of the Rings, The Hobbit
SELECT p.post_title FROM wp_posts p INNER JOIN wp_postmeta m ON m.post_id = p.ID WHERE p.post_type = 'book' AND m.meta_key = 'pagesco_author' AND m.meta_value LIKE '%x\' OR \'1\'=\'1%' ORDER BY p.post_title
=> 0 rows

What to point at: %f printed 9.990000 (a number, never a string that could carry SQL); esc_like() before the % wildcards are added, so a word containing % or _ cannot widen the search; in the hostile line the apostrophes are backslash-escaped inside the quoted string, so OR '1'='1 is just text to compare and nothing matches. Without remove_placeholder_escape() the printed SQL shows a long random string where each literal % is: prepare() protects them until the query runs.

Handout: labs/module-10/l5_3-security/debug_book_search.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped, WordPress prints:

Under $9: 4 book(s): Pride and Prejudice, Animal Farm, The Great Gatsby, The Catcher in the Rye
Notice: Function wpdb::prepare was called <strong>incorrectly</strong>. The query argument of wpdb::prepare() must have a placeholder. Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information. (This message was added in version 3.9.0.) in /var/www/html/wp-includes/functions.php on line 6260
Notice: Function wpdb::prepare was called <strong>incorrectly</strong>. The query does not contain the correct number of placeholders (0) for the number of arguments passed (1). Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information. (This message was added in version 4.8.3.) in /var/www/html/wp-includes/functions.php on line 6260
In stock (whole catalog): 9
Notice: Function wpdb::prepare was called <strong>incorrectly</strong>. The query does not contain the correct number of placeholders (2) for the number of arguments passed (1). Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information. (This message was added in version 4.8.3.) in /var/www/html/wp-includes/functions.php on line 6260
Cheapest: $9
  1. Silent, line 15: absint( '9.99' ) is 9: an integer sanitizer for a decimal price, so the first query asks for books under $9 and finds four instead of five. Use (float) (and %f, which the query already has). The wrong sanitizer is as bad as none.
  2. "called incorrectly" twice, line 27: the in-stock query has no placeholder at all but is given an argument. WordPress says so ("must have a placeholder", then "the correct number of placeholders (0)"). A query with no values needs no prepare(): run it directly.
  3. "called incorrectly", line 35: two placeholders, one argument (the price is missing). prepare() refuses and returns nothing, and $wpdb->get_var( null ) then hands back a value of the PREVIOUS query (9, the in-stock count): a loud bug with a silent consequence.

Fixed (the three changes), it prints, without a message:

Under $9.99: 5 book(s): Pride and Prejudice, Animal Farm, The Great Gatsby, The Catcher in the Rye, Brave New World
In stock (whole catalog): 9
Cheapest: $7.99
Lab 5.4-A (guided): labs/module-10/l5_4-settings-api/newsletter_settings_starter/ → newsletter_settings_solution/ with test_newsletter_settings.sh

Reading it: the starter has no sanitize callback (TODO 1) and a callback that returns $input (TODO 2), so the tags and the impossible frequency were stored exactly as sent, and the unticked box simply vanished from the value. The solution's callback returns a complete array every time: the tags stripped, daily refused with an error and the saved monthly kept, the missing box as false. The two extra rows are TODO 3. Maria's 403 comes from manage_options in add_options_page(), in both versions. The test deletes the option at the end, so the learner's site is unchanged.

Handout: labs/module-10/l5_4-settings-api/debug_newsletter_sanitize.php (deliberately broken)

{"signup_text":"Join our list","frequency":"daily"}
[new lines in wp-content/debug.log]
[26-Oct-2026 13:35:54 UTC] PHP Notice:  Function register_setting was called <strong>incorrectly</strong>. When registering an "array" setting to show in the REST API, you must specify the schema for each array item in "show_in_rest.schema.items". Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information.
