The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 5.3 · Security in WordPress
  1. B At the point of output, for its context: Escape late: at the point of output, with the function for that context (esc_html, esc_attr, esc_url), because you cannot be sure where a stored value has been.
  2. A Any logged-in user, regardless of role, can perform the action: A valid nonce only proves the request came from this user's genuine page; any logged-in user, whatever their role, gets one. The capability check decides whether they are allowed to act.
  3. C $wpdb->prepare( $sql, $value ): The prepare() method inserts each value through a typed placeholder (%s, %d, %f, %i), quoting and escaping it so it cannot break out of the query.
  4. B An autosave carries no form fields and would wipe the saved values: save_post fires on autosaves and revisions, which carry no meta-box fields; without the guard an autosave would overwrite the saved values with empty ones.
  5. C No; you must check the capability yourself before calling it: No. wp_insert_post() and update_post_meta() trust the caller; you must call current_user_can() yourself before writing. The REST API and block editor add that check, but your own handlers must too.
Lesson 5.4 · Admin Pages, Settings API & Options
  1. A settings_fields( 'pagesco_books' ) and do_settings_sections( 'pagesco-books' ): settings_fields takes the option group from register_setting, which names the nonce and the allowed options list; do_settings_sections takes the page slug the sections and fields were added to.
  2. C 7, and the page shows the error: 0 fails the range check, so the callback queues the error and puts the saved value, 7, back. The other fields are still stored; the page shows the error instead of Settings saved.
  3. B A 403: Sorry, you are not allowed to access this page: add_options_page was given manage_options, which editors lack, so WordPress hides the menu item and answers the URL with a 403 Sorry, you are not allowed to access this page.
  4. C So keys added in a later version still have a value on sites that saved an older array: A site that saved the settings before a new key existed has an array without that key. Merging with the defaults gives every key a value, so code can rely on it.
  5. A Delete it, because it only makes sense with the plugin: The option belongs to the plugin and is useless without it, so uninstall deletes it. Deactivation keeps it, so a reactivated plugin finds its settings again.

Exit Ticket

  1. Four checks: genuine (the nonce: check_admin_referer() or wp_verify_nonce()), allowed (current_user_can() for this object), clean (sanitize, and validate what must have a shape), and safe output (escape for the context); with SQL by hand, every value through $wpdb->prepare().
  2. options.php: check_admin_referer( '<group>-options' ), current_user_can( 'manage_options' ) (filterable), the allowed options list built from register_setting(), then update_option() for each option of the group, which runs sanitize_option() and so your callback; then a redirect back with settings-updated=true, and the page prints "Settings saved." or the queued errors.
  3. A refused value: store the old (saved) value, never nonsense, and call add_settings_error( $option, $code, $message ); settings_errors() prints it above the form on the next page load.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A (esc_html). Works by accident here; ask what function the meta box uses in value="…" and why.
  • Q3 A ("the user is allowed"). The most important confusion of the lesson. Run the lesson's Common Mistakes 2 again.
  • Q5 B ("a nonce check"). Did not recognize check_admin_referer() as the nonce check; put the two functions side by side on the board.
  • Q6 A. Expects sanitizing to judge values. Exercise 1's validator makes the difference visible.
  • Q7 B or C. Thinks the plugin's code saves the form. Show the form's action attribute: options.php.
  • Q9 A. Expects HTML to send a zero. Show the request with the box unticked (the test's own post has no such field).
  • Q11 A. True as a second layer; the 403 comes before render() runs (the page never prints).
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 11: Custom Tables, Shortcodes and Block Setup

Answers and reasoning for every Session 11 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-run items, the lab reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 11 12 self-check items Confidential

Start Here · Warm-up

W1
A handler that changes data: the four checks, in order?
Genuine (nonce), allowed (current_user_can() for this object), sanitize and validate, escape on output; SQL by hand through $wpdb->prepare().
W2
settings_fields() takes the group or the page slug?
The group (pagesco_books), the first argument of register_setting(); do_settings_sections() takes the slug.
W3
What does an unticked checkbox send?
Nothing: missing means off (! empty( ... )).
W4
$wpdb->prepare(): which placeholder for a table name?
%i (WordPress 6.2+), today's lesson builds a whole table with it.
W5
What does uninstall.php remove, and what does it keep?
The plugin's own data (the settings option, the role); the shop's books stay. Today the events table joins the "remove" list.

5.5 · Shortcodes, $wpdb & Custom Tables: self-check

Q1
What does this print?
wp eval 'echo wp_json_encode( shortcode_atts( array( "limit" => 5, "location" => "" ),
    array( "limit" => "3", "color" => "red" ), "pagesco_events" ) ), "\n";'
{"limit":"3","location":""}
Why: shortcode_atts() keeps exactly the keys of the defaults: limit takes the given value, as the STRING "3" (attributes are always strings: absint() it before use), location keeps its default, and color, never declared, is dropped. The third argument creates the shortcode_atts_pagesco_events filter.
Q2
Which of these do you get for free with a post type, but must build yourself for a custom table?
A) A REST route, admin screens, revisions and export
B) Fast queries on an indexed column
C) A schema you control column by column
D) JOINs with wp_posts
Why: That is the price of a table (Lesson 5.5, "When a Custom Table Is Right"): the events got a shortcode now, a REST route in Lesson 7.2, and their cleanup in uninstall.php, all written by hand. In return the table has typed columns and an index on starts_at.
B is wrong: That is what the table GIVES you (KEY starts_at); meta queries sort a string column.
C is wrong: Also a benefit of the table, and a duty: you write the migrations.
D is wrong: Any table can be joined; the wishlist lab joins wp_posts.
Q3
On the finished Lesson 5.5 site, what does this print?
wp eval 'global $wpdb; echo $wpdb->prepare( "SELECT title FROM %i WHERE location = %s",
    PagesCo\Books\EventsTable::name(), "Café" ), "\n";'
SELECT title FROM `wp_pagesco_events` WHERE location = 'Café'
Why: %i quotes an identifier with backticks (WordPress 6.2+), %s quotes and escapes a value. The table name comes from EventsTable::name(), built from $wpdb->prefix, never a hard-coded wp_.
Q4
EventsTable::add() calls $wpdb->insert(). Where does it find the new row's ID?
A) In the return value of insert()
B) $wpdb->insert_id
C) $wpdb->last_query
D) Only with a second query, SELECT LAST_INSERT_ID()
Why: insert() returns the number of rows inserted (1) or false; the auto-increment ID of the new row is in $wpdb->insert_id right after it (the lesson's add() returns it, or 0 when the insert failed).
A is wrong: The return value is a count (or false), not an ID.
C is wrong: last_query is the SQL that ran, useful for debugging.
D is wrong: Not needed: wpdb reads it for you.
Q5
Why does EventsTable check the schema version on every request (maybe_upgrade() on init), and not only on activation?
A) Because dbDelta() must run on every request
B) Because the activation hook cannot create tables
C) Because a plugin update replaces the files but does not run the activation hook again
D) Because the version option is not autoloaded
Why: An update ships a higher DB_VERSION and a new schema(); the next request sees the stored version is lower and runs install() once. The check costs one autoloaded get_option() (Lesson 5.5, Project Step 1: no reactivation).
A is wrong: dbDelta() runs only when the stored version is older than the code.
B is wrong: Activation does create it: Activator::activate() calls install().
D is wrong: update_option() autoloads it, which is why the check is cheap.
Q6
On the finished Lesson 5.5 site, what does this print?
wp eval 'echo do_shortcode( "[pagesco_events location=\"Reading corner\" limit=\"1\"]" ), "\n";'
<ul class="pagesco-events"><li class="pagesco-event"><strong>Book club: Dune</strong> <time datetime="2027-11-04T19:00">Thursday, November 4, 7:00 pm</time> <span class="pagesco-event__where">Reading corner, 20 seats</span></li></ul>
Why: location narrows the list (an exact match, through a %s placeholder in EventsTable::find()), limit caps it; the dates come from mysql2date() in the shop's local time. Two events are in the Reading corner; the first by date is the Dune book club.

5.5 · Shortcodes, $wpdb & Custom Tables: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo do_shortcode( "[pagesco_book id=\"14\"]" ) === "" ? "(empty)" : "a card", "\n";'
(empty)
Why: Page 14 is a page, not a published book, so render() returns an empty string and the tag disappears: a visitor never sees an error because an editor mistyped an ID.
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'global $wpdb; echo $wpdb->get_var( "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM " . PagesCo\Books\EventsTable::name() ), "\n";'
6
Why: Six: the seeding script added six events after the uninstall test had recreated the table (IDs 1 to 6).

6.1 · Block Development Setup: self-check

Q7
Which file in build/blocks/book-card/ has no source file of its own in src/?
A) block.json
B) index.js
C) style-index.css
D) index.asset.php, the dependency list and version hash
Why: The build writes index.asset.php itself: the WordPress script handles index.js needs (react-jsx-runtime, wp-block-editor, wp-blocks, wp-i18n) and a hash of its content for cache busting. block.json is copied; index.js and style-index.css are compiled from the sources.
A is wrong: block.json is copied from src/blocks/book-card/block.json.
B is wrong: index.js is compiled from index.js, edit.js and save.js.
C is wrong: style-index.css is compiled from style.scss.
Q8
On the finished Lesson 6.1 site, what does this print?
wp eval 'echo implode( ", ", WP_Block_Type_Registry::get_instance()
    ->get_registered( "pagesco/book-card" )->style_handles ), "\n";'
pagesco-book-card-style
Why: Registration turned block.json's "style": "file:./style-index.css" into a registered style with a handle derived from the block name. That handle is what a block theme prints (inlined) on pages that contain the block, and only there.
Q9
You worked all afternoon with npm start running. What do you run before you commit or deploy?
A) npm run build: the minified production files, without .map files
B) npm install again
C) npx @wordpress/create-block again
D) Nothing: npm start already wrote build/
Why: npm start writes a development build (not minified, with source maps) and watches for changes; npm run build rewrites build/ with the small production files (Lesson 6.1, "While you work").
B is wrong: npm install only installs the tools; it builds nothing.
C is wrong: create-block scaffolds a NEW block.
D is wrong: It did, but the development version, with .map files that should not ship.
Q10
Posts already contain the Book Card. A developer renames the block from pagesco/book-card to pagesco/staff-pick. What happens to those posts?
A) WordPress renames the block in every post automatically
B) Nothing visible: names are cosmetic
C) Their stored comments still say wp:pagesco/book-card, a block that no longer exists: the editor cannot handle them
D) The posts are deleted
Why: The name is stored in every post that uses the block (), so it is a permanent identifier: never rename it once content exists (Lesson 6.1, Reading block.json). Change the title instead; it is only a label.
A is wrong: Nothing rewrites stored content for you.
B is wrong: The name is the block's identity; the title is the cosmetic part.
D is wrong: The content stays; only the editor no longer knows the block.
Q11
On the finished Lesson 6.1 site, what does this print, and why not 4?
grep -c "'wp-" wp-content/plugins/pagesco-books/build/blocks/book-card/index.asset.php
3: wp-block-editor, wp-blocks and wp-i18n; the fourth dependency, react-jsx-runtime, does not start with wp-
Why: The build replaced every @wordpress/* import with the matching global script handle and listed them; WordPress loads them before index.js. That is why the block's own file is about a kilobyte.
Q12
What does npx @wordpress/create-block@latest book-card --no-plugin --target-dir=src/blocks/book-card NOT create?
A) A block.json
B) A plugin PHP file, a package.json and node_modules/
C) An edit.js and a save.js
D) A view.js
Why: --no-plugin writes only the block's own files into the target folder: block.json, index.js, edit.js, save.js, view.js and the two SCSS files. The plugin, its package.json and the npm install are yours (Lesson 6.1, Building with @wordpress/scripts).
A is wrong: block.json is the first file it writes.
C is wrong: Both are scaffolded (edit for the editor, save for the stored HTML).
D is wrong: It writes view.js too; the project deletes it.

6.1 · Block Development Setup: predict, then run

P3
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo implode( ", ", array_keys( require WP_PLUGIN_DIR . "/pagesco-books/build/blocks/blocks-manifest.php" ) ), "\n";'
book-card
Why: The manifest is plain PHP that returns an array keyed by the block's folder in build/blocks: one block, book-card.
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
curl -s http://localhost:8080/ | grep -c 'pagesco-book-card-style'
0
Why: 0 (and grep exits 1): block themes print a block's styles only on pages that contain the block. The home page has no Book Card.

Lab 5.5-B: labs/module-11/l5_5-custom-tables/open_hours_starter/ → open_hours_solution/ with test_open_hours.sh

The test with the starter (the list is ECHOED: it appears before the quotes, and the callback returned ''):

[pagesco_open_hours day="saturday"] => <ul class="pagesco-hours"><li>Monday: 9:00-18:00</li><li>Tuesday: 9:00-18:00</li><li>Wednesday: 9:00-18:00</li><li>Thursday: 9:00-21:00</li><li>Friday: 9:00-18:00</li><li>Saturday: 10:00-16:00</li><li>Sunday: closed</li></ul>''

With the solution:

[pagesco_open_hours day="saturday"] => '<span class="pagesco-hours">Saturday: 10:00-16:00</span>'
[pagesco_open_hours day="SATURDAY"] => '<span class="pagesco-hours">Saturday: 10:00-16:00</span>'
[pagesco_open_hours day="holiday"] => ''
[pagesco_open_hours] => '<ul class="pagesco-hours"><li>Monday: 9:00-18:00</li><li>Tuesday: 9:00-18:00</li><li>Wednesday: 9:00-18:00</li><li>Thursday: 9:00-21:00</li><li>Friday: 9:00-18:00</li><li>Saturday: 10:00-16:00</li><li>Sunday: closed</li></ul>'

shortcode_atts() gives day its default; sanitize_key() lowercases SATURDAY; an unknown day returns '' instead of an error; the whole list is built as a string and returned.

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 5.5 · Shortcodes, $wpdb & Custom Tables
  1. B At the very top of the page, above the doctype: echo prints immediately, and a block theme renders the content before it prints the head, so the HTML ends up above the doctype; the shortcode's place in the content stays empty.
  2. C Thousands of bookings, each with the same few fields, listed by date: Many rows with the same fields, queried by those fields and not edited as content, are where a custom table beats post meta: real column types, indexes and simple SQL.
  3. A A higher DB_VERSION, so maybe_upgrade() runs install() again: maybe_upgrade only calls dbDelta when the stored version is lower than DB_VERSION. Without raising the constant, the new statement never runs.
  4. C {"limit":5,"location":""}: shortcode_atts compares keys exactly and keeps only declared ones, so LIMIT and color are both ignored and the defaults stay. In a real tag, the parser lowercases names before this step, which is why [pagesco_book ID=35] worked.
  5. B $wpdb->get_results( $wpdb->prepare( 'SELECT title FROM %i WHERE location = %s', $table, $loc ) ): prepare puts the table name in through %i and the visitor's value through %s, quoted and escaped, so it can never change the query.
Lesson 6.1 · Block Development Setup
  1. B build/blocks/ and its blocks-manifest.php: The Blocks feature passes build/blocks and its manifest to the registration function; src/ is only read by the build, which is why an unbuilt change never shows up.
  2. A To add the block to the existing pagesco-books plugin instead of creating a second plugin: Without it create-block scaffolds a separate plugin with its own PHP file and build setup; --no-plugin writes only the block files, so the block lives in pagesco-books with everything else.
  3. C It lists the WordPress scripts index.js depends on, and a version hash for caching: The build writes the script handles the file needs (wp-blocks, wp-block-editor...) and a content hash; WordPress loads those dependencies first and uses the hash as the version.
  4. B The build has not run since the change: Registration reads build/blocks-manifest.php and build/, which still hold the old title until npm run build (or npm start) runs again.
  5. C Only the styles from style-index.css; the HTML is already in the post: The HTML was stored when the editor saved the post; the front end only needs the shared styles, which a block theme adds (here inlined) only on pages that contain the block.

Exit Ticket

  1. Return, not echo: the callback runs while the content is filtered, before the page is printed; in a block theme the template is rendered before the <head>, so echoed text lands above the doctype (in a classic theme at the top of the content).
  2. DB_VERSION: raise it; maybe_upgrade() compares it with pagesco_books_db_version on init and runs install(), whose dbDelta() adds the column. Plugin updates replace files without running activation.
  3. The path: src/blocks/book-card → npm run build → build/blocks/book-card + blocks-manifest.php → Blocks::register_blocks() on init → registered type and handles → the editor loads index.js; visitors get the saved HTML and style-index.css.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 with limit as the number 3. Attributes are strings: absint() them.
  • Q2 B. Lists a benefit of tables as a cost: read the question again.
  • Q5 B. Thinks activation cannot create tables; it does, the problem is updates.
  • Q7 A. block.json is copied (and reformatted), not generated.
  • Q10 B. Thinks the name is a label; the title is.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 12: Static and Dynamic Blocks

Answers and reasoning for every Session 12 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-run items, the lab reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 12 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up: Lessons 5.5 and 6.1

  1. W1. The HTML appears at the top of the content (wherever the filter runs), not where the shortcode is: shortcodes RETURN their output. The [pagesco_events] callback returns it.
  2. W2. Because the location comes from shortcode attributes, REST arguments (Lesson 7.2) and anyone who edits a post: every value goes through placeholders, every time.
  3. W3. Into build/blocks/ (blocks-manifest.php and book-card on the projector site), and build/blocks/blocks-manifest.php, read by wp_register_block_types_from_metadata_collection() in the Blocks feature.
  4. W4. None: the manifest lists every built block and the Blocks feature registers them all. Today's Book List proves it.
  5. W5. Did you build? The editor loads build/blocks/book-card/index.js. npm start rebuilds on every save while you work.

The projector site's build folder (W3), and what [pagesco_events] returns, which proves it RETURNS its HTML (W1):

ls wp-content/plugins/pagesco-books/build/blocks
blocks-manifest.php  book-card
wp eval 'echo do_shortcode( "[pagesco_events limit=\"1\"]" ), "\n";'
<ul class="pagesco-events"><li class="pagesco-event"><strong>Book club: Dune</strong> <time datetime="2027-11-04T19:00">Thursday, November 4, 7:00 pm</time> <span class="pagesco-event__where">Reading corner, 20 seats</span></li></ul>

6.2 · Static Blocks: Attributes, Controls & Supports: self-check

Q1
A block attribute is declared "url": { "type": "string", "source": "attribute", "selector": "a", "attribute": "href" }. Where is the URL stored once the post is saved?
A) In the block comment, as JSON
B) In post meta, under the key url
C) In the saved HTML: the href of the first <a> inside the block
D) Nowhere: the editor asks for it again
Why: An attribute with a source lives in the HTML that save() wrote, and the editor finds it again through the selector. Only attributes without a source go into the comment (and only when they differ from their default).
A is wrong: Only source-less attributes such as headingLevel go into the comment.
B is wrong: Post meta needs a binding or code of your own; "source": "meta" is the deprecated old way.
D is wrong: Then every saved card would lose its link.
Q2
On the course site after Lesson 6.2: what does this print?
wp eval 'echo wp_json_encode( parse_blocks( "<!-- wp:pagesco/book-card {\"showBadge\":false} --><div class=\"wp-block-pagesco-book-card\"><h3 class=\"wp-block-pagesco-book-card__title\">Emma</h3></div><!-- /wp:pagesco/book-card -->" )[0]["attrs"] ), "\n";'
{"showBadge":false}
Why: parse_blocks() returns the comment's JSON only. The title Emma is sourced from the HTML, so it is not in attrs; showBadge is there because false differs from its default, true. A card with the badge on would print [].
Q3
Cards are already on the site. Which change to the Book Card needs a new deprecated entry?
A) A new rule in style.scss that rounds the corners more
B) A new sidebar control in edit.js only
C) Wrapping the author in a <cite> in save()
D) A new attribute whose default makes save() print nothing new
Why: Validation compares the stored HTML with what the CURRENT save() returns for the same attributes. A new element changes that HTML, so every stored card turns invalid until a deprecation describes the old save().
A is wrong: CSS is not part of the stored HTML.
B is wrong: edit is never compared with anything.
D is wrong: Lesson 6.2's url and headingLevel were exactly that: no deprecation needed.
Q4
A designer wants the card's heading level in the toolbar that floats above the selected block, like core's Heading block. Where does the control go?
A) Inside <BlockControls> in edit
B) Inside <InspectorControls> in save
C) Inside <InspectorControls> in edit
D) In block.json under supports
Why: BlockControls fills the block toolbar, InspectorControls the sidebar's Settings tab; both belong in edit, which is the editor's interface. save never renders controls.
B is wrong: save returns the stored HTML; controls there would be saved into the post.
C is wrong: That puts it in the sidebar, where the lesson's Card settings panel is.
D is wrong: supports switch on core's own features (colors, spacing); there is no support for a custom control.
Q5
An editor picks the theme's Sage background for a card. What does the post store?
A) The hex color in an inline style
B) The class has-sage-background-color in the HTML and "backgroundColor":"sage" in the comment
C) A row in wp_options
D) Nothing: the theme decides at view time
Why: Preset choices are saved as a slug and a class; the theme's global styles define .has-sage-background-color. Change Sage's hex value in theme.json and every sage card follows, which is why supports beat hand-made color attributes.
A is wrong: Inline styles are for values that are not presets, such as a custom padding per side.
C is wrong: Block settings are content: they live in post_content.
D is wrong: The choice is the editor's, so it has to be stored with the card.
Q6
Why does the course write the badge text Staff pick as a plain string in save() instead of __( 'Staff pick', 'pagesco-books' )?
save() must return the same HTML for the same attributes in every language: a translated string makes cards invalid as soon as someone edits in another locale
Why: Validation re-runs save() in the editor of whoever opens the post. Text in save is content: a literal, or an attribute. Translate edit's labels and placeholders freely (Common Mistake 3).

6.2 · Static Blocks: Attributes, Controls & Supports: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo wp_json_encode( parse_blocks( "<!-- wp:heading --><h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">New this week</h2><!-- /wp:heading -->" )[0]["attrs"] ), "\n";'
[]
Why: A heading at its default level 2 stores nothing in the comment, and its text is rich-text sourced from the h2: [] (an empty array as JSON). Save the same heading as an h3 and you would get {"level":3}.
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo wp_json_encode( WP_Block_Type_Registry::get_instance()->get_registered( "core/paragraph" )->attributes["content"] ), "\n";'
{"type":"rich-text","source":"rich-text","selector":"p","role":"content"}
Why: Core declares the paragraph's text with the source rich-text and the selector p: it lives in the HTML. role: content marks it as the block's content (the editor's content-only editing and pattern overrides use it).

6.3 · Dynamic Blocks & Block Bindings: self-check

Q7
The Book List already sits on twenty pages. You change render.php so every item gets a second line. What else must you do?
A) Add a deprecated entry to the block
B) Open and update each of the twenty pages
C) Run npm run build (render.php is copied into build/), then nothing: every page follows on its next view
D) Bump the block's version so caches notice
Why: A dynamic block stores only its comment, so there is no stored HTML to validate. The build copies render.php into build/blocks/book-list/, the file WordPress runs; after that, the next view of every page uses it.
A is wrong: Deprecations describe old save() output; a dynamic block saves none.
B is wrong: Nothing in the pages changed: they hold only the comment.
D is wrong: The version only changes the ?ver= of the block's CSS and JS.
Q8
On the course site after Lesson 6.3 (six Classic books): what does this print?
wp eval 'echo substr_count( do_blocks( "<!-- wp:pagesco/book-list {\"genre\":\"classic\",\"count\":4} /-->" ), "<li class=" ), "\n";'
4
Why: do_blocks() runs render.php with the comment's attributes, exactly as a page view does. count 4 is inside the 1 to 24 clamp, and the genre filter is a tax_query on the slug, so four of the six Classic books.
Q9
In render.php the wrapper is <ul <?php echo get_block_wrapper_attributes(); ?>>, with no escaping function around it. Why is that safe?
A) It is not: wrap it in esc_html()
B) Block attributes are always numbers
C) Only administrators can add blocks
D) get_block_wrapper_attributes() returns attributes that are already escaped (class and style from the supports)
Why: It is the PHP twin of useBlockProps.save(): it builds the class and style attributes and escapes them itself. Core's own blocks print it the same way. Everything else render.php prints is escaped where it is printed.
A is wrong: esc_html() would turn the quotes into entities and break the markup.
B is wrong: Attributes can be strings typed by anyone who can edit posts (genre, for example).
C is wrong: Any editor or author can insert blocks; escaping is about the data, not the person.
Q10
A list item is bound to core/post-meta with the key pagesco_in_stock. What does it show on The Great Gatsby's page (sold out)?
A) Sold out
B) The fallback text of the list item
C) false
D) Nothing: an empty list item
Why: core/post-meta returns the raw stored value: false arrives as an empty string, so the list item is empty (Dune's would say 1). Binding is not formatting; that is why the shop writes its own source, pagesco/book.
A is wrong: That wording comes from the shop's own source, not from core/post-meta.
B is wrong: The fallback shows only when the source returns null (an unknown or protected key).
C is wrong: PHP's false becomes an empty string when it is printed.
Q11
You register a binding source without uses_context. What does a bound paragraph in single-book.html show?
A) Its fallback text, with no error
B) A PHP warning about the missing context
C) The value for the first book in the catalog
D) The value for the post being shown, as usual
Why: A paragraph never asks for postId on its own. uses_context makes WordPress hand postId and postType to every block bound to the source; without it the callback sees no post, returns null, and the fallback stays: silently.
B is wrong: Bindings fail silently by design.
C is wrong: The callback has no post ID at all.
D is wrong: Only with uses_context.
Q12
On the course site after Lesson 6.3: what does this print (book 40 is Animal Farm, published 1945-08-17)?
<?php
$parsed = parse_blocks( '<!-- wp:paragraph {"metadata":{"bindings":{"content":{"source":"pagesco/book","args":{"field":"year"}}}}} --><p>First published</p><!-- /wp:paragraph -->' )[0];
$block  = new WP_Block( $parsed, array( 'postId' => 40, 'postType' => 'book' ) );

echo wp_strip_all_tags( $block->render() ), "\n";
First published in 1945
Why: new WP_Block( $parsed, $context ) renders the paragraph with a made-up context, so pagesco/book's year field runs for book 40 and replaces the fallback. It is Lesson 6.3's Exercise 1 technique, the way a PHPUnit test would check a source (Lesson 8.1).

6.3 · Dynamic Blocks & Block Bindings: predict, then run

P3
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo substr_count( do_blocks( "<!-- wp:pagesco/book-list {\"count\":30} /-->" ), "<li class=" ), "\n";'
11
Why: render.php clamps count to 1 to 24, and the catalog has eleven books, so all eleven print. The clamp protects the query from a huge number typed into a comment by hand.
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo get_post_meta( 37, "pagesco_in_stock", true ) === "" ? "empty string" : "something else", "\n";'
empty string
Why: Booleans are stored as '1' and '' (the empty string), which is why core/post-meta leaves an empty list item for Gatsby and Catcher, and why Exercise 3 compares pagesco_in_stock with '1'.

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, on a fresh course site at the state each file declares (Lesson 6.2's labs at after:lesson_19, Lesson 6.3's at after:lesson_20: what each lesson starts from), copied into the WordPress root and run with wp eval-file --use-include (PHP labs) or sh in the WP-CLI container (shell labs); folder labs installed and activated first. Paths show /var/www/html/..., which is the learner's wordpress/ folder.

Lab 6.2-A (guided): labs/module-12/l6_2-static-blocks/attribute_sources_starter.php → attribute_sources_solution.php

As shipped:

core/heading content: TODO
core/quote content: TODO
core/button content: TODO
parse_blocks() sees: TODO

The solution prints:

core/heading content: in the HTML (rich-text, h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6)
core/heading level: in the comment (default 2)
core/quote value: in the HTML (html, blockquote)
core/quote citation: in the HTML (rich-text, cite)
core/button tagName: in the comment (default "a")
core/button type: in the comment (default "button")
core/button url: in the HTML (attribute, a @href)
core/button title: in the HTML (attribute, a,button @title)
core/button text: in the HTML (rich-text, a,button)
core/button linkTarget: in the HTML (attribute, a @target)
core/button rel: in the HTML (attribute, a @rel)
parse_blocks() sees:
core/heading {"level":3}
core/image {"id":42,"sizeSlug":"large"}

Reading it: core uses rich-text as a source too (the editor's rich-text value, stored as HTML like html); the quote's whole inner HTML is one attribute; the button's tagName and type are settings, so comment attributes with defaults. parse_blocks() returns {"level":3} and the image's id and size: the src, the alt and the heading's text are in the HTML only. Accept any equivalent formatting of TODOs 2 and 3; array_key_exists() matters because a default can be false.

Lab 6.2-B: labs/module-12/l6_2-static-blocks/card_price_starter/ → card_price_solution/ with test_card_price.sh

The test with the starter's pagesco-card-price.php in mu-plugins/:

The cards and what follows them:
<h3 class="wp-block-pagesco-book-card__title">The Hobbit</h3>
<h3 class="wp-block-pagesco-book-card__title">A book without a link</h3>
price lines: 0

With the solution's:

The cards and what follows them:
<h3 class="wp-block-pagesco-book-card__title">The Hobbit</h3>
<p class="pagesco-card-price">Today: $10.99</p>
<h3 class="wp-block-pagesco-book-card__title">A book without a link</h3>
price lines: 1

Reading it: render_block_pagesco/book-card passes the card's stored HTML; WP_HTML_Tag_Processor finds the link by class and reads its href (never a regular expression on HTML); url_to_postid() turns the book's address into its ID; the price is read on every view, so it is live although the card is static. The card without a link gets nothing. It is a dynamic ADDITION to a static block, a preview of Lesson 6.3's reason for dynamic blocks; the test creates and force-deletes its own post.

Handout: labs/module-12/l6_2-static-blocks/debug_card_report.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped, PHP prints:

Warning: Undefined array key "title" in /var/www/html/debug_card_report.php on line 28
Warning: Undefined array key "innerHtml" in /var/www/html/debug_card_report.php on line 30
Deprecated: strlen(): Passing null to parameter #1 ($string) of type string is deprecated in /var/www/html/debug_card_report.php on line 30
: h2, 0 characters of HTML
Warning: Undefined array key "title" in /var/www/html/debug_card_report.php on line 28
Warning: Undefined array key "innerHtml" in /var/www/html/debug_card_report.php on line 30
Deprecated: strlen(): Passing null to parameter #1 ($string) of type string is deprecated in /var/www/html/debug_card_report.php on line 30
: h2, 0 characters of HTML
  1. Warning, line 28: $block['attrs']['title']: the title is sourced from the HTML, so it is never in the comment's attrs (Lesson 6.2, Common Mistake 1). Read it with WP_HTML_Processor (the fix below), as the lesson's Exercise 1 does.
  2. Warning + Deprecated, line 30: innerHtml is spelled innerHTML in a parsed block; the missing key gives null, and strlen( null ) is deprecated since PHP 8.1. One bug, two messages.
  3. Silent, line 29: ?? 2: the card's default heading level is 3 (block.json), and a card at its default stores no headingLevel. The second card is an h3 but the report says h2. Use ?? 3, or better, the registered default.

Fixed (all three), it prints:

Dune: h2, 166 characters of HTML
Emma: h3, 164 characters of HTML

The fixed script is in the generator (blocks_S12.py, CARD_REPORT_FIXED), run on the same site.

<p class="wp-block-pagesco-next-events">No events at this location yet.</p>
count 99: 6 events

Reading it: no count means the Events to show setting (5, the default); the location is sanitized and passed to EventsTable::find(), which prepares the SQL (Lesson 5.5); the empty case prints a sentence inside the same wrapper; count 99 is clamped to 10, and the table has six events. Common answers to accept: ?? (int) Settings::get(...) instead of isset(); wp_date() is wrong here (starts_at is already local time; mysql2date() formats it as stored). Remove the plugin afterwards.

Lab 6.3-B: labs/module-12/l6_3-dynamic-blocks/core_sources_starter.php → core_sources_solution.php

As shipped:

Dune      pagesco_author     [TODO]
Dune      pagesco_price      [TODO]
Gatsby    pagesco_in_stock   [TODO]
Dune      pagesco_in_stock   [TODO]
Dune      _edit_lock         [TODO]
Dune      pagesco_writer     [TODO]
Dune      link               [TODO]

The solution prints:

Dune      pagesco_author     [Frank Herbert]
Dune      pagesco_price      [14.99]
Gatsby    pagesco_in_stock   []
Dune      pagesco_in_stock   [1]
Dune      _edit_lock         [No data]
Dune      pagesco_writer     [No data]
Dune      link               [http://localhost:8080/books/dune/]

Reading it: core/post-meta returns raw values (14.99 without a dollar sign; the boolean as '' or 1); a protected key (_edit_lock) and an unknown key both return null, so the fallback "No data" stays, silently; core/post-data's link field gives the permalink. The same rendering with a made-up context is how the lesson's Exercise 1 tests pagesco/book.

Handout: labs/module-12/l6_3-dynamic-blocks/debug_genre_counts.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped, PHP prints:

Warning: Undefined array key "limit" in /var/www/html/debug_genre_counts.php on line 18
Warning: Undefined variable $pagesco_items in /var/www/html/debug_genre_counts.php on line 32
<ul class="wp-block-pagesco-lab-genres"><li>Fiction: 11</li></ul>
<ul class="wp-block-pagesco-lab-genres"><li>Fiction: 11</li></ul>
  1. Warning, line 18: $attributes['limit'] on a block without a limit: the attribute has no default, so the key is missing (Lesson 6.3, Common Mistake 1). isset( $attributes['limit'] ) ? ... : 3.
  2. Warning, line 32: $pagesco_items .= on a variable that was never set. Add $pagesco_items = ''; before the loop. (PHP shows the warning once per line with this site's settings, so it appears once although both blocks hit it.)
  3. Silent, line 19: min( 1, max( 10, ... ) ) is the clamp written backwards: it always gives 1, so both blocks show one genre. min( 10, max( 1, $limit ) ).

Fixed, the two blocks print three and four genres, the biggest first:

<ul class="wp-block-pagesco-lab-genres"><li>Fiction: 11</li><li>Classic: 6</li><li>Science Fiction: 3</li></ul>
<ul class="wp-block-pagesco-lab-genres"><li>Fiction: 11</li><li>Classic: 6</li><li>Science Fiction: 3</li><li>Dystopian: 3</li></ul>

Dystopian and Science Fiction both have three books; their order among themselves is the database's, so accept either. The fixed script is GENRES_FIXED in blocks_S12.py.

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 6.2 · Static Blocks: Attributes, Controls & Supports
  1. A In the saved HTML, inside the element the selector points to: A sourced attribute lives only in the saved HTML; the editor reads it back with the selector, and the comment JSON stays free of it.
  2. B A ToggleControl inside InspectorControls, in edit: Whatever edit renders inside InspectorControls appears in the sidebar while the block is selected; PanelBody groups it into a collapsible panel.
  3. C Spreading useBlockProps.save() onto the wrapper in save: Supports add their classes and styles through useBlockProps.save(); a wrapper without it saves none of them.
  4. C {"headingLevel":4}: parse_blocks returns exactly the comment JSON: the sourced title stays in the HTML, and defaults such as showBadge are not filled in.
  5. B A deprecated entry with the previous attributes, supports and save: A deprecated entry with the old attributes, supports and save lets the editor recognize existing cards and open them as valid blocks of the new version.
Lesson 6.3 · Dynamic Blocks & Block Bindings
  1. B Only the block comment with its attributes: A dynamic block saves only its comment with the non-default attributes; render.php produces the HTML on every view.
  2. A 8: count has no default, so it is missing from the comment and render.php uses Settings::get( 'books_per_page' ), now 8.
  3. B The paragraph's fallback text: Keys starting with an underscore are protected; core/post-meta returns null for them, so the paragraph keeps its fallback text, silently.
  4. C Sold out: The context names book 37, the source reads pagesco_in_stock, finds it false and returns the formatted Sold out, which replaces the paragraph's content.
  5. C When something asks for the block's variations, such as the editor: block.json names the file, and WordPress requires it lazily when the variations are requested, for example by the editor through the block-types REST route.

Exit Ticket

  1. Title and level: the title in the saved HTML, inside the element its selector names; the heading level in the block comment, only when it is not the default 3.
  2. render.php and attributes: check optional attributes (isset() or a default in block.json), cast and clamp numbers, sanitize strings (sanitize_key() for a slug), and escape everything printed; start the wrapper with get_block_wrapper_attributes().
  3. A fallback on a book page: a misspelled meta key, a key not registered with show_in_rest or starting with an underscore (core/post-meta), a custom source without uses_context, or a source that returned null for that field.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A ("in the comment"). Thinks every attribute is JSON. Show the stored Card test post: the URL is in the href, the comment is empty.
  • Q2 with the title in it. The same picture: parse_blocks() never reads the HTML. Run the command.
  • Q3 A or B. Thinks validation is about files. It compares stored HTML with save(): CSS and edit are never compared.
  • Q5 A ("the hex color"). Remembers inline styles; presets are classes and slugs, so the theme can change the color later.
  • Q7 A ("a deprecation"). Carries Lesson 6.2's rule over to dynamic blocks; there is no stored HTML to validate.
  • Q9 A ("wrap it in esc_html()"). Good instinct, wrong place: the function escapes its own output, and double escaping breaks the attribute.
  • Q10 A ("Sold out"). Confuses core/post-meta with the shop's source. Show Gatsby's first template version: an empty item.

Session 13: The Interactivity API and Using the REST API

Answers and reasoning for every Session 13 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-run items, Checkpoint 2's rubric and model answer, the lab reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 13 12 self-check items Checkpoint 2 Confidential

Warm-up: Lessons 6.2 and 6.3

  1. W1. Only the comment's: {"backgroundColor":"sage"}. Title, author, recommendation and link are in the HTML; headingLevel and showBadge are at their defaults.
  2. W2. When content with the old HTML exists that must stay editable (a client's posts): always. While the block exists only on a test site: delete the test posts instead.
  3. W3. render.php, on every view: no count means the Books per list setting (6), clamped to 1 to 24.
  4. W4. 'uses_context' => array( 'postId', 'postType' ) in register_block_bindings_source(); without it the fallback stays, silently.
  5. W5. $10.99: the large paragraph is bound to pagesco/book's price field, which formats pagesco_price on every view.

wp eval '$b = wp_list_filter( parse_blocks( get_page_by_path( "staff-picks", OBJECT, "post" )->post_content ), array( "blockName" => "pagesco/book-card" ) ); echo wp_json_encode( reset( $b )["attrs"] ), "\n";'
{"backgroundColor":"sage"}
curl -s http://localhost:8080/books/the-hobbit/ | grep -o 'has-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">[^<]*'
has-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">$10.99

6.4 · The Interactivity API: self-check

Q1
A visitor opens /find-a-book/?genre=fantasy. Where does the browser's store get state.genre's starting value from?
A) From the address bar, read by view.js
B) From the state JSON that render.php's wp_interactivity_state() put into the page
C) From localStorage
D) From a REST request the store sends on load
Why: render.php validates ?genre= and passes it to wp_interactivity_state(); WordPress prints the plain values as JSON (the script tag with the id wp-script-module-data-@wordpress/interactivity), and the runtime merges it into the store's state before anything runs.
A is wrong: view.js only WRITES the address (history.replaceState()).
C is wrong: Nothing in the block uses browser storage.
D is wrong: The Book Filter makes no requests: all books are in the HTML (Lesson 7.2 designs the REST version).
Q2
Which of these directives does the SERVER not evaluate when it renders the block?
A) data-wp-bind--hidden="!state.isVisible"
B) data-wp-text="state.visibleCount"
C) data-wp-class--is-selected="state.isSelected"
D) data-wp-on--click="actions.selectGenre"
Why: Events only exist in the browser, so data-wp-on (and watch, init, run) are browser-only. bind, class, style, text, context, each and interactive are processed on the server too, which is why the page is correct before any JavaScript runs.
A is wrong: The server writes hidden on the nine non-fantasy books.
B is wrong: The server fills the span with 2.
C is wrong: The server adds class="is-selected" to the Fantasy link.
Q3
On the course site after Lesson 6.4 (three Dystopian books of eleven): what does this print?
curl -s 'http://localhost:8080/find-a-book/?genre=dystopian' | grep -c '<li hidden'
8
Why: All eleven books are in the HTML; the server ran data-wp-bind--hidden for each and wrote hidden on the eight that are not dystopian. curl runs no JavaScript: this is what every visitor gets first.
Q4
Each genre link must know its own slug. Where does that slug belong?
A) In the element's context, wp_interactivity_data_wp_context( array( 'slug' => $slug ) )
B) In the state, as one key per genre
C) In a global JavaScript variable
D) In the link's class name
Why: State is shared by the whole store (one genre is selected for everyone); context is data attached to one element and visible to the directives inside it. getContext() reads it in getters and actions, wp_interactivity_get_context() in the PHP closures.
B is wrong: Works, but every link's directives would need to name their own key: context is what per-element data is for.
C is wrong: The runtime would not track it, so nothing would update.
D is wrong: Classes are for styling; the store cannot read them as data.
Q5
An action calls event.preventDefault() to stop a link. What does WordPress 6.8 and later require for it?
A) Nothing: actions always run synchronously
B) data-wp-on-async--click instead of data-wp-on--click
C) Wrapping the action in withSyncEvent()
D) Returning false from the action
Why: Since 6.8 the runtime may run actions after the browser has handled the event, too late to prevent anything. withSyncEvent() says the action needs the event right away; without it 7.1 still works but logs a deprecation warning (Common Mistake 2).
A is wrong: That was true before 6.8.
B is wrong: on-async is for actions that need NOTHING from the event.
D is wrong: Return values of actions are ignored.
Q6
A visitor's browser blocks the Book Filter's script module, and she clicks Fantasy. What happens, and why?
The link is followed (a page load) and the server renders the filtered page: the links are real ?genre= links and render.php reads the address
Why: Progressive enhancement: the HTML works on its own; JavaScript only makes it faster. It came almost for free because the state starts in PHP and the derived values have PHP closures.

6.4 · The Interactivity API: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
curl -s 'http://localhost:8080/find-a-book/?genre=romance' | grep -o 'visibleCount">[0-9]*'
visibleCount">11
Why: render.php accepts only slugs of genres that have books, so romance falls back to All books: 11. Nothing from the address reaches the page unchecked (the lesson's Exercise 1).
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo wp_interactivity_process_directives( "<div data-wp-interactive=\"pagesco/x\"><b data-wp-text=\"state.missing\">old text</b></div>" ), "\n";'
<div data-wp-interactive="pagesco/x"><b data-wp-text="state.missing"></b></div>
Why: The server evaluates data-wp-text against the store's state; state.missing has no value, so the element's text is REPLACED by nothing: the old text is gone. No message: check the namespace and the state key when a text is empty.

7.1 · Using the REST API: self-check

Q7
Anonymously, GET /wp-json/wp/v2/books/30?context=edit. What comes back?
A) 200 with the raw title and the content as block markup
B) 404 rest_no_route
C) 401 rest_forbidden_context
D) 403 rest_cannot_edit
Why: context=edit returns raw values only to users who could edit the item. Nobody is logged in, so it is "who are you?": 401. Maria with an application password gets the raw fields; Devon, an author, 403.
A is wrong: Only for users who may edit the book.
B is wrong: The route exists; only the context is refused.
D is wrong: 403 means "I know you, and no": the request is anonymous.
Q8
On the course site (six Classic books, genre ID 7): what does this print?
curl -s 'http://localhost:8080/wp-json/wp/v2/books?genres=7&per_page=4&_fields=id' -D - -o /dev/null | grep -i '^X-WP-TotalPages'
X-WP-TotalPages: 2
Why: X-WP-TotalPages is the number of pages at this per_page: six books in pages of four are two pages. The body (sent to /dev/null here) would hold the first four; a client loops up to the header's number.
Q9
A Dashboard page sends Maria's cookie and an X-WP-Nonce header with an old, made-up value. What does the REST API answer?
A) 403 rest_cookie_invalid_nonce: the page must fetch a fresh nonce
B) 200: the cookie is enough
C) 401 rest_not_logged_in, as if no cookie had been sent
D) 500: the nonce check crashed
Why: With no nonce, the cookie is ignored and the request is anonymous. With a WRONG nonce, WordPress refuses outright, so a stale page knows it must refresh (apiFetch does that by itself through the rest-nonce endpoint).
B is wrong: The nonce is what protects the cookie from CSRF.
C is wrong: That is the answer WITHOUT a nonce.
D is wrong: A failed check is a normal error answer.
Q10
/wp/v2/books/30?_embed=wp:term&_fields=id,title answers without any genres. Why?
A) The book has no genres
B) _embed works only for authenticated requests
C) wp:term is not an embeddable relation
D) _fields removed _links, so there was nothing to follow, and _embedded is cut off too
Why: Since 5.4 WordPress embeds only links that survive _fields. Add _links and _embedded to the list: _fields=id,title,_links,_embedded (Common Mistake 1).
A is wrong: Dune is Fiction and Science Fiction.
B is wrong: Public data embeds anonymously.
C is wrong: The genre link says embeddable: true.
Q11
Why can you create application passwords on the course site although it runs on plain http://localhost:8080?
A) Application passwords never need HTTPS
B) WP_ENVIRONMENT_TYPE is local; elsewhere WordPress offers them only on HTTPS
C) Docker encrypts the traffic
D) Because the administrator created them
Why: Basic authentication only encodes the password; on plain HTTP anyone on the way can read it. WordPress therefore offers application passwords on HTTPS sites or where the environment type is local, as compose.yaml sets it (Lesson 1.2).
A is wrong: They do, outside local environments.
C is wrong: The containers talk plain HTTP.
D is wrong: Maria created hers with WP-CLI; the rule is about the connection.
Q12
Name two things wp.apiFetch() adds to a request that a plain fetch() on the Dashboard does not.
The REST nonce (X-WP-Nonce) for the logged-in user, and the API's root URL (also _locale=user, a JSON body and the server's error object on failure)
Why: The page prints apiFetch's setup when a script depends on wp-api-fetch: createRootURLMiddleware, the nonce middleware, the nonce endpoint for refreshing. Without the nonce the request is anonymous (Common Mistake 2).

7.1 · Using the REST API: predict, then run

P3
Predict the output, then run it.
curl -s 'http://localhost:8080/wp-json/wp/v2/books?search=the&per_page=1&_fields=id' -D - -o /dev/null | grep -i '^X-WP-TotalPages'
X-WP-TotalPages: 4
Why: search matches the title, content and excerpt; four books contain "the" in their title (or text), so at one per page X-WP-TotalPages is 4. The header answers "how many" without downloading the books.
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
curl -s 'http://localhost:8080/wp-json/wp/v2/genres/8?_fields=name,count'; echo
{"count":2,"name":"Fantasy"}
Why: One term by its ID, two fields: _fields works on every route, single items included. Fantasy is genre 8 with two books.

Checkpoint 2: Rubric and Model Answer

Five rows, 0 to 2 points each (10 in all). Score from the learner's file (run it once on your projector site at the end of Lesson 6.3; it changes nothing) and the Part D page. 8 to 10: on track. 5 to 7: review the weakest row before Session 14. Under 5: a one-to-one before Session 15.

CriterionStrong (2)Partial (1)Missing (0)
Part A · the query (5.2, 5.3)One get_posts()/WP_Query: meta_query with price <= max as DECIMAL (or NUMERIC) and stock '1'; meta_key + orderby meta_value_num; output matches line ARight books but sorted by title, or the price compared as a string, or two queries merged in PHPNo meta_query (filters in PHP after loading all books), or wrong books
Part B · safe SQL (5.5)$wpdb->prepare() with %i for the table (or the table name from EventsTable::name()) and %s for the location; get_var(); (int); the hostile location counts 0 events and breaks nothingPrepared, but the table name hard-coded as wp_pagesco_events, or get_results() + count()The location concatenated into SQL, or Part B not working
Part C · blocks (6.2, 6.3)C1: the comment built with wp_json_encode() and rendered with do_blocks(), titles read from the output; C2: parse_blocks() of the post, filtered by blockName, count and the first card's attrsOne of C1 and C2 right, or C1 calls get_posts() itself instead of rendering the blockNeither, or the card's title read from attrs (Lesson 6.2's Mistake 1)
Output and escapingThe four lines match the model answer; prices formatted with two decimals; nothing printed unescaped that came from a user (the location is only counted)One line differs (a missing $, an extra space), or one value printed without esc_html() in a function that builds HTMLSeveral lines differ or the script prints a PHP message
Part D · written (Modules 5 and 6)D1 to D3 match the model answers, each with its reasonTwo right, or right answers without reasonsOne or none
Model answer: labs/module-13/checkpoint-2/checkpoint_solution.php
<?php
/**
 * checkpoint_solution.php: Checkpoint 2 (Modules 5 and 6), the Weekly Picks report: MODEL ANSWER (Session 13)
 * state: after:lesson_21
 * expect: A  Under $10, in stock: Pride and Prejudice ($7.99), Animal Farm ($8.49), Brave New World ($9.49), 1984 ($9.99)
 * expect: B  Café: 2 events · Rooftop: 0 events · Reading corner'; DROP TABLE x; --: 0 events
 * expect: C1 Fantasy, cheapest first: The Hobbit, Lord of the Rings
 * expect: C2 Staff picks post: 1 Book Card, comment attributes {"backgroundColor":"sage"}
 *
 * Instructor copy. The shop wants a weekly report for its newsletter, built from the plugin's data: Parts A to C
 * are the scored code (three functions); Part D (three written answers) is on the workbook's Checkpoint page.
 * The main section at the end is the same in the starter, and the script changes nothing on the site.
 * Run: wp eval-file --use-include checkpoint_solution.php
 */

/**
 * Part A (Lessons 5.2, 5.3): books in stock that cost at most $max, cheapest first, as "Title ($8.49)".
 *
 * @param float $max The highest price.
 * @return string[]
 */
function pagesco_cp_cheap_books( float $max ): array {
	$books = get_posts(
		array(
			'post_type'      => 'book',
			'post_status'    => 'publish',
			'posts_per_page' => -1,
			'meta_key'       => 'pagesco_price',
			'orderby'        => 'meta_value_num',
			'order'          => 'ASC',
			'meta_query'     => array(
				array(
					'key'     => 'pagesco_price',
					'value'   => $max,
					'compare' => '<=',
					'type'    => 'DECIMAL(10,2)',
				),
				array(
					'key'   => 'pagesco_in_stock',
					'value' => '1',
				),
			),
		)
	);

	$lines = array();
	foreach ( $books as $book ) {
		$lines[] = sprintf( '%s ($%s)', $book->post_title, number_format_i18n( (float) get_post_meta( $book->ID, 'pagesco_price', true ), 2 ) );
	}

	return $lines;
}

/**
 * Part B (Lesson 5.5): how many events take place at a location: one prepared COUNT query on the events table.
 *
 * @param string $location The exact location, as typed by anyone.
 */
function pagesco_cp_events_at( string $location ): int {
	global $wpdb;

	return (int) $wpdb->get_var(
		$wpdb->prepare( 'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM %i WHERE location = %s', PagesCo\Books\EventsTable::name(), $location )
	);
}

/**
 * Part C (Lessons 6.2, 6.3): C1 renders a Book List block for a genre, cheapest first, and returns its titles;
 * C2 describes the Book Cards stored in a post.
 *
 * @param string $genre A genre slug.
 * @return string[]
 */
function pagesco_cp_list_titles( string $genre ): array {
	$attributes = array(
		'genre'   => $genre,
		'orderBy' => 'price',
	);
	$html       = do_blocks( '<!-- wp:pagesco/book-list ' . wp_json_encode( $attributes ) . ' /-->' );

	preg_match_all( '#<a href="[^"]*">([^<]*)</a>#', $html, $found );

	return $found[1];
}

/**
 * @param string $slug A post slug.
 * @return array{count: int, attrs: array}
 */
function pagesco_cp_cards_in( string $slug ): array {
	$post  = get_page_by_path( $slug, OBJECT, 'post' );
	$cards = array_values( wp_list_filter( parse_blocks( $post ? $post->post_content : '' ), array( 'blockName' => 'pagesco/book-card' ) ) );

	return array(
		'count' => count( $cards ),
		'attrs' => $cards ? $cards[0]['attrs'] : array(),
	);
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- main: the same in the starter
echo 'A  Under $10, in stock: ', esc_html( implode( ', ', pagesco_cp_cheap_books( 10.0 ) ) ), "\n";

$pagesco_places = array( 'Café', 'Rooftop', "Reading corner'; DROP TABLE x; --" );
echo 'B  ', implode( ' · ', array_map( fn( $place ) => $place . ': ' . pagesco_cp_events_at( $place ) . ' events', $pagesco_places ) ), "\n";

echo 'C1 Fantasy, cheapest first: ', esc_html( implode( ', ', pagesco_cp_list_titles( 'fantasy' ) ) ), "\n";

$pagesco_cards = pagesco_cp_cards_in( 'staff-picks' );
printf( "C2 Staff picks post: %d Book Card, comment attributes %s\n", $pagesco_cards['count'], wp_json_encode( $pagesco_cards['attrs'] ) );

It prints (the site after Lesson 6.3):

A  Under $10, in stock: Pride and Prejudice ($7.99), Animal Farm ($8.49), Brave New World ($9.49), 1984 ($9.99)
B  Café: 2 events · Rooftop: 0 events · Reading corner'; DROP TABLE x; --: 0 events
C1 Fantasy, cheapest first: The Hobbit, Lord of the Rings
C2 Staff picks post: 1 Book Card, comment attributes {"backgroundColor":"sage"}

As shipped, the starter prints the four lines with TODO and -1 values and no message. Accept: WP_Query instead of get_posts(); %i or the table name from EventsTable::name() concatenated (it is not user input); any way of reading the titles from C1's HTML that does not query the books again. Do not accept 'type' => 'NUMERIC' for the price: NUMERIC makes MySQL cast to a whole number (CAST('10.49' AS SIGNED) is 10), so with a maximum of 10 Fahrenheit 451 ($10.49) and The Hobbit ($10.99) slip into line A, measured on the course site; that is Partial in row 1. Without any type the comparison is between strings and finds nothing.

Part D: model answers
  1. D1. The review: static, it is content an editor writes and it should stay as written (and survive the plugin). The three cheapest: dynamic, the data (prices, stock, new books) changes elsewhere, so render.php must ask on every view; a static block would go stale like Lesson 3.3's pattern.
  2. D2. A nonce (wp_nonce_field() in the form, check_admin_referer() or wp_verify_nonce() before saving; the Settings API does both). It stops cross-site request forgery: another site making a logged-in administrator's browser submit the form.
  3. D3. The genre choices are real links to ?genre= addresses, and render.php reads and validates ?genre= to set the starting state, with PHP closures for the derived state, so the server renders each filtered page itself (hidden on the other books, aria-current on the link).

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, on a fresh course site at the state each file declares (Lesson 6.4's labs at after:lesson_21, Lesson 7.1's at after:lesson_22), copied into the WordPress root and run with wp eval-file --use-include (PHP labs) or sh in the WP-CLI container (shell labs).

Lab 6.4-A (guided): labs/module-13/l6_4-interactivity-api/open_today_starter.php → open_today_solution.php

As shipped:

<div data-wp-interactive="pagesco/hours"><p>TODO: today's hours</p>
<button aria-expanded="false" data-wp-on--click="actions.toggleWeek" data-wp-bind--aria-expanded="state.showWeek">The whole week</button><ul>
<li data-wp-context='{"day":"Friday"}'>Friday: 9:00-18:00</li>
<li data-wp-context='{"day":"Saturday"}'>Saturday: 10:00-16:00</li>
<li data-wp-context='{"day":"Sunday"}'>Sunday: closed</li>
</ul></div>

The solution prints:

<div data-wp-interactive="pagesco/hours"><p data-wp-text="state.todayText">Today (Saturday): 10:00-16:00</p>
<button aria-expanded="false" data-wp-on--click="actions.toggleWeek" data-wp-bind--aria-expanded="state.showWeek">The whole week</button><ul>
<li aria-current="false" hidden data-wp-context='{"day":"Friday"}' data-wp-bind--hidden="state.isHidden" data-wp-bind--aria-current="state.isToday" data-wp-class--is-today="state.isToday">Friday: 9:00-18:00</li>
<li aria-current="true" class="is-today" data-wp-context='{"day":"Saturday"}' data-wp-bind--hidden="state.isHidden" data-wp-bind--aria-current="state.isToday" data-wp-class--is-today="state.isToday">Saturday: 10:00-16:00</li>
<li aria-current="false" hidden data-wp-context='{"day":"Sunday"}' data-wp-bind--hidden="state.isHidden" data-wp-bind--aria-current="state.isToday" data-wp-class--is-today="state.isToday">Sunday: closed</li>
</ul></div>

Reading it: the server filled the paragraph (data-wp-text), wrote hidden on the two days that are not today (isHidden: the week is folded), aria-current true/false and the class on Saturday. data-wp-on--click is left for the browser: the store's toggleWeek action (state.showWeek = ! state.showWeek) and two getters mirroring the closures would make the button work, the lesson's view.js pattern. Accept closures that call wp_interactivity_state( 'pagesco/hours' ) with the namespace.

As shipped:

Showing  of 5 events
(no <li> yet: TODO 1)

The solution prints:

Showing 2 of 5 events
<li data-wp-each-child="pagesco/events::state.events" hidden data-wp-bind--hidden="state.isElsewhere"><time data-wp-text="context.event.when">Nov 4</time> <span data-wp-text="context.event.title">Book club: Dune</span></li>
