The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 6.4 · The Interactivity API
  1. A It also builds viewScriptModule files as script modules: It adds a module build, so viewScriptModule files such as view.js are built with a view.asset.php of type module; without it they are skipped silently.
  2. C All eleven books, nine of them with the hidden attribute: render.php prints every book, and the server evaluates data-wp-bind--hidden through the isVisible closure, so the nine others arrive with the hidden attribute.
  3. B data-wp-text="state.visibleCount" on the span: data-wp-text sets the element's text content from a value, on the server and in the browser; here the span holds state.visibleCount.
  4. A So the server can evaluate the directives and send correct HTML before any JavaScript runs: The server evaluates directives while rendering; with the closure it can compute isVisible per book, so the HTML is already filtered and nothing changes when the page hydrates.
  5. C The link is followed and the server renders the fantasy books, with a page load: No store is wired to the directives, so the click follows the link and the server renders the filtered page: correct, but a full page load each time, with no message.
Lesson 7.1 · Using the REST API
  1. B GET /wp-json/wp/v2/books?per_page=5&_fields=id,title.rendered: per_page=5 gives five books, newest first by default, and _fields keeps only id and the rendered title.
  2. C 401: the request is treated as anonymous, and the edit is refused: Without an X-WP-Nonce header WordPress ignores the cookie, treats the request as anonymous and refuses the edit with 401. wp.apiFetch adds the nonce.
  3. A With an application password of a user whose role can edit books, over HTTPS: A program gets its own application password, for a user with no more rights than it needs, sent with Basic authentication over HTTPS, and revocable on its own.
  4. C 403: Devon is authenticated, so it is not 401; his role cannot edit another author's book, so the answer is 403 Forbidden.
  5. B 3: Eleven books in pages of four make three pages (4, 4 and 3); the header is the same on every page.

Exit Ticket

  1. State and derived state: the state is the selected genre (with each book's genres); derived are isVisible, isSelected and visibleCount.
  2. 401 means "who are you?" (not authenticated); 403 is "I know you, and no" (authenticated, not allowed).
  3. The partner's script: an application password of a WordPress user whose role can edit books (an editor), sent with Basic authentication over HTTPS; never the account password.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A ("from the address bar"). Has not seen the state JSON: show the <script id="wp-script-module-data-..."> from the demo.
  • Q2 B or C. Thinks the server only prints HTML: run the curl for ?genre=fantasy again.
  • Q4 B ("in the state"). Works for small cases; context is per element. Redraw the three columns.
  • Q7 D (403). Confuses 401 and 403: the request has no user at all.
  • Q9 C (401 as anonymous). Remembers the no-nonce case; a WRONG nonce is refused outright.
  • Q10 A or B. Blames the data or the login: it is _fields cutting the links.
  • Q11 A. Missed the HTTPS rule: application passwords are offered only on HTTPS or a local environment.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 14: Custom Endpoints, External APIs and Cron

Answers and reasoning for every Session 14 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-run items, the lab reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 14 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up: Lessons 6.4 and 7.1

  1. W1. 9 of eleven: the server evaluated data-wp-bind--hidden for each book (two fantasy books stay visible).
  2. W2. It calls event.preventDefault(); since 6.8 actions may run after the event, too late to stop the link.
  3. W3. The role: 403 means "I know you, and no" (Devon on Maria's book). 401 would be the credentials.
  4. W4. A HEAD request (curl -I) and the header: X-WP-TotalPages: 3 on the projector site.
  5. W5. apiFetch sends Maria's cookie AND the page's wp_rest nonce; a script has neither, so it needs an application password.

On the projector site (after Lesson 7.1):

curl -s 'http://localhost:8080/find-a-book/?genre=fantasy' | grep -c '<li hidden'
9
curl -s -I 'http://localhost:8080/wp-json/wp/v2/books?per_page=5' | grep -i '^X-WP-TotalPages'
X-WP-TotalPages: 3

7.2 · Custom REST Endpoints: self-check

Q1
A route registers 'methods' => WP_REST_Server::CREATABLE. Which HTTP method does that endpoint answer?
A) GET
B) POST
C) POST, PUT and PATCH
D) DELETE
Why: READABLE is GET, CREATABLE POST, EDITABLE 'POST, PUT, PATCH', DELETABLE DELETE. The constants say what you mean; the events route uses READABLE and CREATABLE on one route.
A is wrong: That is READABLE.
C is wrong: That is EDITABLE.
D is wrong: That is DELETABLE.
Q2
On the course site after Lesson 7.2 (Books per list = 6, eleven books): what does this print?
wp eval '$r = rest_do_request( new WP_REST_Request( "GET", "/pagesco/v1/books" ) ); echo $r->get_status(), " ", count( $r->get_data() ), " ", $r->get_headers()["X-WP-TotalPages"], "\n";'
200 6 2
Why: rest_do_request() runs the route without HTTP: the same validation, permission check and callback. per_page defaults to the Books per list setting (6), so six books and two pages.
Q3
An anonymous client POSTs to /pagesco/v1/events WITHOUT a title. Which answer does it get first?
A) 400 rest_missing_callback_param: arguments are validated before the permission check
B) 401 rest_forbidden: the permission check comes first
C) 500: the callback fails on a missing title
D) 201: the event is created without a title
Why: The order is validate, sanitize, permission, callback. A missing required argument stops the request at the first desk, before anyone asks who the client is (the predict item P2 shows it with rest_do_request()).
B is wrong: Only once the arguments are valid.
C is wrong: The callback never runs.
D is wrong: required stops it.
Q4
Maria sends a bad starts_at AND a negative capacity. The answer names only starts_at. Why?
A) capacity is not validated
B) The schema stops at the first error it finds in alphabetical order
C) A negative capacity is turned into 0
D) validate_callbacks run first; the schema checks of the other arguments run in the sanitizing step, which a failed validation never reaches
Why: Lesson 7.2, Step 3: starts_at has a validate_callback; capacity's minimum is checked by the schema during sanitizing. Clients should expect errors one round at a time.
A is wrong: Fix the date and the capacity error comes next.
B is wrong: There is no alphabetical rule.
C is wrong: The schema refuses it; nothing is coerced.
Q5
Which permission_callback fits a route that DELETES events?
A) '__return_true': the callback checks the ID
B) 'is_user_logged_in'
C) A check of current_user_can( 'edit_others_posts' ) that returns a WP_Error with rest_authorization_required_code()
D) None: DELETE routes are protected by WordPress
Why: Every write needs a capability check as specific as the job; rest_authorization_required_code() gives 401 to nobody and 403 to a logged-in user who may not. The lesson's Exercise 3 reuses can_manage_events().
A is wrong: Anyone on the internet could delete events.
B is wrong: Any subscriber is logged in.
D is wrong: WordPress protects nothing you do not check (and warns when the key is missing).
Q6
GET /pagesco/v1/books/14 asks for the Events page (a page, not a book). Why does the answer say only "No book was found with that ID." and not "that is a page"?
An endpoint must not tell visitors what exists behind an ID they may not see: drafts, private books and other post types all get the same 404
Why: Leaking existence is information disclosure. get_book() requires a published book and answers one 404 for everything else.

7.2 · Custom REST Endpoints: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
curl -s 'http://localhost:8080/wp-json/pagesco/v1/books?per_page=0'; echo
{"code":"rest_invalid_param","message":"Invalid parameter(s): per_page","data":{"status":400,"params":{"per_page":"per_page must be between 1 (inclusive) and 100 (inclusive)"},"details":{"per_page":{"code":"rest_out_of_bounds","message":"per_page must be between 1 (inclusive) and 100 (inclusive)","data":null}}}}
Why: per_page has a minimum of 1: the schema refuses 0 with 400 before the callback runs, with the same message core's routes use.
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval '$r = rest_do_request( new WP_REST_Request( "POST", "/pagesco/v1/events" ) ); echo $r->get_status(), " ", $r->get_data()["code"], "\n";'
400 rest_missing_callback_param
Why: Validation comes before the permission check: the missing required title stops the request at the first desk (400 rest_missing_callback_param), although nobody is logged in.

7.3 · External APIs, Transients & WP-Cron: self-check

Q7
The supplier's host does not resolve, and code calls wp_remote_retrieve_body( $response ) on the WP_Error. What does it get?
A) A fatal error
B) null and a Warning
C) The error message
D) An empty string
Why: The wp_remote_retrieve_*() helpers accept a WP_Error and return '' (or 0) instead of failing: safe to call, but '' decodes to null, so check is_wp_error() first anyway.
A is wrong: That is why the helpers exist.
B is wrong: No message at all.
C is wrong: That is $response->get_error_message().
Q8
What does this print, for a transient nobody ever set?
wp eval 'var_dump( get_transient( "pagesco_books_nothing_here" ) );'
bool(false)
Why: get_transient() returns false for "missing or expired". That is why a cache must never STORE false (it would look missing), and why the project stores an array even for a failure.
Q9
A plugin fetches a book cover from a URL an editor types into a field. Which function?
A) wp_safe_remote_get(): it refuses private networks and odd ports
B) wp_remote_get() with a short timeout
C) file_get_contents()
D) wp_remote_head()
Why: A URL from a user could point at http://192.168.1.1/ or a cloud metadata service: server-side request forgery. The safe variants refuse those before connecting; the site's own host stays allowed.
B is wrong: A timeout does not stop the request reaching an internal address.
C is wrong: It bypasses the HTTP API's protections and filters.
D is wrong: It is just a HEAD request.
Q10
You want to run the daily sync now, while developing, without moving tomorrow's 03:00 run. What do you type?
A) wp cron event run pagesco_books_daily_sync
B) wp eval 'do_action( "pagesco_books_daily_sync" );'
C) wp cron test
D) curl -s http://localhost:8080/wp-cron.php
Why: WP-CLI's event run reschedules a recurring event from now (a 03:00 job run at 10:15 moves to 10:15); firing the hook yourself runs the work and leaves the schedule alone (Lesson 7.3's warning box).
A is wrong: It moves the schedule.
C is wrong: It only checks the loopback.
D is wrong: It runs what is DUE; tomorrow's 03:00 is not.
Q11
Where does the supplier's API key belong?
A) In the plugin's Client class, as a constant
B) In a transient, refreshed daily
C) In wp-config.php (or an environment variable it reads), on each site
D) In the REST API response, for the JavaScript
Why: A key in code ends up in Git, backups and every copy of the plugin, and the live site needs a different one. wp config set writes the constant; the live site gets its own.
A is wrong: Code is shared; secrets are not.
B is wrong: Transients can vanish at any time.
D is wrong: Never send secrets to browsers.
Q12
On the course site after Lesson 7.3: what does this print?
wp cron event list --hook=pagesco_books_daily_sync --fields=hook,recurrence
| pagesco_books_daily_sync | 1 day |
Why: Exactly one daily event: Activator schedules it once, guarded by wp_next_scheduled(), and deactivation clears it. Two rows here would be Common Mistake 2 (scheduling on every request).

7.3 · External APIs, Transients & WP-Cron: predict, then run

P3
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'var_dump( is_wp_error( wp_remote_get( "http://localhost:8080/supplier-sandbox/v1/availability" ) ) );'
bool(false)
Why: The sandbox answers 401 with JSON, which is a perfectly good HTTP response: not a WP_Error. Only the status code tells you it failed.
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo wp_safe_remote_get( "http://10.0.0.1/" )->get_error_message(), "\n";'
A valid URL was not provided.
Why: wp_safe_remote_get() refuses private networks before connecting: "A valid URL was not provided." (the site's own host stays allowed).

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, on a fresh course site at the state each file declares (Lesson 7.2's labs at after:lesson_23, Lesson 7.3's at after:lesson_24), copied into the WordPress root and run with wp eval-file --use-include (PHP labs) or sh in the WP-CLI container (shell labs); folder labs installed and activated first.

Lab 7.2-A (guided): labs/module-14/l7_2-custom-endpoints/opening_hours_starter/ → opening_hours_solution/ with test_opening_hours.sh

With the starter's plugin:

Plugin 'pagesco-opening-hours' activated.
Success: Activated 1 of 1 plugins.
== Saturday
{"todo":"TODO: the hours"}
== Sunday
{"todo":"TODO: the hours"}
== A day that does not exist
{"todo":"TODO: the hours"}
== The whole week: how many days, and Thursday
0
(no Thursday)
== What OPTIONS says about the argument
(no day argument)

With the solution's:

Plugin 'pagesco-opening-hours' activated.
Success: Activated 1 of 1 plugins.
== Saturday
{"day":"saturday","closed":false,"opens":"10:00","closes":"16:00"}
== Sunday
{"day":"sunday","closed":true}
== A day that does not exist
{"code":"rest_invalid_param","message":"Invalid parameter(s): day","data":{"status":400,"params":{"day":"day is not one of monday, tuesday, wednesday, thursday, friday, saturday, and sunday."},"details":{"day":{"code":"rest_not_in_enum","message":"day is not one of monday, tuesday, wednesday, thursday, friday, saturday, and sunday.","data":null}}}}
== The whole week: how many days, and Thursday
7
{"day":"thursday","closed":false,"opens":"9:00","closes":"21:00"}
== What OPTIONS says about the argument
"day":{"description":"One day of the week, in lowercase English. Without it: the whole week."

Reading it: the 400 for funday comes from the enum, not from the learner's code; without a default the argument is null when not sent, which means the whole week; Sunday is closed: true with no times (a client tests the flag); OPTIONS documents the argument. __return_true is right: opening hours are public. Remove the plugin afterwards.

Lab 7.2-B: labs/module-14/l7_2-custom-endpoints/schema_rules_starter.php → schema_rules_solution.php

As shipped:

price = "14.99"                   TODO
price = "free"                    TODO
in_stock = "true"                 TODO
in_stock = "maybe"                TODO
per_page = "500"                  TODO
orderby = "pages"                 TODO
genres = "fantasy,classic"        TODO
title = ""                        TODO
starts_at = "2027-12-16T19:00:00" TODO
starts_at = "16/12/2027 7pm"      TODO

The solution prints:

price = "14.99"                   yes: 14.99
price = "free"                    no: price is not of type number.
in_stock = "true"                 yes: true
in_stock = "maybe"                no: in_stock is not of type boolean.
per_page = "500"                  no: per_page must be between 1 (inclusive) and 100 (inclusive)
orderby = "pages"                 no: orderby is not one of title, newest, and price.
genres = "fantasy,classic"        yes: ["fantasy","classic"]
title = ""                        no: title must be at least 1 character long.
starts_at = "2027-12-16T19:00:00" yes: "2027-12-16T19:00:00"
starts_at = "16/12/2027 7pm"      no: Invalid date.

Reading it: query strings carry strings, and the schema turns "14.99" into a float and "true" into a boolean; "fantasy,classic" becomes a list; the messages are the ones clients get in data.params. A date-time format must be RFC 3339 (2027-12-16T19:00:00): the shop's own "2027-12-16 19:00" needs Lesson 7.2's validate_starts_at().

Handout: labs/module-14/l7_2-custom-endpoints/debug_staff_picks_route.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped, WordPress prints:

Notice: Function register_rest_route was called <strong>incorrectly</strong>. The REST API route definition for <code>pagesco/v1/picks</code> is missing the required <code>permission_callback</code> argument. For REST API routes that are intended to be public, use <code>__return_true</code> as the permission callback. Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information. (This message was added in version 5.5.0.) in /var/www/html/wp-includes/functions.php on line 6260
?limit=2   200 ["1984","Animal Farm"]
Warning: Undefined array key "limit" in /var/www/html/debug_staff_picks_route.php on line 43
(none)     200 ["1984","Animal Farm","Brave New World","Dune","Fahrenheit 451"]
?limit=50  200 ["1984","Animal Farm","Brave New World","Dune","Fahrenheit 451","Lord of the Rings","Pride and Prejudice","The Catcher in the Rye","The Great Gatsby","The Hobbit","To Kill a Mockingbird"]
  1. doing_it_wrong (route at line 20): no permission_callback. Outside a REST request the Notice prints; inside one it would only be an X-WP-DoingItWrong header. Add 'permission_callback' => '__return_true' (a public read).
  2. Warning, line 43: get_query_params() holds only what the client sent, so limit is missing without ?limit=; the default lives in the request's parameters: $request['limit'].
  3. Silent, line 30: 'sanitize_callback' => 'absint' switches the schema off for limit, so 50 passes and all eleven books come back (Lesson 7.2, Mistake 2). Remove it (the type sanitizes), or add 'validate_callback' => 'rest_validate_request_arg'.

Fixed (all three), it prints:

?limit=2   200 ["1984","Animal Farm"]
(none)     200 ["1984","Animal Farm","Brave New World"]
?limit=50  400 "rest_invalid_param"

The fixed script is PICKS_FIXED in blocks_S14.py (it prints an error's code instead of the whole error).

Lab 7.3-A (guided): labs/module-14/l7_3-external-apis-cron/remote_answers_starter.php → remote_answers_solution.php

As shipped:

genre=fantasy     pagesco_todo: TODO: ask and check the answer
genre=romance     pagesco_todo: TODO: ask and check the answer
books/99          pagesco_todo: TODO: ask and check the answer
a web page        pagesco_todo: TODO: ask and check the answer
shop.invalid      pagesco_todo: TODO: ask and check the answer

The solution prints:

genre=fantasy     2 books: The Hobbit, Lord of the Rings
genre=romance     pagesco_remote_http_error (400): Invalid parameter(s): genre
books/99          pagesco_remote_http_error (404): No book was found with that ID.
a web page        pagesco_remote_bad_response: The answer is not JSON.
shop.invalid      http_request_failed: cURL error 6: Could not resolve host: shop.invalid (Domain name not found)

Reading it: the 400 and the 404 are normal responses with JSON bodies, turned into WP_Errors that keep the status; the About page is a 200 that is not JSON (the third check); only shop.invalid gives no answer at all, a WP_Error from the transport. Accept a status check of 200 !== $code for this API.

Lab 7.3-B: labs/module-14/l7_3-external-apis-cron/events_cache_starter.php → events_cache_solution.php

As shipped:

View 1: 5 events, error "TODO: no cache yet", requests so far: 1
View 2: 5 events, error "TODO: no cache yet", requests so far: 2
View 3: 5 events, error "TODO: no cache yet", requests so far: 3
After the failure, view 1: 0 events, error "TODO: no cache yet", requests so far: 4
After the failure, view 2: 0 events, error "TODO: no cache yet", requests so far: 5
rows left: 0

The solution prints:

View 1: 5 events, error "", requests so far: 1
View 2: 5 events, error "", requests so far: 1
View 3: 5 events, error "", requests so far: 1
After the failure, view 1: 0 events, error "cURL error 7: Failed to connect", requests so far: 2
After the failure, view 2: 0 events, error "cURL error 7: Failed to connect", requests so far: 2
rows left: 0

Reading it: one request for three views; after the simulated failure (pre_http_request answers instead of the network) the failure itself is cached for a minute, so the second view does not ask again; the value is always an array, never false; the cleanup leaves no rows. global $pagesco_requests is deliberate: WP-CLI runs the file inside a function.

Lab 7.3-C: labs/module-14/l7_3-external-apis-cron/cron_plan_starter.sh → cron_plan_solution.sh

As shipped:

Events of pagesco_books_lab_job after three schedule calls: 0
+------+------------+
| hook | recurrence |
+------+------------+
+------+------------+
First run (site time): TODO
Removed: 0 + 0
Left: 0

The solution prints:

Events of pagesco_books_lab_job after three schedule calls: 1
+-----------------------+------------+
| hook                  | recurrence |
+-----------------------+------------+
| pagesco_books_lab_job | 12 hours   |
+-----------------------+------------+
First run (site time): 04:30
+----------------------------+---------------+------+
| hook                       | recurrence    | args |
+----------------------------+---------------+------+
| pagesco_books_lab_reminder | Non-repeating | [7]  |
+----------------------------+---------------+------+
Removed: 1 + 1
Left: 0

Reading it: three schedule calls, one event, because each checks wp_next_scheduled() first (and the start time is fixed, so even an exact duplicate would be refused); the first run is 04:30 in the site's time zone; the reminder carries its argument, and clearing it needs the same arguments.

Handout: labs/module-14/l7_3-external-apis-cron/debug_event_counts.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped, PHP prints:

Warning: Undefined array key "items" in /var/www/html/debug_event_counts.php on line 35

Warning: Undefined array key "items" in /var/www/html/debug_event_counts.php on line 35

Warning: Undefined array key "items" in /var/www/html/debug_event_counts.php on line 35
Warning: foreach() argument must be of type array|object, null given in /var/www/html/debug_event_counts.php on line 35
Warning: Undefined array key "items" in /var/www/html/debug_event_counts.php on line 35

  1. Warnings, line 35: the events route answers a LIST, not { items: ... }: $data['items'] is undefined and foreach gets null. Count the list: is_array( $data ) ? count( $data ) : 0.

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 7.2 · Custom REST Endpoints
  1. A rest_api_init: rest_api_init fires when WordPress builds the REST server, only on requests that need it; registering anywhere else triggers a called incorrectly notice.
  2. C 8 books, and X-WP-Total: 11, X-WP-TotalPages: 2: per_page defaults to Settings::get( 'books_per_page' ), now 8, so page 1 has 8 books and the headers say 11 in total on 2 pages.
  3. B The callback receives 500: the maximum is not checked: With a sanitize_callback of its own, the argument is no longer run through rest_parse_request_arg, so the maximum is never checked and the callback receives 500.
  4. A A function that returns current_user_can( 'edit_others_posts' ), or a WP_Error with rest_authorization_required_code(): A write needs a capability check; edit_others_posts lets editors and administrators add shop-wide events, and the function answers 401 or 403 otherwise.
  5. C 500: Without a status of 404 in the error data, WordPress sends 500 Internal Server Error with data null.
Lesson 7.3 · External APIs, Transients & WP-Cron
  1. C A normal response array; wp_remote_retrieve_response_code() returns 503: An error status is still a response: an array whose code wp_remote_retrieve_response_code() reports as 503, with the HTML as the body. Only a missing answer is a WP_Error.
  2. A In the object cache only, where it may be evicted before the hour is over: With an external object cache, transients are stored in the cache only, and may be evicted before they expire; without one they are two rows in wp_options.
  3. B In the activation callback, after checking wp_next_scheduled(), with wp_clear_scheduled_hook() on deactivation: Schedule once on activation, guarded by wp_next_scheduled(), and clear it with wp_clear_scheduled_hook() on deactivation; the callback itself is added on every request.
  4. B With the first request after 3:00, around 8 a.m.: WP-Cron checks for due events on requests; the first request after 3:00, here the first visitor around 8 a.m., starts it. A system cron calling wp cron event run --due-now removes that dependency.
  5. C So that a broken supplier is not asked, and waited for, on every screen view: Without a cached failure, every Books screen view would call the broken supplier again and wait for its timeout; five minutes protects both sites and still retries soon.

Exit Ticket

  1. The four desks: validate (schema and validate_callbacks), sanitize (schema or sanitize_callbacks, defaults), permission (permission_callback), callback.
  2. Three failures: no answer (a WP_Error: DNS, connection, timeout); an error status (401, 404, 503: a normal response); a body that is not the expected data (HTML, no items).
  3. The daily job: in the activation callback, if ( ! wp_next_scheduled( $hook ) ); wp_clear_scheduled_hook() on deactivation; the callback added on every request.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 C. Mixes CREATABLE and EDITABLE: show WP_REST_Server's constants.
  • Q3 B (401 first). Thinks permission comes first: redraw the four desks and run P2.
  • Q4 B. Guessing at the schema's behavior: the validate_callbacks run first, then sanitizing.
  • Q5 B (is_user_logged_in). Any subscriber is logged in: a capability names the job.
  • Q7 B or C. Has not met the helpers' tolerance of WP_Error; still check is_wp_error() first.
  • Q10 A. Uses WP-CLI's event run: it moves the schedule.
  • Q11 A. Puts secrets in code: they end up in Git and in every copy.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 15: Debugging, Testing and Performance

Answers and reasoning for every Session 15 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-run items, the lab reference outputs, the lessons' recorded cards, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 15 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up

  1. W1 (Lesson 7.3). No answer at all (a WP_Error: DNS, refused, timeout); an answer that is not a success (an array with a 401 or 503 status); a success whose body is not the JSON you expect. Only the first is a WP_Error.
  2. W2 (Lesson 7.3). Availability::get() reads through: it calls the supplier, stores the answer again (12 hours, 5 minutes after an error) and returns it. A transient may vanish at any time, so code never relies on it being there.
  3. W3 (Lesson 7.3). wp cron event run on a recurring event reschedules it to now plus the interval: the 03:00 job would move to whenever you ran it.
  4. W4 (Lesson 7.2). A 500. The route's callback should give its WP_Error array( 'status' => 404 ) (or the right code); permission callbacks use rest_authorization_required_code().
  5. W5 (Lesson 7.3). In wp-config.php (PAGESCO_BOOKS_SUPPLIER_URL and _KEY, set with wp config set): configuration per environment, never in the plugin's code. Lesson 8.4 moves them to the server's environment.

8.1 · Debugging & Testing: self-check

Q1
The live Pages & Co. shop should keep a record of PHP warnings for the developer, and show visitors none. Where should WP_DEBUG_LOG point?
A) true, so the messages go to wp-content/debug.log
B) A file outside the web root, such as /home/pagesco/logs/wordpress.log
C) false: a live site must never record anything
D) The theme's folder, where only the theme developer looks
Why: With display off and the log in a folder the web server does not serve, the messages are kept and nobody can download them. Lesson 8.1's Mistake 1: on the course site curl gets wp-content/debug.log with status 200.
A is wrong: That is the default, inside the web root: bots try that address on every site.
C is wrong: Then a live problem leaves no evidence at all; logging is fine, the location is the point.
D is wrong: Also inside the web root, and a theme update would delete it.
Q2
On the course site after Lesson 7.3, with WordPress loaded (wp eval-file): what does this print?
<?php
add_filter(
	'pre_http_request',
	fn() => array(
		'headers'  => array(),
		'body'     => '<h1>Service Unavailable</h1>',
		'response' => array( 'code' => 503, 'message' => 'Service Unavailable' ),
		'cookies'  => array(),
		'filename' => null,
	)
);
$result = ( new PagesCo\Books\Supplier\Client() )->availability( array( '9780441172719' ) );
echo $result->get_error_code(), ' ', $result->get_error_data()['status'], "\n";
pagesco_supplier_http_error 503
Why: pre_http_request answers in place of the network, so the client never calls the sandbox. A 503 is an answer, not a transport failure: Client::decode() turns any status outside 200 to 299 into pagesco_supplier_http_error with the status in the error's data. That exact code and that exact number are what SupplierClientTest asserts, and why a test that only asked "is it an error?" would miss the bug of Lesson 8.1's red run.
Q3
A plugin crashes on init and every WP-CLI command now dies with the same fatal error. Which command still deactivates it?
A) wp plugin deactivate pagesco-crash --force
B) wp eval 'deactivate_plugins( "pagesco-crash/pagesco-crash.php" );'
C) wp plugin delete pagesco-crash
D) wp --skip-plugins=pagesco-crash plugin deactivate pagesco-crash
Why: WP-CLI loads WordPress, and with it every active plugin, before it runs any command. --skip-plugins loads WordPress without the named plugin, so the deactivation can run. Recovery mode is the browser's way out; for a must-use plugin only renaming the file helps.
A is wrong: There is no --force that skips loading the plugin: the fatal error happens first.
B is wrong: wp eval loads WordPress too, so it dies before your code runs.
C is wrong: Also loads WordPress first; and deleting is not the first move when deactivating will do.
Q4
Why does the lesson name its temporary backtrace logger 0-pagesco-debug-backtrace.php?
A) Must-use plugins load in alphabetical order: it must be there before the plugin that asks too early
B) WordPress ignores must-use plugins whose names start with a letter
C) The 0- tells WordPress to load it only when WP_DEBUG is on
D) So that PHPCS skips the file
Why: A hook added after the notice fired would miss it. wp_get_mu_plugins() sorts the files by name, so 0- loads before pagesco-too-early.php, adds its doing_it_wrong_run callback, and catches the notice with the call stack.
B is wrong: Every PHP file directly in mu-plugins/ loads.
C is wrong: WordPress gives file names no meaning beyond their order.
D is wrong: PHPCS only checks what its ruleset names; mu-plugins are not in the plugin's ruleset at all.
Q5
PHPCS reports 30 times PrefixAllGlobals.NonPrefixedVariableFound in the blocks' render.php files. What did Lesson 8.1 do, and why?
A) Renamed every variable to start with pagesco_books_
B) Turned the rule off for the whole plugin
C) Excluded that rule for those two file patterns in phpcs.xml.dist, with a comment: WordPress includes them inside a function, so the variables are local
D) Added // phpcs:ignore to each of the 30 lines
Why: A ruleset exception that is narrow (one rule, the files it does not fit) and explained (the comment) is a decision a reviewer can read. Thirty ignore comments would hide the same decision thirty times; a plugin-wide switch would hide real globals elsewhere.
A is wrong: Possible, but noisy, and it fixes nothing: the variables are not globals.
B is wrong: Too wide: a real global anywhere else would go unreported.
D is wrong: phpcs:ignore is for a single line that is right despite the rule; thirty of them is a ruleset decision.
Q6
In the plugin's folder, with the wp-env test environment started: which command runs the plugin's PHPUnit tests, and why not just phpunit?
npx @wordpress/env run cli --env-cwd=wp-content/plugins/pagesco-books vendor/bin/phpunit: the plugin's own PHPUnit 9.6; the container's global phpunit is 10.5, which the WordPress 7.1 test library does not support
Why: Two versions of PHPUnit in one process fail with "Call to undefined method PHPUnit\Framework\TestSuite::empty()" (Lesson 8.1, Common Mistake 2). --env-cwd is the folder to run in, relative to the WordPress root inside the container. composer test runs the same vendor/bin/phpunit.

8.1 · Debugging & Testing: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'var_dump( WP_DEBUG_DISPLAY, ini_get( "display_errors" ) );'
bool(true)
string(6) "stderr"
Why: WP_DEBUG_DISPLAY is true, and WP-CLI sets PHP's display_errors to stderr: messages go to the terminal's error stream, which is why your terminal shows warnings that a pipe into a file would not catch. On a web request the same setting prints them into the page.
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'add_filter( "pre_http_request", fn() => new WP_Error( "http_request_failed", "down" ) ); $r = wp_remote_get( "https://api.riverside.example/v1/availability" ); echo get_class( $r ), ": ", $r->get_error_message(), "\n";'
WP_Error: down
Why: pre_http_request answered with a WP_Error before any connection was tried (the fictional address would not resolve anyway). wp_remote_get() returns it unchanged: a transport failure is a WP_Error, an HTTP error status is not (Lesson 7.3).

8.2 · Performance & Caching: self-check

Q7
On the course site: what does this print?
wp eval 'global $wpdb; $b = $wpdb->num_queries; get_option( "pagesco_lab_nope" ); get_option( "pagesco_lab_nope" ); echo $wpdb->num_queries - $b, "\n";'
1
Why: The first get_option() of an option with no row asks the database and finds nothing; WordPress then remembers the name in the notoptions cache for the rest of the request, so the second costs nothing. One query per request is what pagesco_books_settings cost every page until Lesson 8.2 gave it an autoloaded row.
Q8
On a site without a persistent object cache, these two commands run one after the other. What does the second print?
wp eval 'wp_cache_set( "stock", 24, "pagesco_demo" );'
wp eval 'var_dump( wp_cache_get( "stock", "pagesco_demo" ) );'
A) int(24)
B) bool(false)
C) NULL
D) A Warning: the group pagesco_demo does not exist
Why: Each WP-CLI command is its own PHP process, a request of its own, and WordPress's own object cache lives in that process's memory: the second starts empty and wp_cache_get() returns false for a miss. With an object-cache.php drop-in (Redis, Memcached, the lesson's SQLite Object Cache) it would print int(24).
A is wrong: That needs a persistent object cache; the course site has none (wp_using_ext_object_cache() is NULL).
C is wrong: A cache miss is false, which is why the lesson's code checks is_array() or is_string() for a hit.
D is wrong: Groups need no registration; an unknown key is simply a miss.
Q9
A shop with 500 books runs get_posts( array( 'post_type' => 'book', 'fields' => 'ids', 'posts_per_page' => -1 ) ), then get_post_meta() for each ID, with an empty cache. About how many queries is that, and what is the fix?
A) 2 queries: WordPress loads the meta with the IDs
B) 500 queries; the fix is a persistent object cache
C) 501 queries; the fix is update_postmeta_cache( $ids ) before the loop: 2 queries
D) 1 query; meta is autoloaded like options
Why: With 'fields' => 'ids' get_posts() skips priming the meta cache, so each get_post_meta() asks for its own post: N+1. update_postmeta_cache() loads the meta of all IDs in one query (Lesson 8.2 measured 12 to 2 for eleven books; the lab query_count shows 11 and 1).
A is wrong: That is what a normal WP_Query does; fields => ids is the shortcut that skips it.
B is wrong: Off by one (the ID query), and a warm persistent cache only hides the problem until it is flushed.
D is wrong: Post meta is never autoloaded; only options have autoload.
Q10
EventsTable::find() treats a cached value as a hit with if ( is_array( $events ) ). A colleague suggests the shorter if ( $events ). What would break?
A) Nothing: both conditions treat every cached value the same way
B) A cached empty result, array(), would count as a miss: a location without events would query the database on every call
C) A cached WP_Error would reach the shortcode
D) Two different limits would share one cache entry
Why: An empty array is falsy in PHP, so a truthiness check turns a valid, cached answer (no events here) into a miss, every time. is_array() states what a hit is; false !== $events would work too, since a miss is false (run: wp eval 'var_dump( false !== array(), (bool) array() );'). Two limits sharing an entry is a different bug, prevented by the key: the debug handout debug_events_cache.php has exactly that one.
A is wrong: array() is falsy: if ( array() ) does not run its block.
C is wrong: find() never stores a WP_Error.
D is wrong: That is prevented by the key, md5( $limit . '|' . $location ), not by the check.
Q11
In Lesson 8.2's lab measurement the front page had a CLS of 0.101, just over the 0.1 limit. What caused it, and what fixed it?
A) The web fonts; font-display: swap fixed it
B) The speculation rules prefetching the next page
C) The Navigation block's external stylesheet; inlining it fixed it
D) The hero image had no reserved space; the Image block's aspectRatio attribute fixed it
Why: The browser laid the page out without knowing the image's height, and moved everything below it when it arrived. Block templates get no automatic width and height (wp_filter_content_tags skips the template context), so the theme adds the aspect ratio itself: CLS fell to 0.001.
A is wrong: The font swap caused only the 0.001 that was left.
B is wrong: Prefetching happens on a click and moves nothing on the current page.
C is wrong: A render-blocking stylesheet delays the first paint; it does not shift content afterwards.
Q12
Which WordPress filter adds a <link rel="preload"> for the front page's hero image to the head, and which attribute tells the browser to fetch it first?
wp_preload_resources, with 'fetchpriority' => 'high' (and 'as' => 'image')
Why: The browser finds the preload in the head before it reads the body. In the lesson's lab measurement LCP went from about 1.48 s to about 1.40 s: a modest gain, honestly reported, because the image was in the HTML anyway.

8.2 · Performance & Caching: predict, then run

P3
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'global $wpdb; $b = $wpdb->num_queries; get_post( 30 ); get_post( 30 ); echo $wpdb->num_queries - $b, "\n";'
1
Why: The first get_post() loads the row and caches it in the posts group; the second comes from the object cache. Within one request WordPress never asks twice for the same post.
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'var_dump( wp_using_ext_object_cache() );'
NULL
Why: NULL: no object-cache.php drop-in, so WordPress uses its own in-memory cache (wp_using_ext_object_cache() is only set to true by a drop-in). In Lesson 8.2 it printed bool(true) while the SQLite Object Cache was installed.

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, on a fresh course site at the state each file declares (WordPress 7.1.3, PHP 8.5, MySQL 9.7, WP-CLI 2.12), copied into the WordPress root and run with wp eval-file --use-include (PHP labs) or sh in the WP-CLI container (shell labs). Paths show /var/www/html/..., which is the learner's wordpress/ folder.

Lab 8.1-A (guided): labs/module-15/l8_1-debugging-testing/fake_supplier_starter.php → fake_supplier_solution.php

As shipped:

PASS a good answer is keyed by ISBN
TODO check 2: a 401 becomes pagesco_supplier_http_error with the status in its data
TODO check 3: a 200 that is not JSON becomes pagesco_supplier_bad_response
TODO check 4: a network failure comes back unchanged
1 of 4 checks passed, 1 requests answered by the fake, none by the network

The solution prints:

PASS a good answer is keyed by ISBN
PASS a 401 becomes pagesco_supplier_http_error with the status in its data
PASS a 200 that is not JSON becomes pagesco_supplier_bad_response
PASS a network failure comes back unchanged
4 of 4 checks passed, 4 requests answered by the fake, none by the network

What each check pins down: (2) a status outside 200 to 299 is pagesco_supplier_http_error, with the status in the data, even with a JSON body; (3) a 200 alone is not trusted: a body that is not JSON is pagesco_supplier_bad_response; (4) a transport failure comes back unchanged, so the Supplier column can tell "not reachable" from "answered 503". Common sticking points: '401' compared with === (the data holds the integer 401); a fake left in place from the previous check (pagesco_lab_fake() removes the old one first: ask why that matters); global in the script, which fails because --use-include runs the file inside a function.

Lab 8.1-B: labs/module-15/l8_1-debugging-testing/next_event_starter/ → next_event_solution/ with test_next_event.sh

The test with the starter's pagesco-books-next-event.php in mu-plugins/ (state after Lesson 8.1's Step 2):

PHPCBF CAN FIX THE 1 MARKED SNIFF VIOLATIONS AUTOMATICALLY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The footer line, as a visitor gets it:
<p class="pagesco-next-event">Next event: Book club: Dune</p>

With the solution's:

PHPCS with the plugin's ruleset:
PHPCS: no findings
The footer line, as a visitor gets it:
<p class="pagesco-next-event">Next event: Book club: Dune</p>

Reading the report: the plugin header is a DocBlock, but PHPCS takes it for the function's comment (line 10) and finds no file comment (line 1): the fix is a file DocBlock with @package and a separate one for the function. Line 17 holds four findings: the output is not escaped (twice: __() and $event), the translators comment is missing, and the text domain is the theme's. The solution escapes the whole sentence once with esc_html() around the sprintf(), puts /* translators: ... */ right before the string, and uses pagesco-books. The visitor sees exactly the same line: standards are about the code, not the page. Remove the file afterwards.

Handout: labs/module-15/l8_1-debugging-testing/debug_supplier_log.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped, PHP prints:

Warning: Attempt to read property "ID" on null in /var/www/html/debug_supplier_log.php on line 34
Warning: Undefined array key "X-API-Key" in /var/www/html/debug_supplier_log.php on line 24
dune         24 copies at the supplier
1984         31 copies at the supplier
hobbit       0 copies at the supplier
  1. Warning, line 34: the slug hobbit does not exist (it is the-hobbit), so get_page_by_path() returns null and ->ID reads a property of null. Its quiet consequence is the last line: an empty ISBN, so "0 copies" for a book the supplier has. Evidence beats the output: the Warning says which line, the output only looks odd.
  2. Warning, line 24: the header is called X-Api-Key (Client::headers()), not X-API-Key: PHP array keys are case-sensitive, even when HTTP headers are not.
  3. Silent: with the key's name fixed, the logger writes the whole API key into debug.log (below). No message, and the most serious bug of the three: logs are read by more people, and kept longer, than the code. Log the first eight characters, as Lesson 8.1's logger does.

The two loud bugs fixed, the new line of debug.log (the silent bug, visible):

[26-Oct-2026 13:23:25 UTC] supplier GET http://localhost:8080/supplier-sandbox/v1/availability, key sandbox-7f3a9c

All three fixed: the log line and the output:

[26-Oct-2026 13:23:25 UTC] supplier GET http://localhost:8080/supplier-sandbox/v1/availability, key sandbox-…
dune         24 copies at the supplier
1984         31 copies at the supplier
the-hobbit   9 copies at the supplier
Lab 8.2-A (guided): labs/module-15/l8_2-performance-caching/query_count_starter.php → query_count_solution.php

As shipped:

call                                                                     first     again
get_option( 'blogname' )                                                     0         0
TODO row 2: an option that has no row
TODO row 3: two meta values of one book
TODO row 4: the same WP_Query twice
TODO row 5: one meta read per book
TODO row 6: one meta query for all books

The solution prints:

call                                                                     first     again
get_option( 'blogname' )                                                     0         0
get_option( 'pagesco_lab_missing' )                                          1         0
get_post_meta( 33, 'pagesco_price' ) and 'pagesco_author'                    1         0
WP_Query: the three cheapest books                                           6         0
get_post_meta() over 11 books, one by one                                   11         0
get_post_meta() over 11 books, after update_postmeta_cache()                 1         0

Reading the rows: (1) autoloaded options were loaded in one query before the script ran; (2) a missing option costs one query, then WordPress remembers it in notoptions; (3) the first meta read loads all of the book's meta, the second is free; (4) WP_Query's six queries (IDs, posts, meta, terms) and its own result cache (6.1); (5) N+1, one query per book; (6) update_postmeta_cache() turns them into one. Every "again" column is 0: within one request WordPress never asks twice. Accept a different row 4 count on a site with other book data; the 0 is the point.

Lab 8.2-B: labs/module-15/l8_2-performance-caching/cheapest_cache_starter.php → cheapest_cache_solution.php

As shipped (no cache, no invalidation):

first call: 1 query, Pride and Prejudice at $7.99
second call: 1 query, Pride and Prejudice at $7.99
after Dune's price drop: 1 query, Dune at $1.99
after the price is back: 1 query, Pride and Prejudice at $7.99
Dune's price restored to 14.99
TODO 1 and 2: the second call should cost 0 queries, the call after each price change 1

The solution prints:

first call: 1 query, Pride and Prejudice at $7.99
second call: 0 queries, Pride and Prejudice at $7.99
after Dune's price drop: 1 query, Dune at $1.99
after the price is back: 1 query, Pride and Prejudice at $7.99
Dune's price restored to 14.99

With TODO 1 alone (the cache, but no wp_cache_set_last_changed() in the writer), the call after the price drop prints 0 queries, Pride and Prejudice at $7.99: a stale answer, with no message, exactly Lesson 8.2's Mistake 1. TODO 2 makes every write renew the key. Why is_string() for the hit: a miss is false. The script restores Dune's price; a learner who stopped it halfway restores it with wp post meta update 30 pagesco_price 14.99.

Handout: labs/module-15/l8_2-performance-caching/debug_events_cache.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped, PHP prints:

Next event: Warning: Undefined property: stdClass::$titel in /var/www/html/debug_events_cache.php on line 36

Next three: Book club: Dune
Next five:  Book club: Dune
Warning: Undefined variable $pagesco_seats in /var/www/html/debug_events_cache.php on line 41
Seats in those five: 20
[new lines in wp-content/debug.log]
[26-Oct-2026 13:26:42 UTC] PHP Warning:  Undefined property: stdClass::$titel in /var/www/html/debug_events_cache.php on line 36
[26-Oct-2026 13:26:42 UTC] PHP Warning:  Undefined variable $pagesco_seats in /var/www/html/debug_events_cache.php on line 41
  1. Warning, line 36: ->titel is not a property of the row (title). The line prints "Next event: " with nothing after it.
  2. Warning, line 41: $pagesco_seats is added to before it exists; PHP treats it as 0 and says so. Start it at 0 before the loop.
  3. Silent, line 24: the key has no $limit, so the first call's answer (one event) is returned for three and for five: "Next three" and "Next five" show one title, and the seats are one event's. The key must name everything the result depends on (Lesson 8.2, the events cache's md5( $limit . '|' . $location )).

Fixed (the three changes), it prints:

Next event: Book club: Dune
Next three: Book club: Dune, Story time: The Hobbit, An evening with Ana Ruiz
Next five:  Book club: Dune, Story time: The Hobbit, An evening with Ana Ruiz, Poetry open mic, Book club: Fahrenheit 451
Seats in those five: 125

The order of discovery matters: the two Warnings are found first, and only then does the output look plausible enough to hide the third. Counting the titles is the evidence.

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 8.1 · Debugging & Testing
  1. B WP_DEBUG true, WP_DEBUG_DISPLAY false, WP_DEBUG_LOG set to a path outside the web root: Errors are recorded but never shown, and the log lives where the web server does not serve it, unlike the default wp-content/debug.log.
  2. A An email with the plugin's name, the error and a recovery mode link: The fatal error handler finds the plugin from the file in the error and emails a recovery mode link that logs in with that plugin paused.
  3. C So that the test controls the answer, including failures, and does not depend on the network: The filter answers in place of the network, so each test decides exactly what the supplier says, including 503s and broken JSON, and the result depends only on your code.
  4. C A phpcs:ignore comment for that rule on that line, with the reason: The output is escaped by core, which PHPCS cannot know; an ignore comment for exactly that rule on exactly that line, with the reason, documents the decision.
  5. A The test library reinstalls that site's database on every run: The test library drops and reinstalls the tables of the database it is configured with on every run, so it must never be a database you care about.
Lesson 8.2 · Performance & Caching
  1. B One query per request; an autoloaded row for the option removes it: Each request asks the database once and learns that the row does not exist; an autoloaded row is read with all other autoloaded options, for no extra query.
  2. C Until the end of the current request: The default object cache is an array in PHP's memory, emptied at the end of every request; only a drop-in such as Redis keeps it between requests.
  3. A So that every change to the table makes all older cached results unreachable: Every write renews the group's last-changed value, so all keys built before the write are never read again: no stale result without having to find and delete each entry.
  4. B Because WordPress skips loading optimizations for block templates, and the image is not in the Media Library: wp_get_loading_optimization_attributes() returns early for the template context, and width and height are only added for Media Library images; the theme must take care of its own images.
  5. C That a change you make can be compared against it on the same machine; field data from real visitors decides the rest: Lab measurements compare changes on one machine with the same settings; real visitors' phones and networks, measured as field data after launch, decide the Core Web Vitals.

Exit Ticket

  1. A critical error: the server's error log (on development: wordpress/wp-content/debug.log) and the recovery mode email WordPress sent the admin address (it names the plugin, the file and the line); from the command line wp --skip-plugins=<plugin> plugin deactivate <plugin> works although every other command dies. A must-use plugin: rename the file.
  2. The last-changed value in the key: flush_cache() renews it after every insert or delete, so after a change every key is new and every old answer is simply never read again; nothing has to find and delete old entries. Without it, a persistent cache would show the old list until it expired.
  3. Lab against field: a lab measurement compares before and after a change on the same machine and setup (CLS 0.101 to 0.001 is real); it cannot say what visitors on mid-range phones over weak connections experience, how a full catalog or real traffic behaves, or what a page cache changes: field data after the launch (Lesson 8.4).

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A ("true, wp-content/debug.log"). Remembers that logging is good, not where the log goes. Run Mistake 1's curl again: 200.
  • Q2 with pagesco_supplier_bad_response. Thinks an HTML body makes it a bad response first. The status is checked before the body (Client::decode()): read the method's three numbered comments.
  • Q3 B ("wp eval deactivate_plugins"). Thinks wp eval runs before WordPress loads. Everything in WP-CLI except a few commands loads WordPress, plugins included.
  • Q8 A ("int(24)"). Does not separate one request from the next. Two WP-CLI commands are two PHP processes; show P4 (wp_using_ext_object_cache() is NULL).
  • Q9 B ("500, persistent cache"). A warm persistent cache hides N+1; flush it and the 501 queries are back. The fix is in the code.
  • Q10 A ("nothing breaks"). Forgets that an empty array is falsy. Run wp eval 'var_dump( false !== array(), (bool) array() );': the second value is the truthiness of array(). A good moment to say: verify claims about code by running them.
  • Q11 A ("the fonts"). Remembers the 0.001 that was left. The 0.1 jump was the image without reserved space.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 16: Distribution and Deployment

Answers and reasoning for every Session 16 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-run items, the lab reference outputs, the lessons' recorded cards, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 16 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up

  1. W1 (Lesson 8.1). To a log outside the web root (or the server's log), display always off: wp-content/debug.log is a public download (200 for anyone).
  2. W2 (Lesson 8.1). npx @wordpress/env run cli --env-cwd=wp-content/plugins/pagesco-books vendor/bin/phpunit, in the plugin's own wp-env test site on port 8889, never in the site you develop on.
  3. W3 (Lesson 8.2). N+1 queries; update_postmeta_cache( $ids ) before the loop loads all the meta in one query.
  4. W4 (Lesson 8.2). add() calls flush_cache(), which renews wp_cache_get_last_changed( 'pagesco_events' ), and that value is part of every key.
  5. W5 (Lesson 8.2). The Image block's aspectRatio (CLS 0.101 to 0.001: the space is reserved) and a preload with fetchpriority high in wp_preload_resources (LCP a little earlier).

8.3 · Internationalization, Accessibility & Distribution: self-check

Q1
make-pot prints Warning: Multiple placeholders should be ordered. for __( 'Showing %s of %s books', 'pagesco-books' ). What is the fix?
A) Nothing: it is only a warning, and the POT file was written
B) Split it into two strings, one per number
C) Numbered placeholders, 'Showing %1$s of %2$s books', with a translators comment naming both
D) Use _n() instead of __()
Why: With %1$s and %2$s a language can put the numbers in another order (the lab's Spanish translation does: "De %2$s libros, se muestran %1$s"); the comment tells the translator what each number is. make-pot writes the file anyway and exits 0, which is why its warnings scroll by unnoticed.
A is wrong: The string ships untranslatable in languages with another word order.
B is wrong: Fragments are worse: a translator cannot know how they join.
D is wrong: _n() chooses a plural form; it does not number placeholders.
Q2
The Book Card's editor script is build/blocks/book-card/index.js. In the WP-CLI container this prints its hash. Which file holds its Spanish strings?
printf '%s' 'build/blocks/book-card/index.js' | md5sum | cut -d' ' -f1
languages/pagesco-books-es_ES-f9065c2ff2c4d291b5b56d1be2a45f3f.json
Why: WordPress looks for the JSON whose name contains the MD5 of the script's path relative to the plugin; make-json computes it from the PO's #: references, which is why the POT must point at the built file (--exclude=src/blocks). Lesson 8.3's languages/ folder has exactly this file.
Q3
The contrast script printed slate on paper 5.8:1 AA. What does that allow?
A) Normal-size text in slate on paper meets WCAG AA (4.5:1), but not AAA (7:1)
B) Only large text: normal text needs 7:1
C) Nothing: anything under 7:1 fails WCAG
D) Slate is safe on every background of the palette
Why: AA needs 4.5:1 for normal text and 3:1 for large text and control outlines; 7:1 is AAA. The pair passes AA, which is what most contracts mean. Exercise 2 shows slate failing on ink and on oxblood: a ratio belongs to a pair, not to a color.
B is wrong: 4.5:1 is the AA limit for normal text; 3:1 for large.
C is wrong: AAA is the stricter level; AA is the usual target.
D is wrong: Slate on ink is 2.4:1 and slate on oxblood 1.5:1 (Exercise 2).
Q4
Why does the new uninstall.php read the opt-in with get_option( 'pagesco_books_settings', array() ) and not with Settings::get( 'delete_data' )?
A) get_option() is faster
B) Settings::get() would translate the value
C) uninstall.php runs after the option is deleted
D) During uninstall the plugin is not loaded: its classes, post type and taxonomy are not there
Why: WordPress runs uninstall.php without booting the plugin. That is also why the file registers the genre taxonomy before get_terms() (Common Mistake 2: invalid_taxonomy, and five orphaned genres). An empty row means delete_data is missing, which means off.
A is wrong: Both read the same autoloaded row; speed is not the point.
B is wrong: Settings::get() translates nothing about delete_data.
C is wrong: uninstall.php reads the option first and deletes it near the end.
Q5
Which of these must NOT be in the release ZIP of Pages & Co. Books?
A) build/blocks/blocks-manifest.php
B) phpcs.xml.dist
C) languages/pagesco-books-es_ES.l10n.php
D) vendor/autoload.php
Why: Development files (rulesets, phpunit.xml.dist, .wp-env.json, tests/) do not ship: Plugin Check reports them (application_detected, hidden_files), and the lab release_check.sh fails a ZIP that contains them. The build, the translations and the autoloader are what the plugin runs.
A is wrong: Without the manifest no block is registered.
C is wrong: The Spanish translation is part of the release.
D is wrong: The plugin requires it on every load; only the development packages must stay out.
Q6
What does --no-purge change in wp i18n make-json pagesco-books-es_ES.po --no-purge?
It keeps the JavaScript strings in the PO file; without it make-json moves them into the JSON files and removes them from the PO
Why: Strings used in both PHP and JavaScript ("Mark sold out") would otherwise go missing from the next .mo, and the source PO loses entries.

8.3 · Internationalization, Accessibility & Distribution: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo get_locale(), " ", determine_locale(), "\n";'
en_US en_US
Why: Both en_US: the site language is English, and in WP-CLI determine_locale() has no user or request to change it. After Lesson 8.3 installs es_ES the site stays English until wp site switch-language es_ES; a user's locale applies to wp-admin only.
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
printf '%s' 'build/blocks/book-card/index.js' | md5sum | cut -d' ' -f1
f9065c2ff2c4d291b5b56d1be2a45f3f
Why: The MD5 of the path relative to the plugin: the long part of the Book Card's Spanish JSON file name (self-check Q2).

8.4 · Deployment & Maintenance: self-check

Q7
Maria changed the header in the live site's Site Editor. You deploy theme 1.2.0 with a new parts/header.html. What do visitors see?
A) The new header from the theme's file
B) Maria's header: Site Editor changes are saved in the database, and they win over the theme's files
C) Both headers, one above the other
D) An error until someone resets the template part
Why: Code goes up, content comes down: an edit in the Site Editor on the live site is content (a wp_template_part post). Changes that should become code are exported into the theme, committed and deployed (Lesson 3.3); then the database copy is reset.
A is wrong: Only if the template part had never been edited on the live site.
C is wrong: WordPress uses one version of a template part.
D is wrong: Nothing is broken; the database version simply wins.
Q8
On the course site after Lesson 8.3: what does this print, and which table makes the difference?
echo "$(wp db tables | wc -l) $(wp db tables --all-tables-with-prefix | wc -l)"
12 13: wp_pagesco_events, the plugin's own table, which WordPress does not register
Why: wp db tables and wp search-replace work on the registered (core) tables by default; --all-tables-with-prefix adds the plugin's table. A launch export without it has 12 tables, and the live Events page says "No events are scheduled" (Common Mistake 2; the debug handout debug_launch_check.sh).
Q9
Why does every migration command in Lesson 8.4 say --skip-columns=guid?
A) The GUID is an identifier feed readers use to recognize posts, not a link: it must not change
B) GUID columns are serialized and would break
C) The guid column is too long for search-replace
D) WordPress rewrites GUIDs itself on the first request
Why: http://localhost:8080/?p=30 looks like a URL but identifies the post; changing it makes feed readers show every post as new. The launch database keeps 34 GUIDs with the old address, and that is right.
B is wrong: The guid column holds plain strings.
C is wrong: Length is not the issue.
D is wrong: WordPress never rewrites GUIDs.
Q10
The project's .gitignore says !/themes/, then /themes/*, then !/themes/pagesco/. Why not just !/themes/pagesco/ after /*?
A) Git reads .gitignore from the bottom up
B) The order does not matter; it is only for readability
C) Without the first line Git would also track Twenty Twenty-Five
D) Git cannot let a file back in when its folder is ignored: the folder is let in, its contents ignored again, then the one subfolder let in
Why: /* ignores the themes folder itself, so a rule for something inside it is never even read. The three-line pattern is the documented way. git check-ignore -v names the line that ignores a file when you wonder why it is missing.
A is wrong: Git reads it top to bottom; the last matching rule wins.
B is wrong: With only the last line, themes/pagesco/ stays ignored.
C is wrong: Twenty Twenty-Five is ignored by /themes/* either way.
Q11
In a clean copy of the tagged plugin (git archive), which Composer command builds the production autoloader, and what does it leave out?
composer install --no-dev --optimize-autoloader: the development packages (PHPUnit, PHPCS), so vendor/composer has no autoload_files.php that requires them
Why: The working copy's autoloader requires files from development packages on every load (deep_copy.php, PHPUnit's Functions.php, the polyfills); a ZIP without those packages dies with "Failed opening required" (Lesson 8.3's Mistake 3). --optimize-autoloader writes a class map.
Q12
On the rehearsal server the account pagesco_app has no LOCK TABLES right. What happens to wp db export "$BACKUP"?
A) It works: WP-CLI locks nothing
B) It asks for the root password
C) It fails with error 1044, and leaves a small header-only file under the backup's name
D) It exports only the tables the account may lock
Why: mariadb-dump locks each table while it reads it. The recorded run shows the error and an 831-byte file: a file in the backup folder is not a backup; its size and a restore test are what count. The lesson grants exactly LOCK TABLES, as root, and then proves the backup by restoring a deleted book.
A is wrong: wp db export runs mariadb-dump, which uses LOCK TABLES.
B is wrong: WP-CLI uses wp-config.php's account and asks nothing.
D is wrong: It stops at the first table it cannot lock.

8.4 · Deployment & Maintenance: predict, then run

P3
Predict the output, then run it.
wp db tables | wc -l
12
Why: The twelve tables WordPress registers; the plugin's wp_pagesco_events is not among them (--all-tables-with-prefix adds it).
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
wp search-replace 'http://localhost:8080' 'https://www.pagesco.example' --all-tables-with-prefix --skip-columns=guid --dry-run --format=count
9
Why: Nine replacements, as for 8081: the address counts, not its length. --format=count prints only the number.

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, on a fresh course site at the state each declares (WordPress 7.1.3, PHP 8.5, MySQL 9.7, WP-CLI 2.12).

Lab 8.3-A (guided): labs/module-16/l8_3-i18n-a11y-distribution/plural_strings_starter.php → plural_strings_solution.php

As shipped:

en  1 book in stock | 9 books in stock
en  Showing 3 of 11 books
en  Order | Order
en  Books in Fantasy
es  1 book in stock | 9 books in stock
es  Showing 3 of 11 books
es  Order | Order
es  Books in Fantasy
TODO 1 to 4: every es line should be Spanish

The solution prints:

en  1 book in stock | 9 books in stock
en  Showing 3 of 11 books
en  Order | Order
en  Books in Fantasy
es  1 libro disponible | 9 libros disponibles
es  De 11 libros, se muestran 3
es  Orden | Pedido
es  Libros de Fantasy

Why each starter string fails: (1) two fragments and no plural form; (2) unnumbered placeholders, so the translator's reordered string never matches the msgid; (3) one msgid for two meanings, so the file's two contexts never match; (4) a fragment with the genre glued on. The translation file stays unchanged: the code changes. Context keys in the file are "context\x04msgid", exactly what make-php writes.
