The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 8.3 · Internationalization, Accessibility & Distribution
  1. C 'Showing %1$s of %2$s books' with a translators comment naming both numbers: A whole sentence with numbered placeholders and a translators comment: the translator sees what each part is and may change the order.
  2. A The MD5 hash of the script's path in the plugin, assets/js/stock-widget.js: WordPress hashes the path of the script it is about to print, relative to the plugin, and looks for a JSON file with that hash; make-json takes the paths from the PO references.
  3. B The page passed the rules a machine can check; keyboard and screen reader tests are still needed: Automated rules catch a part of the problems, such as markup and contrast; focus visibility, meaningful alternative text, reading order and screen reader experience need people to test.
  4. C Because the plugin is not loaded, so the genre taxonomy is not registered: During uninstall the plugin is not loaded, so its taxonomy is not registered, and get_terms() rejects unregistered taxonomies with invalid_taxonomy; register it in uninstall.php first.
  5. B Because the development autoloader requires files from packages the ZIP does not contain: With development packages installed, the autoloader requires some of their files on every load; the ZIP does not contain them, so the plugin would die with a fatal error on the client's site.
Lesson 8.4 · Deployment & Maintenance
  1. B Only the plugin ZIP built from the release tag: Code goes up, content comes down: after the launch the live database is the original, so an update deploys only the release files built from the new tag.
  2. C The plugin's own events table: By default WP-CLI works on the tables WordPress registers; the plugin's wp_pagesco_events table is not one of them, so the export silently leaves it out.
  3. A The new pattern stays unknown until the cached pattern list expires: Without development mode WordPress caches the theme's pattern list in a site transient for the current Version, for up to 30 minutes; a new Version makes the cache invalid at once.
  4. C Read and change the shop's database, and nothing beyond it: Least privilege limits the damage: the account can read and change the shop's own database, which is serious, but it reaches no other database and cannot manage users or the server.
  5. B It never runs: the event stays scheduled and nothing starts it: With page-view spawning off, nothing runs due events until something calls wp-cron.php or wp cron event run: the job waits, scheduled, forever.

Exit Ticket

  1. Code, content, configuration: the plugin or theme (Git, a tagged release, a ZIP deployed: up); the books, events and pages (the database: once at launch, then created live; copies come down); wp-config.php's secrets and the supplier's key (written per environment, never copied).
  2. String rules: any two of: whole sentences with placeholders; numbered placeholders when there are several; a translators comment; _n() for counts; _x() for ambiguous words; literal strings only; escape the translation.
  3. Hard requirements: accept any honest pair with the right layer; the brief's own table ("Where each requirement is handled") is the reference for the conversation in Session 17.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A ("only a warning"). Treats warnings as noise. Show the lab's Spanish line that reorders the numbers.
  • Q3 C ("under 7:1 fails"). Mixes AA and AAA.
  • Q5 A or D. Thinks build/ or the autoloader are development files; they are what runs.
  • Q7 A ("the new header"). The code/content split has not landed: show Lesson 3.2's wp_template_part posts.
  • Q9 B ("serialized"). Confuses GUIDs with options; the guid column is a plain string.
  • Q12 A ("it works"). Has never restored a backup; restore_drill is the cure.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 17: Final Project Kickoff and Studio I

Answers and reasoning for every Session 17 item: the brief by layer, the self-check, the predict-then-run items, the studio reference outputs for Milestones 1 to 4, the lab reference solutions and the exit ticket.

Session 17 12 self-check items Confidential

Start Here · The Brief by Layer

#RequirementLayer and class (the lesson's map)
1Reserve, three days, expiredHolds::place() computes pickup_by from the setting; Holds::status() computes "expired"
2Limits, duplicates, sold out, settingsHolds::place(); settings hold_days and max_holds on Settings > Pages & Co. Books
3My holds, cancel, only your ownthe My Holds block; HoldsTable::finish() with the user ID in its WHERE; a "not found" for anyone else's hold
4Staff list and "collected"Books > Holds (Admin\HoldsPage), the collect handler, capability edit_others_posts
5The emailthe plugin's own action pagesco_books_hold_placed, and Holds\HoldMail listening to it
6No JavaScript, screen readers, no reloadforms that post to admin-post.php; an Interactivity API store that sends the same form to the REST API; role="status" messages
7Live prices on the home pagethe pagesco/book binding source learns a book's slug; the theme's staff picks pattern binds its prices
8Personal data, tested, safe deploysthe daily job deletes holds 30 days after their pickup date; uninstall drops the table; lint, tests, smoke test; a code-only deploy with a backup first

9.1 · Self-check

Q1
Why does the reference solution keep holds in a custom table rather than a post type?
A) A post type cannot store a date
B) A hold is one customer, one book, a status and a date: typed columns with indexes answer "this customer's holds" and "what is waiting" directly, and old rows can be deleted cleanly
C) Custom tables are always faster than wp_posts
D) Post types cannot be private
Why: Lesson 9.1, "The data: why a custom table": a hold has no title, content, URL or revisions; as posts every question would be a meta query, and holds would leak into places built for content (exports, search, the posts routes).
A is wrong: Post meta can store any date; the problem is querying it.
C is wrong: Not always: the point is the shape of the data and the two questions the shop asks.
D is wrong: They can (public => false); that is not the reason.
Q2
Where does the reference solution keep the status expired?
A) In the status column, set by the daily cron job
B) In a post meta field on the book
C) In a transient that expires after hold_days
D) Nowhere: Holds::status() computes it from pickup_by and today's date every time a hold is shown
Why: A stored "expired" needs a job to update it and is wrong between the moment it should change and the moment the job runs. The table stores waiting, collected or cancelled; expired is derived.
A is wrong: The daily job deletes old holds 30 days after pickup; it never marks them.
B is wrong: Holds are not book data, and a book can have many holds.
C is wrong: A transient could vanish at any time (Lesson 7.3): never the only home of data.
Q3
On the course site at the end of Lesson 8.4, what does this print? And after Milestone 4?
grep -c '::class,' wp-content/plugins/pagesco-books/src/Plugin.php
20 (twenty features); 25 after Milestone 4 (five new features, one line each)
Why: Lesson 5.1's rule: one class in src/, one line in Plugin::feature_classes(). Add each line together with its file: a line for a class that does not exist yet stops every request with "Class not found".
Q4
The plugin is already active on your development site. You add Holds\HoldsTable::install() to Activator::activate(). When is the holds table created on YOUR site?
A) On the next request that loads WordPress: maybe_upgrade() runs on init, finds no version option and installs it
B) Never, until you deactivate and reactivate the plugin
C) When you run wp db query
D) At 3 a.m., with the daily job
Why: An active plugin's activation hook does not run again, and neither does it on an update (Lesson 8.4). Milestone 2's check proves it: wp db query (which does not start WordPress) sees no table, one curl later the table and version 1 exist. The same happens on the live site in Milestone 9.
B is wrong: That would work too, but the design makes it unnecessary, and it is impossible on a live update.
C is wrong: wp db query talks to MySQL without loading WordPress: no PHP of yours runs.
D is wrong: The daily job deletes old holds; it does not create tables.
Q5
A reviewer finds these lines in Holds::cancel() (the reference has one call on one line). What can a logged-in customer now do?
$done = HoldsTable::finish(
	$hold_id,
	'cancelled'
);
if ( ! $user_id || ! $done ) {
A) Nothing new: the $user_id check still stops it
B) Cancel only their own holds, as before
C) Cancel ANY customer's waiting hold by sending its ID
D) Collect holds as staff
Why: finish() puts the user ID into the SQL WHERE only when it is passed. Without it, the WHERE is id and status: any logged-in customer cancels anyone's hold. This is the insecure direct object reference of the Security and Privacy Checklist; HoldsTest's test_only_the_customer catches it (Milestone 7).
A is wrong: $user_id only proves someone is logged in, not that the hold is theirs.
B is wrong: That needs the owner in the query, which this line no longer passes.
D is wrong: Collecting is another method and another capability.
Q6
Why does Holds::place() answer the limit, a duplicate and a sold-out book with status 409, not 400 or 403?
A) 409 is the only status WordPress allows in a WP_Error
B) The request is well formed and the customer is allowed to ask; the shop's current state says no (Conflict)
C) 403 would log the customer out
D) 409 makes the browser retry automatically
Why: 400 means a malformed request (a missing or wrong argument, which the REST route's args catch), 401/403 who is asking, 404 no such thing. Every door passes the status in the error's data on, so the REST route and the form handler answer the same.
A is wrong: Any status can go in the data array.
C is wrong: No status logs anyone out.
D is wrong: Browsers do not retry on 409.
Q7
Common Mistake 2, run as Devon (an author) about Maria's post 15. What does it print, and which check belongs in a handler?
wp eval 'var_dump( current_user_can( "delete_posts" ), current_user_can( "delete_post", 15 ) );' --user=devon
bool(true) then bool(false): the meta capability with the ID, delete_post, 15
Why: delete_posts asks "may this user delete posts at all?" (his own, yes). A handler must ask about THIS post. Holds are not posts and have no meta capability, so there ownership goes into the SQL WHERE with the user ID from the login.
Q8
Why does the confirmation email live in HoldMail, listening to pagesco_books_hold_placed, instead of a wp_mail() call inside Holds::place()?
A) wp_mail() cannot be called from a class
B) Actions run faster than function calls
C) Emails must be sent from a must-use plugin
D) The rules class knows nothing about email: others (an SMS, a staff notice in Exercise 2) attach to the same moment without changing the rules
Why: An action is an extension point for a moment others may want to act at (Lesson 2.3). The rules class stays one job; Exercise 2 adds the shop's email at priority 20 from outside the plugin.
A is wrong: Any PHP code can call wp_mail().
B is wrong: do_action() adds a little work, not speed.
C is wrong: Any plugin can send email.
Q9
Which line computes the last day of pickup, three days from today, the way the reference does?
A) wp_date( 'Y-m-d', time() + $days * DAY_IN_SECONDS )
B) date( 'Y-m-d', time() + $days * DAY_IN_SECONDS )
C) current_time( 'mysql', true )
D) gmdate( 'Y-m-d' ) . ' + 3'
Why: pickup_by is a date in the shop's time zone (the counter's calendar): wp_date() formats a timestamp in the site's time zone. date() and gmdate() use PHP's own zone (UTC in WordPress), which moves the day near midnight.
B is wrong: date() ignores the site's time zone setting.
C is wrong: That is now, in UTC, as a datetime: what created_at stores.
D is wrong: String concatenation, not date arithmetic.
Q10
HoldsTable::waiting() uses this trick. What does it print, and what does it mean for the query?
wp eval 'global $wpdb; echo $wpdb->prepare( "WHERE ( %d = 0 OR user_id = %d )", 0, 0 ), "\n";'
WHERE ( 0 = 0 OR user_id = 0 ): always true, so the user filter is switched off (all customers); with 4 it becomes ( 4 = 0 OR user_id = 4 )
Why: One prepared statement serves every combination of filters: a 0 turns its filter off, any other value keeps it, and every value still goes through prepare().
Q11
Milestone 4 sends a forged "Mark collected" from Maria's own logged-in session, but without the form's nonce. What does admin-post.php answer?
A) 303, back to Books > Holds with ?pagesco_hold=collected
B) 401: Maria is not logged in
C) 403 with The link you followed has expired.
D) 400: the action is unknown
Why: check_admin_referer() fails without a valid nonce for pagesco_books_collect_hold_5, and WordPress dies with Lesson 5.3's message and 403. The nonce is what tells a real button press from a request another page sent with Maria's cookies.
A is wrong: That is what the real button gets.
B is wrong: Her cookies are valid: that is what makes the forgery dangerous.
D is wrong: The handler exists; 400 is what admin-post.php answers when no handler does.
Q12
The lesson's mental model is the shop's counter. Name its three layers and the one job of each.
the data (a table class that only stores), the rules (one class, Holds, that only decides), the doors (REST routes, forms, blocks that only check who is asking and pass the question on)
Why: When a rule changes, it changes in one place and every door follows. The guide's requirement map shows which layer handles each line of the brief.

9.1 · Predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'var_dump( current_user_can( "edit_post", 15 ), current_user_can( "edit_posts" ) );' --user=devon
bool(false)
bool(true)
Why: Same lesson as Q7, other verb: edit_post with the ID asks about Maria's post (no), edit_posts asks about posts in general (yes: his own). Every "may I change THIS?" check takes the ID.
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
grep -c '::class,' wp-content/plugins/pagesco-books/src/Plugin.php
20
Why: Twenty features before the project; Milestone 2 makes it 21, Milestone 3 22, Milestone 4 25 (Q3).
P3
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo get_option( "date_format" ), " | ", wp_date( get_option( "date_format" ), strtotime( "2026-11-05 12:00:00" ) ), "\n";'
F j, Y | November 5, 2026
Why: Holds::label() formats pickup_by with the site's date_format option through wp_date(); the date is read at noon so no time zone can move it to the day before.
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'global $wpdb; echo $wpdb->prepare( "WHERE ( %d = 0 OR user_id = %d )", 4, 4 ), "\n";'
WHERE ( 4 = 0 OR user_id = 4 )
Why: With a real ID the condition keeps the filter: only user 4's rows. With 0 it is always true (Q10).

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, on a site at the end of Lesson 8.4 (after:lesson_29), copied into the WordPress root and run with wp eval-file --use-include.

Lab 9.1-A (guided): labs/module-17/l9_1-final-project/ownership_sql_starter.php → ownership_sql_solution.php

As shipped:

Devon closes wish 1          TODO
Devon closes wish 3          TODO
Maria closes wish 1          TODO
Maria closes wish 1 again    TODO
A visitor closes wish 2      TODO
Maria's wishes, newest first:
  TODO (pagesco_lab_wishes_of() returns nothing yet)

The solution prints:

Devon closes wish 1          pagesco_wish_not_found (404)
Devon closes wish 3          closed
Maria closes wish 1          closed
Maria closes wish 1 again    pagesco_wish_not_found (404)
A visitor closes wish 2      pagesco_wish_not_found (404)
Maria's wishes, newest first:
  wish 2: The Hobbit, open
  wish 1: Dune, closed

What to look for: the owner in the update's WHERE (three keys, three formats), 1 === on $wpdb->update() (it returns the number of rows changed, 0 when nothing matched, false on an error), the user from get_current_user_id(), one error for all refusals. Maria's second attempt is also a 404: the wish is no longer open, the same "not found" as Lesson 9.1's cancel of a hold that is not waiting.

A visitor books event 1          pagesco_rsvp_login_required (401)
Jordan books event 99            pagesco_rsvp_no_event (404)
Jordan books event 2             Story time: The Hobbit

Jordan books event 5             Book club: Fahrenheit 451

Priya books event 7              Book club: Pride and Prejudice
Book club: Dune on 2027-11-04    upcoming
Book club: Dune on 2027-11-05    past

The order of the rules matters: who is asking first (no query for visitors), then whether the thing exists, then the shop's state. Count the existing seats BEFORE adding (>= 2); a learner who counts after adding refuses the second seat. The computed status compares two strings of the same format, as Holds::status() does with dates.

Lab 9.1-C: labs/module-17/l9_1-final-project/own_action_starter/ → own_action_solution/ with test_own_action.php

With the starter's pagesco-lab-requests.php in mu-plugins/:

Listeners on the action: 1
Listeners heard: nothing yet (TODO 1)

With the solution's:

Listeners on the action: 2
10 customer note: Dune for jordan
20 shop note: order "Dune" (request 1)
10 customer note: Emma for priya
20 shop note: order "Emma" (request 2)

The customer's note at 10 runs before the shop's at 20 for each request; the shop's listener needs 20, 2 (priority, accepted arguments): without the 2, PHP stops with an ArgumentCountError the moment the action fires (worth letting a learner see once). Remove the file afterwards: rm wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/pagesco-lab-requests.php.

Handout: labs/module-17/l9_1-final-project/debug_trash_door.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped, PHP and WordPress print:

Notice: Function wpdb::prepare was called <strong>incorrectly</strong>. The query does not contain the correct number of placeholders (3) for the number of arguments passed (2). Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information. (This message was added in version 4.8.3.) in /var/www/html/wp-includes/functions.php on line 6260
devon may trash "3": yes
Reason: Warning: Undefined array key "why" in /var/www/html/debug_trash_door.php on line 36
[new lines in wp-content/debug.log]
[26-Oct-2026 12:59:26 UTC] PHP Notice:  Function wpdb::prepare was called <strong>incorrectly</strong>. The query does not contain the correct number of placeholders (3) for the number of arguments passed (2). Please see <a href="https://developer.wordpress.org/advanced-administration/debug/debug-wordpress/">Debugging in WordPress</a> for more information. (This message was added in version 4.8.3.) in /var/www/html/wp-includes/functions.php on line 6260
[26-Oct-2026 12:59:26 UTC] PHP Warning:  Undefined array key "why" in /var/www/html/debug_trash_door.php on line 36
  1. doing_it_wrong (a Notice), line 32: three placeholders (%i, %d, %s) and two values. prepare() refuses and returns an empty string, and $wpdb->get_var( '' ) returns the PREVIOUS query's answer: the "3" is Devon's user ID from get_user_by(). Add the third value, 'post'.
  2. Warning, line 36: the request has reason, the code reads why. Read keys from the place that defines them.
  3. Silent, line 28: current_user_can( 'delete_posts' ) asks whether Devon may delete posts at all (his own: yes). The door must ask about this post: current_user_can( 'delete_post', $pagesco_post_id ). Lesson 9.1's Common Mistake 2; the dangerous one, because nothing announces it.

Fixed, it prints:

devon may trash "Welcome to the new Pages & Co. website": no
Reason: duplicate

Opening: Readiness

There is no graded warm-up. The readiness check's correct state: git tag -n lists release-1 ("First launch") and release-2 ("Plugin 1.1.1, theme 1.1.1"), the working copy is clean, the plugin is 1.1.1 and pagesco-live's three services run. Learners who were not there leave with a note on the board and fix it before Session 18.

Exit Ticket

  1. Three layers: data (HoldsTable: the table and its queries, no rules), rules (Holds: every rule, the status, the messages), doors (HoldActions for the forms, HoldsEndpoint for REST, the Reserve and My Holds blocks; HoldsPage is the staff's screen).
  2. Priya's DELETE: 404 pagesco_hold_not_found ("You have no waiting hold with that ID."). Holds::cancel() calls HoldsTable::finish( $hold_id, 'cancelled', $user_id ) with her ID from the login; the update's WHERE matches no row.
  3. Next milestone: any honest answer with the lesson's done-when; Milestone 4's is the forged request answered 403 and the commit, Milestone 5's the smoke test's eight ok lines.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 C ("tables are faster"). Speed is not the argument; the shape of the data and its two questions are. Ask what a hold's title would be.
  • Q2 A ("the cron job marks them"). Thinks of a status as something stored. Ask: what does the page show between midnight and the job's run?
  • Q4 B ("reactivate"). Has not yet met the update path; it matters on the live site, where nobody reactivates (Milestone 9).
  • Q5 A. Confuses "logged in" with "owns it". The guided lab separates the two.
  • Q6 C or D. Guessing at statuses: hand out the five used (200/201, 401, 403, 404, 409) as a card.
  • Q9 B ("date()"). Works on most days and moves the pickup day near midnight. Show wp_timezone_string() on their site.
  • Q11 A. Thinks the logged-in cookie is enough: exactly why forgeries work without a nonce.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 18: Final Project Studio II, Showcase and Next Steps

Answers and reasoning for every Session 18 item: both self-checks and predict items, the studio reference outputs for Milestones 5 to 9, the lab reference solutions, Lesson 9.2's reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 18 12 self-check items Confidential

9.1 · Self-check

Q1
Milestone 7: composer lint finds 11 warnings, all in HoldsTable.php (DirectQuery and NoCaching). What does the reference do?
A) Adds a phpcs:ignore comment to every query
B) Turns the two sniffs off in phpcs.xml.dist for the whole plugin
C) Documents ONE exception in phpcs.xml.dist, for that file, with its reason: a custom table has no WordPress API, and holds are per user and change with every click, so a cache would mostly be invalidated before it is read
D) Moves the holds into post meta so the warnings go away
Why: Lesson 8.1's rule: fix a finding when the fix is cheap, document an exception when it is not, where the events table's exception already is. The events, read by every visitor, ARE cached (Lesson 8.2): the decision is per table, with a reason.
A is wrong: Eleven ignore comments hide the decision instead of writing it down once.
B is wrong: That would also silence the sniffs for code that should be fixed.
D is wrong: That trades a documented decision for the slow meta queries the lesson argued against.
Q2
On the reference after Milestone 8, what does this print? Why did the THEME's version change too?
wp eval 'echo wp_get_theme( "pagesco" )->get( "Version" ), " ", PAGESCO_BOOKS_VERSION, "\n";'
1.2.0 1.2.0: the theme's template and pattern changed, and without development mode the live site caches pattern files until the theme's Version changes (Lesson 8.4)
Why: A new feature is a minor version (1.2.0) for the plugin; the theme gets one because templates/single-book.html and patterns/featured-books.php changed. Version_audit in the kit's labs reads all five places a version lives.
Q3
Milestone 9, on the rehearsal server: right after wp plugin install /deploy/pagesco-books-1.2.0.zip --force, SHOW TABLES LIKE finds no holds table. Why not yet?
A) wp plugin install ran with the OLD plugin in memory, and wp db query does not start WordPress: no new code has run init yet
B) The ZIP was built without src/Holds
C) The database user may not create tables
D) Tables are only created by the activation hook, so it never will be
Why: The next command that loads WordPress (wp theme install, in the lesson) runs the new code; on init, HoldsTable::maybe_upgrade() finds no version option and creates the table. On a real server the first page view does the same.
B is wrong: That ZIP would stop every request with "Class not found" (Common Mistake 1), not leave a quiet gap.
C is wrong: pagesco_app has CREATE (Lesson 8.4's grants); the table appears one command later.
D is wrong: That is exactly what maybe_upgrade() is for.
Q4
After the deploy, the rehearsal server answers 404 for /my-holds/. What is the right fix?
A) Import the development database on the live site
B) Deploy the plugin again with --force
C) Flush the permalinks on the development site
D) Create the page ON the live site (it is content: it lives in the database), with wp post create, as the readme's upgrade notice says
Why: Code goes up, content stays (Lesson 8.4). The page made in Milestone 5 is a row in the development database. The recorded run: 404 before, 200 after creating it.
A is wrong: That would overwrite the live shop's real data with test customers and test holds.
B is wrong: The code is already there; the page is not code.
C is wrong: The live site's permalinks are fine: the page simply does not exist there.
Q5
Milestone 7 measures the front page at 31 queries for a visitor; the baseline was 22. Where do the nine come from, and why does the reference accept them?
three per staff pick (get_page_by_path() for the slug, then the book and its meta) for the live prices; indexed primary-key lookups under a millisecond each, the page has no per-user content so a page cache serves it, and an object cache keeps them
Why: Decide with numbers (Lesson 8.2) and write the decision down. A book's page stays at 25: the Reserve block leaves before its first query when nobody is logged in.
Q6
Why does each Cancel button on My holds carry an aria-label that names its book?
<button type="submit" class="wp-element-button" aria-label="Cancel the hold on 1984">Cancel</button>
<button type="submit" class="wp-element-button" aria-label="Cancel the hold on Dune">Cancel</button>
A) Search engines index aria-labels
B) A screen reader would otherwise read "Cancel, Cancel, Cancel" with no way to tell which hold each one cancels
C) WordPress requires an aria-label on every button
D) It makes the button work without JavaScript
Why: Every control needs a name that makes sense out of context (Lesson 8.3). The visible text stays short; the accessible name says what it acts on.
A is wrong: Accessibility names are for assistive technology, not search.
C is wrong: Nothing in core enforces it.
D is wrong: The form works without JavaScript because it posts to admin-post.php.

9.1 · Predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'echo count( PagesCo\Books\Holds\HoldsTable::waiting( "2000-01-01" ) ), "\n";'
2
Why: waiting() returns the holds that are waiting and whose pickup date is on or after the date given; from 2000-01-01 nothing counts as expired, so both waiting holds of the saved site (Jordan's Dune and 1984) count. From today the answer depends on the day: that is the computed "expired".
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
wp option get pagesco_books_holds_db_version
1
Why: The schema version the code wrote after creating the table; maybe_upgrade() compares it with HoldsTable::DB_VERSION on every init and installs again only when the code is newer.

9.2 · Self-check

Q7
A front end on another origin asks the shop's API. What does this print, and does it make the holds routes public?
curl -s -I -H 'Origin: https://shop.example' http://localhost:8080/wp-json/pagesco/v1/books \
  | tr -d '\r' | grep -i '^access-control-allow-origin'
Access-Control-Allow-Origin: https://shop.example; no: WordPress echoes any origin, so a page anywhere may READ the answers, but the permission callbacks still refuse (a visitor gets 401 from /holds)
Why: CORS decides what a browser lets a page read, not who may act. Safety comes from the permission callbacks and nonces, never from CORS (Lesson 9.2's key takeaway).
Q8
A headless storefront shows the heading "Pages &amp; Co." (the entity, as text). What is the right fix?
A) Switch the heading to innerHTML
B) Change the site title in Settings to "Pages and Co."
C) Know which fields are HTML: decode the stored-escaped name once to text (DOMParser), then put it on the page with textContent
D) Escape it again with encodeURIComponent()
Why: blogname is stored HTML-escaped and the REST index returns it that way (Lesson 1.2, 9.2's Common Mistake 1). textContent is safe and shows the entity literally; innerHTML would show the ampersand and run any HTML in the field.
A is wrong: It fixes the ampersand and opens the page to any HTML that reaches the field.
B is wrong: Changes the shop's name to hide a bug.
D is wrong: URL encoding is for addresses, not text on the page.
Q9
What does WordPress 7.1 ship for AI?
A) Plumbing: the AI client (wp_ai_client_prompt()), the Connectors screen and a switch to turn AI off; no model, no provider and no core feature
B) A small built-in model for alt text
C) A paid OpenAI integration enabled on every site
D) Nothing: AI is only in the separate AI plugin
Why: On the course site wp_supports_ai() is true, but no installed provider offers a model, so prompts return a WP_Error; core never calls the client itself (Q10).
B is wrong: Core ships no model.
C is wrong: Providers are separate plugins, inactive until installed, and need a key.
D is wrong: The client and Connectors ARE in core since 7.0; the features are in plugins.
Q10
What does this print on the course site, and what does it tell you?
grep -rl 'wp_ai_client_prompt *(' wp-admin wp-includes
wp-includes/ai-client.php: only the file that defines the function; no core screen, block or feature calls the AI client
Why: Core provides one shared, provider-neutral client; plugins (the WordPress project's AI plugin first) build the features.
Q11
Lesson 9.2's model-check.php lists the pagesco/check-stock ability as a tool for a model. Which name does the model see?
A) pagesco/check-stock
B) check_stock
C) pagesco-check-stock
D) wpab__pagesco__check-stock
Why: The ability's name made safe for function-calling APIs: the prefix wpab__ and the slash as a double underscore. The resolver then runs the ability as the current user: data for Maria, ability_invalid_permissions for Devon.
A is wrong: Function-calling APIs do not allow the slash.
B is wrong: The namespace stays in the name.
C is wrong: A double underscore replaces the slash, after the wpab__ prefix.
Q12
Before the repository goes public, Lesson 9.2 searches with git log --all -S, not only the current files. Why?
a secret that was committed once and deleted later is still in the history, and anyone who clones the repository can read it; -S lists every commit that added or removed the string, on every branch
Why: The kit's publish_scan lab checks the files on disk (what you are about to commit); the history search checks what you already committed. Both, before publishing.

9.2 · Predict, then run

P3
Predict the output, then run it.
curl -s 'http://localhost:8080/wp-json/?_fields=name'
{"name":"Pages &amp; Co."}
Why: The site title as WordPress stores it, HTML-escaped. A front end decodes it once to text (Lesson 9.2's Common Mistake 1).
P4
Predict the output, then run it.
wp eval 'var_dump( function_exists( "wp_ai_client_prompt" ), wp_supports_ai() );'
bool(true)
bool(true)
Why: The client exists (7.0+) and nothing switched AI off. That says nothing about a model: is_supported_for_text_generation() is false on the course site.

ok      My holds shows the waiting hold
ok      the customer cancels the hold
ok      a customer cannot mark holds collected (status 403)
exit status 0

Lab Reference Solutions

Every output below is real: the lab files run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, l9_1-final-project/ on a site at the end of Lesson 8.4, l9_2-next-steps/ at the end of Lesson 9.1.

Lab 9.1-A: labs/module-18/l9_1-final-project/version_audit_starter.php → version_audit_solution.php

As shipped:

pagesco-books.php header   1.1.1
PAGESCO_BOOKS_VERSION      TODO
readme.txt Stable tag      TODO
package.json version       TODO
theme style.css Version    TODO
Verdict: TODO

The solution prints:

pagesco-books.php header   1.1.1
PAGESCO_BOOKS_VERSION      1.1.1
readme.txt Stable tag      1.1.1
package.json version       1.1.1
theme style.css Version    1.1.1
All five agree: 1.1.1

On a learner's site after Milestone 8 every line says 1.2.0. get_plugin_data() with false, false neither marks up nor translates (and so does not load the text domain too early); get_file_data() reads any Name: value header line, the readme's Stable tag included; theme header names are case-sensitive (Version).

Lab 9.1-C: labs/module-18/l9_1-final-project/smoke_rest_starter.sh → smoke_rest_solution.sh

The solution prints (exit status 0):

ok      an unknown genre is refused (status 400)

Each check uses the response, never PHP: status codes with -w '%{http_code}', a header with -I, a redirect with %{redirect_url}. On the rehearsal server the same script takes http://localhost:8081.

Handout: labs/module-18/l9_1-final-project/debug_release_check.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped:

Warning: Undefined array key "verison" in /var/www/html/debug_release_check.php on line 23
plugin header    1.1.1  -> 1.2.0 ok
Deprecated: version_compare(): Passing null to parameter #1 ($version1) of type string is deprecated in /var/www/html/debug_release_check.php on line 33
package.json            -> 1.2.0 ok
theme style.css         -> 1.2.0 ok
  1. Warning, line 23: the key is version, not verison; the null it returns is the cause of the next message.
  2. Deprecated (in the printf): version_compare() received null. Fixing the key removes both.
  3. Silent, line 24: get( 'version' ) returns false (header names are case-sensitive: Version); version_compare() turns false into an empty string, which is "older" than 1.2.0, so the line says ok for the wrong reason.

Fixed, it prints:

plugin header    1.1.1  -> 1.2.0 ok
package.json     1.1.1  -> 1.2.0 ok
theme style.css  1.1.1  -> 1.2.0 ok
Lab 9.2-A (guided): labs/module-18/l9_2-next-steps/cors_headers_starter.sh → cors_headers_solution.sh

As shipped:

== GET /pagesco/v1/books, from http://localhost:3000 ==
Allow-Origin: http://localhost:3000
Allow-Credentials: TODO
Expose-Headers: TODO
Books: TODO
holds as a visitor: TODO

The solution prints:

== GET /pagesco/v1/books, from http://localhost:3000 ==
Allow-Origin: http://localhost:3000
Allow-Credentials: true
Expose-Headers: X-WP-Total, X-WP-TotalPages, Link
Books: 11 on 2 pages
holds as a visitor: 401, Allow-Origin: http://localhost:3000

WordPress repeats any origin and allows credentials, so the browser sends cookies with the page's requests: a nonce is what stops a forged change, as in Lesson 9.1. The 401 on /holds is the permission callback; CORS only makes the refusal readable.

Lab 9.2-B: labs/module-18/l9_2-next-steps/ai_readiness_starter.php → ai_readiness_solution.php

As shipped:

AI client in this WordPress: yes
AI allowed on this site: yes
AI providers listed:  (active: 0)
A text model available: TODO
Answer: TODO
With AI switched off: allowed yes, answer: TODO

The solution prints:

AI client in this WordPress: yes
AI allowed on this site: yes
AI providers listed: anthropic, google, openai (active: 0)
A text model available: no
Answer: Ask us at the counter about Dune.
With AI switched off: allowed no, answer: Ask us at the counter about Dune.

With a provider and a key the answer would be the model's sentence, through sanitize_text_field(). The filter switches AI off for one request; the constant WP_AI_SUPPORT (Lesson 9.2's Exercise 3) does it for the whole site.

Handout: labs/module-18/l9_2-next-steps/debug_headless_client.php (deliberately broken)

As shipped:

Warning: Undefined array key "title" in /var/www/html/debug_headless_client.php on line 19

  1. Warning, line 19: the REST index's field is name (the request asks for _fields=name); title does not exist.
  2. Deprecated, the same line: html_entity_decode() received the null. Fixing the key removes both.

Last in the list: To Kill a Mockingbird

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 9.1 · Final Project: Launch the Pages & Co. Website
  1. B 404 pagesco_hold_not_found: for her, the hold does not exist: Cancelling puts her user ID in the WHERE clause, so no row matches and the rules class answers pagesco_hold_not_found with status 404, the same answer as for an ID that does not exist. Jordan's hold stays waiting.
  2. A In Holds::place(), which every door calls: Every door, the REST route, the form handler and the tests, calls Holds::place(), so a rule there holds for all of them, and changes in one place.
  3. C HoldsTable::maybe_upgrade() runs on init and finds no version option, so it creates the table: HoldsTable registers maybe_upgrade() on init; the version option is missing (0), lower than DB_VERSION 1, so it runs dbDelta() and records the version. Activation hooks never run on an update.
  4. C The binding found no published book with that slug, so the paragraph kept its own text: The paragraph keeps its own text when the binding source returns null, which BookBindings does when the slug matches no published book: a renamed slug, a draft, or a deleted book.
  5. B Listing the ZIP and loading a new class with the ZIP's own autoloader: Only a check of the built ZIP sees what ships: counting its src/Holds files, and loading a new class with the ZIP's own autoloader, as Milestone 8 does.
Lesson 9.2 · Next Steps: Headless, AI in Core & Your Career
  1. B Read X-WP-TotalPages and fetch every page: The route pages its results (six per page, the shop's Books per list setting). X-WP-Total says 11 and X-WP-TotalPages says 2; a front end loops until the last page.
  2. C A WP_Error: no models support text generation: No provider plugin is installed, so no model supports text generation: generate_text() returns a WP_Error with the code prompt_invalid_argument and status 400.
  3. A An ANTHROPIC_API_KEY environment variable or constant on the server: WordPress looks for ANTHROPIC_API_KEY as an environment variable, then as a PHP constant, then in the option the Connectors screen saves. On a server, the environment or wp-config.php keeps it out of Git and out of the database dumps you copy around.
  4. C The ability's permission callback refuses Devon, and the model gets an error back: The resolver executes the ability as the current user, so its permission_callback (edit_others_posts) refuses Devon, and the model receives an error response instead of the shop's data.
  5. B Search Trac and GitHub for a ticket, then report it with exact steps to reproduce: Someone may already have reported it; if not, a ticket with the version, the exact steps, what you expected and what happened is what lets contributors confirm and fix it. That is the evidence habit of the whole course.

Exit Ticket

  1. Proves it: HoldsTest's test_only_the_customer_can_cancel_their_hold (the recorded failing run shows it catches the bug) and Milestone 3's holds-check.php (Priya's "not found"). Cannot: holds-smoke.sh, which plays one customer only.
  2. Up: pagesco-books.zip and pagesco.zip 1.2.0 (code). Stays / created on live: the My holds page and the shoptest account; the holds table is created by the new code itself (maybe_upgrade).
  3. Next: their own; a good answer names a feature and a lesson (custom post types 5.2, blocks 6.x, REST 7.2 ...).

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 B. Silencing the sniff plugin-wide: the habit to break before a client codebase.
  • Q3 D. Believes only activation creates tables; ask what happens on every update of a live plugin.
  • Q4 A. The most dangerous answer of the session: importing a development database overwrites live customers. Spend a minute on it.
  • Q8 A. innerHTML fixes the symptom and opens an XSS hole.
  • Q9 B or C. The headlines; show the lesson's var_dump on their own site.
  • Q11 A. Thinks the model calls the ability by its own name; the function-calling name has no slash.
